## 1. Gate boundary and non-negotiable decisions

This document replaces the rejected Slice100 discovery. The user approved this plan gate on 2026-07-15 with the following binding execution conditions:

1. The per-task cadence in section 13 is binding: focused RED, smallest implementation, focused verification, independent review, then explicit user approval before the next task.
2. Task 7's receipt/edge schema must return for explicit design review before its migration is written.
3. The Task 7 backfill migration must prove upgrade -> downgrade -> upgrade on disposable PostgreSQL before it can pass review.
4. No table, environment variable, or feature flag outside this plan may be introduced without an explicit plan amendment.

Task 7 is the latest independently approved implementation checkpoint. It comprises the selected 21+79 methodology spine from Task 6 plus the frozen-v21 receipt/edge schema, guards, ACLs, source freeze, completion/finalization, and dedicated erasure composition described in sections 1.36-1.70. The accepted Task-4 hardening autonomy is expired and does not authorize another acceptance namespace. Task 7's local commits do not activate `full-strict-v21` or discharge the Task-11 27-cut boundary. In parallel, Task 9 may add only the disjoint provider-periphery modules and RED tests reserved in section 1.33; it may not edit existing orchestrator, gate, migration, worker, registry-composition, launcher, or runtime files, and it may make no live provider/model call. The reviewer has approved every Task-8 semantic ruling and the five conditions recorded in section 1.72. Task 8 is authorized only through Task 8.1 RED materialization and the second literal artifact/fixture review gate. It must stop before formula code, migration `0039`, repositories, worker composition, dashboard, deliverable, or durable application-data mutation. The only database work inside Task 8.1 is the required isolated disposable-PostgreSQL application-role execution census for changed SQL authorities. Prompt evaluation/promotion, FULL analysis runs, Gate A/B probes or spend, push, PR, and merge remain unauthorized.

The following decisions are binding:

1. Acceptance uses the real configured HTTP API and the canonical `PipelineWorker`. It uses durable PostgreSQL/RLS repositories, `PostgresAuditSink`, PostgreSQL idempotency, Redis rate limiting, and a persistent configured object-store directory. `TestClient`, `_default_api_client`, temporary directories, SQLite, and in-memory sinks/stores are forbidden on the acceptance path.
2. Slice100 includes the two priced full runs. They are not deferred to Slice101. There are two explicit user approval gates: Gate A immediately before any live probe/evaluation, and Gate B immediately before the full priced runs.
3. The two top-level company folders produce two deals, two document sets, two runs, two step ledgers, and two output packages. Company 1 runs first; Company 2 starts only if Company 1 passes.
4. The source tree is read-only. It is neither copied nor linked into the worktree. The acceptance client uploads each source file through the authenticated product API using non-sensitive generated upload names.
5. All 267 files must reconcile. PNG, all MP4, all three files over 50 MiB, and any encrypted, corrupt, or unreadable file must be successfully handled. Missing capability is a blocker, never an intended deferral.
6. Both methodologies are mandatory: the checked-in 21-question commercial DD methodology and the real 79-row FDD workbook. Every row has durable coverage; every applicable row has a task and a non-empty governed result.
7. Every recorded graph/RAG/enrichment/debate/Layer-2/VEP consumer gap is implemented in Slice100. No exclusion is proposed because the user requires a complete end-to-end system.
8. All 18 enrichment providers and every LLM, embedding, graph, OCR/media, API, worker, database, audit, idempotency, rate-limit, budget, and object-store dependency are preflighted and runtime-proven. Configuration presence alone is not proof.

### 1.1 Approved 2026-07-15 enrichment-registry amendment

The authoritative enrichment registry expands from 15 to 18 production connectors by adding `epo_open_patent`, `semantic_scholar`, and `perplexity`. This amendment is effective in this plan immediately but is implementation-authorized only inside Task 9; it does not broaden or pause Task 2. Task 9 must begin RED-first and replace literal provider counts in probe tables, signed budget-policy provider rows, expected-edge families, Gate-A checklists, and verifier matrices with derivation from the one authoritative registry. The final signed budget policy therefore has the registry-derived 18 provider components plus `anthropic` and `openai_embeddings` (20 component rows). Task 2's already-reviewed baseline validation remains unchanged until that Task 9 RED/implementation checkpoint; it cannot be used as Gate-A evidence after the registry amendment.

All three connectors meet the same production bar as every existing provider: `provider-probes-v1`, encrypted tenant-scoped credential versions, real PROD rights decisions, durable normalized facts and safe request/source provenance, RAG indexing when rights permit, explicit applicability decisions, producer/output roots, expected consumer edges, completed consumer receipts, and the common fail-closed codes in section 9. Any missing or failed Gate-A probe is a hard blocker.

Consumer routing is registry data and a completion contract. `epo_open_patent` and `semantic_scholar` facts route to the technical and sector members of the eight `ANALYSIS` specialist agents; `perplexity` facts route to the market and sector specialists. All applicable facts from these providers also require expected edges and receipts from `SCORING`, conflict-check execution, all three `LAYER2_IC_CHALLENGE` roles (`ic_advocate`, `ic_challenger`, `ic_arbiter`), and the applicable deliverable subroles. They do not route into the Layer-1 Evidence Trust Court: Layer 1 remains evidence-integrity-only, while external context joins the trust-verified VEP on the Layer-2 side as required by TDD section 6.2.

Perplexity synthesis is never a primary fact. Its connector materializes cited sources as the evidence objects at a governed low source tier, preserves citation/retrieval provenance, and links any retained synthesis only through those citation objects. Unsupported synthesis is dropped or produces a low-grade Sanad; No-Free-Facts has no exception. A prose answer, answer hash, or provider invocation alone cannot satisfy persistence, Sanad, RAG, or consumer-receipt acceptance.

### 1.2 Approved 2026-07-16 Task 2b RLS normalization amendment

Migration `0034` is an RLS-only corrective migration. It adds no table, column, index, trigger, environment variable, feature flag, role, or Task-7 receipt/edge schema. Its authoritative tenant-bearing inventory is the following 42 tables, derived from the migrated PostgreSQL catalog and pinned here so catalog drift fails review:

`audit_events`, `break_glass_grants`, `byok_policies`, `calc_sanads`, `claims`, `data_room_package_files`, `data_room_packages`, `deal_assignments`, `deals`, `debate_sessions`, `defects`, `deliverables`, `deterministic_calculations`, `document_artifacts`, `document_spans`, `documents`, `enrichment_credentials`, `enrichment_rights_approvals`, `erasure_requests`, `evidence_items`, `evidence_trust_findings`, `group_memberships`, `groups`, `human_gate_actions`, `human_gates`, `idempotency_records`, `layer2_ic_challenges`, `layer2_ic_findings`, `legal_holds`, `muhasabah_records`, `overrides`, `provider_budget_policies`, `provider_budget_usage`, `run_steps`, `runs`, `sanads`, `strict_preflight_proofs`, `tenants`, `validated_evidence_packages`, `vector_embeddings`, `webhook_delivery_attempts`, and `webhooks`.

On upgrade, every listed table has RLS both `ENABLE`d and `FORCE`d. All prior permissive tenant policies are removed so they cannot combine as an accidental bypass, and each table receives exactly one policy applying to `ALL` commands and both policy predicates. The canonical predicate, used identically for `USING` and `WITH CHECK`, is:

```sql
NULLIF(current_setting('idis.tenant_id', true), '') IS NOT NULL
AND tenant_id = NULLIF(current_setting('idis.tenant_id', true), '')::uuid
```

The `tenants` design is deliberate: the row's `tenant_id` is the tenant identity. An application transaction may select, update, or delete only the row whose identity equals its transaction-local `idis.tenant_id`, and may insert only a row carrying that same identity. Administrative migration/bootstrap work remains outside the application role. Application reads of tenant metadata, including the tenant-region store, must establish the transaction-local tenant context before querying; an explicit `WHERE tenant_id = ...` is not an RLS substitute.

The RED contract must first fail against revision `0033` for the two reference defects—`runs` lacks `WITH CHECK`, and `claims` lacks the null guard—and for every other noncanonical table. It then proves, using the real application role and disposable PostgreSQL, cross-tenant denial for both a read path over a real row and a write path on each of the 42 tables; an unset or empty tenant context must also expose no tenant row and authorize no tenant write. Catalog checks require the exact 42-table inventory, `relrowsecurity=true`, `relforcerowsecurity=true`, one canonical policy per table, and both predicates present. The application role is re-proved `NOSUPERUSER` and `NOBYPASSRLS`.

Migration verification is upgrade `0033 -> 0034`, downgrade `0034 -> 0033`, then upgrade `0033 -> 0034` on disposable PostgreSQL. Downgrade restores the exact pre-`0034` policy names, predicates, and ENABLE/FORCE state rather than leaving a hybrid security posture. Compatibility tests prove the worker-lease claim, heartbeat, fenced step/output write, reclaim, and cancellation paths still satisfy the new `runs` `WITH CHECK`; tenant-region lookup establishes RLS context; and the full repository tree catches legacy code or tests that relied on a loose policy. A missing table, extra tenant-bearing table, policy mismatch, skipped required PostgreSQL proof, or cross-tenant read/write success fails `RLS_HEALTH_BLOCKED`.

9. All actually executed prompts are genuinely evaluated and promoted through Slice99 governance. Final strict admission requires `IDIS_REQUIRE_PROMOTED_PROMPTS=1`.
10. Any internal `FAILED`, `PARTIAL`, `DEFERRED`, `BLOCKED`, skipped/not-attempted state, empty applicable result, or missing durable record fails the step and run with a stable safe code. A missing required **incoming** consumer receipt fails when that consumer attempts completion; an unfulfilled outgoing expectation fails when its consumer attempts completion and, without exception, at finalization. A producer is not failed merely because a later consumer has not run yet.
11. Safe outputs contain aliases, UUIDs, counts, stable codes, boolean health, aggregate byte counts, and hashes only. They contain no company names, filenames, source paths, raw content, prompts, credentials, object keys, provider payloads, or sensitive identifiers.
12. No success wording is permitted until both company runs, package verification, process restart, authenticated re-download, cross-tenant denial, and leakage checks pass.

### 1.3 Approved Task 4 operator inputs and audited encryption clarification

Task 4 uses the pinned project-owned synthesized-speech fixture and phrase contract in section 10. The fixture is explicitly not represented as a consented human recording; all eight real data-room MP4s remain the acceptance evidence. The configured ingestion ceiling remains exactly 536,870,912 bytes, and the source tree remains read-only. A read-only `ffprobe` census found exactly eight MP4s with a maximum observed duration of 128 seconds; this is metadata-only and is not represented as transcription evidence.

The operator statement that the data room has no password-protected documents is confirmed in the security-relevant sense: **zero files require a non-empty password**. A byte-level read-only census also found that PDF encryption metadata must not be reported as zero: of 223 PDFs, 81 are not encryption-marked and 142 are encryption-marked but open successfully with the empty credential; zero require a configured secret and zero fail structurally in this census. Task 4 therefore records those three safe aggregate dispositions separately. The approved manifest state for this data room remains `none configured, none required`; empty-credential-openable PDFs are parsed normally, while any file requiring a non-empty password, rejecting a configured password, or remaining corrupt/unreadable is an unexpected hard blocker. This clarification adds no table, environment variable, or feature flag.

The page-level read-only OCR **admission** census is also binding: the 223 PDFs contain 2,595 pages; 181 PDFs are all-native-text, 35 are all-OCR, and seven are mixed native/OCR. Exactly 42 PDFs and 284 pages enter the zero-native-text admission set. That source fact is immutable and remains bound by the safe content-hash-set contract digest `30e4f703eb40330855b8e7e5d6b64404386ef530c1773802ed15dfb6ab74dc89`; paths, names, and extracted text are excluded. The read-only census also retains internal content-SHA-256 bindings to the exact OCR-required page-number set and the total page count for each admitted PDF; their keys exactly equal the 42-file hash set and the required-page-set cardinalities sum to 284. These bindings are validation state, not a private-name report. They are distinct from the actual full-resolution OCR execution census after the classifier-first amendment in §1.16. Every unique signed candidate page must occur in its hash-bound required-page set and subtracts that exact page from execution, but a PDF leaves the execution file set only when no required page remains. Exhausting OCR-required pages does not imply a whole-document terminal when native-text pages remain; a classifier terminal is counted only when classified pages cover every page from 1 through the bound total page count. For acceptance-007, the verified set contains one classified page in each of two one-page PDFs, so both files are OCR-exhausted and classifier-terminal, deriving 40 PDF OCR files/282 OCR pages plus the two PNG OCR files, for 42 actual OCR-executed files. These execution values must be computed from verified candidate identities and the source bindings, never copied as unexplained literals. The durable v4 parser proof records `pdf_ocr_admission_file_count=42`, `pdf_ocr_admission_page_count=284`, `pdf_classifier_first_file_count=2`, `pdf_classifier_first_page_count=2`, `pdf_classifier_only_file_count=2`, `pdf_classifier_only_page_count=2`, `pdf_classifier_terminal_file_count=2`, `pdf_ocr_file_count=40`, `pdf_ocr_execution_page_count=282`, and `ocr_file_count=42`, and enforces their subset and additive reconciliations. The maximum required page index and maximum OCR-required page count in one PDF remain 72; eight PDFs require a page beyond the old default of 10 and four require a page beyond 50. `IDIS_OCR_MAX_PAGES` is consequently pinned to exactly `72` in strict profile validation and typed readiness.

### 1.4 Approved Task 4 durable native-text identity amendment

Native HTML and plain-text parses must remain truthful in the durable `documents.doc_type` field. The pre-Task-4 runtime maps both parser identities to `PDF`, and the existing `valid_document_doc_type` check from revision `0016` excludes `HTML` and `TEXT`; parser metadata alone is not a substitute for a truthful canonical column. Task 4 therefore adds the linear, schema-minimal revision `0035` after `0034`. It widens only that existing check constraint to the exact prior set plus `HTML` and `TEXT`, adds the matching model enum values and ingestion mappings, and adds no table, column, index, trigger, environment variable, feature flag, or Task-7 receipt/edge schema.

RED proves that real HTML/TXT ingestion currently persists `PDF`. PostgreSQL verification proves `0034 -> 0035`, truthful HTML/TEXT inserts at `0035`, a fail-closed downgrade rejection with stable code `TASK4_NATIVE_DOCUMENT_TYPES_BLOCK_DOWNGRADE` while either type exists, then (after explicit test-row removal) `0035 -> 0034 -> 0035` with the exact constraint set at every point. Downgrade never relabels data. Current-head assertions advance to `0035`; tests intentionally pinned to revision `0034` for the approved Task-2b RLS semantics remain pinned.

### 1.5 Approved Task 4 dual-track video evidence amendment

The read-only real-source proof found that the configured local faster-whisper runtime produces positive transcript spans for six MP4s and a truthful zero-text result for two audio-bearing MP4s. Deterministic five-point frame sampling on those two files produced real OCR text at every sampled point. The observed `6/2` distribution is safe-report evidence only and is never a runtime constant, acceptance expectation, branch, or fixture oracle.

Every MP4 must execute both evidence tracks and persist both completed parser receipts in the existing document metadata JSONB: `media-stt-receipt-v1` and `media-frame-ocr-receipt-v1`. A completed receipt may truthfully report zero evidence. Per-video success requires both receipts plus at least one non-empty track: `stt_span_count > 0 OR frame_ocr_span_count > 0`. If both are empty, the parser and terminal gate fail `MEDIA_NO_EXTRACTABLE_EVIDENCE`. Failure, timeout, malformed output, or incomplete work in either required track is terminal and cannot be masked by evidence from the other track. This is parser provenance, not the Task-7 consumer-receipt schema, and adds no table, migration, environment variable, or feature flag.

Frame sampling is the plan-pinned `periodic-plus-scenes-v1` policy. From the single validated ffprobe duration, expressed as integer milliseconds, it schedules the first decodable frame, the last interior frame, the midpoint of every 1,000-ms bucket across the complete video, and every ffmpeg scene transition detected at the fixed `0.20` threshold. Candidate timestamps are converted from PTS to integer milliseconds, clamped to `[0, duration_ms - 1]`, sorted, and deduplicated. The schedule is capped at 600 candidates; exceeding that ceiling fails `MEDIA_FRAME_SAMPLE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` and never truncates. Frames are decoded without upscaling at no more than 1920x1080, and every scheduled frame is OCRed with the admitted Tesseract configuration. A frame receipt is complete only when `completed_sample_count == scheduled_sample_count` and its schedule hash matches the rederived ordered timestamps.

Each non-empty sampled frame produces one real `TIMECODE` span whose locator contains `source=video_frame_ocr`, the stable sample index, `start_ms=sample_ms`, `end_ms=min(duration_ms, sample_ms + 1)`, and normalized OCR confidence. STT spans retain `source=media_transcript`. Monotonicity is enforced independently within each track; overlap between the two real tracks is valid. MP4 rows retain `ocr_performed=false`, so they affect neither the 42-PDF/284-page source admission census nor the classifier-first 40-PDF/282-page plus two-PNG execution census, and add `media_frame_ocr_performed=true`. The exact completion reason is `media_evidence_completed`; legacy STT-only completed-looking rows cannot clear strict preflight or terminal reconciliation.

One 900-second monotonic per-video deadline, supplied by the reviewed `IDIS_MEDIA_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=900`, starts before binary resolution and covers the single duration probe, pre/post model fingerprints, faster-whisper inference, scene scan, all frame decoding and OCR, result transfer, and subprocess/private-frame cleanup. Every phase receives only the current remaining time; no phase resets or extends the deadline. The admitted configuration hash continues to bind the original 900-second value rather than a decreasing per-phase remainder. `IDIS_MEDIA_MAX_DURATION_SECONDS` is pinned to 300, covering the observed 128-second maximum while bounding the sampling schedule.

The two receipts carry only safe counts, statuses, hashes, confidence aggregates, runtime/config provenance, and the reviewed policy identity. The STT runtime provenance explicitly carries the local model-files SHA-256 and the plan-pinned faster-whisper constructor values `cpu_threads=4` and `num_workers=1`; the existing canonical configuration SHA-256 binds those three values together with the admitted compute type. Adding those two exact integer fields advances only the nested media runtime-provenance metadata shape to `faster-whisper-runtime-provenance-v2`; the adapter contract remains `idis-faster-whisper-adapter-v1`, and this is neither a database schema nor a Task-7 receipt/edge schema. Reconciliation rederives receipt counts and ordered evidence hashes from durable spans instead of trusting booleans. Safe output may report observed STT-positive and frame-OCR-positive counts, but never text, frames, source names, paths, or a required split. Stable failures include `MEDIA_FRAME_OCR_UNAVAILABLE`, `MEDIA_FRAME_OCR_TIMEOUT`, `MEDIA_FRAME_OCR_FAILED`, `MEDIA_FRAME_OCR_INCOMPLETE`, `MEDIA_FRAME_SAMPLE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, and `MEDIA_NO_EXTRACTABLE_EVIDENCE`.

Strict frame-OCR readiness uses a separate immutable visual asset because the pinned speech fixture is an audio-only MP4 and cannot truthfully prove frame discovery, decode, or OCR. The project-owned fixture is `tests/fixtures/runtime_health/frame-ocr-health-v1.mp4`: 10,228 bytes, SHA-256 `581a4dc749d576662859019d2006c354b03094733e1156fcf91a96f90df6643c`. Its manifest is `tests/fixtures/runtime_health/frame-ocr-health-v1.json`, SHA-256 `266d1bf36adb38c62fcd49e070cb7dc6453ef33d3a332a564453c58ec8a92ef3`, with project-ownership/license-evidence SHA-256 `998c66f026f466989e7ad9cdfd225e90fdcb6ef57684936bc85947f37dd0d709`. It is locally generated project-authored text and composition with no third-party recording, media, company data, or data-room material; the local system font and ffmpeg are generation tools, not distributed source assets. The fixed normalized phrase is `media frame ocr health one two three`.

A real fresh-process ffmpeg/Tesseract execution over that visual fixture observed five scheduled samples, five completed samples, and five exact non-empty phrase receipts; its schedule digest was `7bcae72ccdd16c86cb3201771887e21d613ea4e03bc00050f1b4414a30ee56c9`. This `5/5` is a health-fixture observation only. It is never an expected count, branch, success threshold, or acceptance constant for any of the eight real videos; real-video schedules remain derived from duration and scene transitions, and real-video success remains the two-receipt/at-least-one-non-empty-track contract above.

### 1.6 Approved Task 4 real durable-ingestion proof ownership

Task 4 now owns the real ingestion-only proof that was previously left as a Task-13 CLI seam. It wires the already planned strict-launcher gate options `--api-base-url`, `--prepare-data-rooms`, `--preflight-only`, `--verify-restart-survival`, and `--verify-tenant-isolation` into the existing `idis.evaluation.real_example_gate`; it adds neither a parallel runner nor a new flag. The approved 2026-07-17 operator amendment makes the already HMAC-bound pair `bootstrap-v1` plus `IDIS_REQUIRE_PROMOTED_PROMPTS=0` intrinsically select the reviewed `task4-ingestion-v1` API/gate scope. Durable gate mode accepts only a reviewed `StrictRuntimeProfile` passed in-process by `idis.runtime.strict_launcher gate`; direct script/module invocation, `strict_profile=None`, a wrong launcher command/generation/scope, or an API/gate profile mismatch fails `STRICT_FULL_LIVE_BLOCKED` before any upload or durable collection. The authenticated API readiness surface exposes only `SHA-256(base_profile_hmac)`, and the gate exposes only that same safe hash in its receipt. On both the initial and restarted API, the gate derives the hash from its own launcher-supplied profile and requires an exact constant-time match with the API value before proceeding. The raw HMAC and profile values never enter an API response, report, log, or audit payload. The matched safe hash is bound into both proof phases. It reuses `prepare_two_company_source_packages`, the append-only resume ledger, `collect_durable_terminal_rows`, and `reconcile_terminal_ingestion` through a real external HTTP client. The legacy one-deal `real_example_run_harness` remains forbidden.

The external client uses finite phase-specific HTTP bounds derived from already approved runtime configuration, not an unbounded request and not a new environment variable: a finite connect bound, a finite write bound, and a read bound equal to the larger of the admitted OCR and aggregate media deadlines plus a fixed local transport/transaction grace. Deterministic sustained pacing is exactly `60 / IDIS_RATE_LIMIT_INTEGRATION_RPM` seconds between requests. `IDIS_RATE_LIMIT_BURST_MULTIPLIER` sizes token-bucket capacity only and never increases the sustained request rate or appears in that divisor. Before the first upload, the gate must obtain healthy strict OCR and media presence/configuration receipts and execute the real pinned `stt-human-en-v1` faster-whisper inference; a configuration-only STT check, missing binary/model/tessdata, fixture/hash/phrase/timecode mismatch, or failed inference blocks all uploads.

The proof holds private terminal rows in memory only and persists under `.local_reports/` only safe aggregate receipts and canonical digests. Its canonical code closure is the sorted recursive set of every `src/idis/**/*.py` file, not a hand-selected Task-4 subset, plus `contracts/contract_lock.json`, `openapi/IDIS_OpenAPI_v6_3.yaml`, the production acceptance wrapper `scripts/invoke_strict_native_process.ps1`, and its Windows launcher/service relationship validator `scripts/strict_process_identity.psm1`. Each member path and byte hash participates in the closure digest; an unreadable member, symlink/reparse alias, duplicate normalized path, or path-set drift fails rather than silently reducing coverage. The separately pinned runtime-health assets above and the plan's own digest are bound alongside that closure. The initial phase records only the SHA-256 of a fresh per-process API-boot UUID. After the strict API process is killed and relaunched through the same reviewed profile, verification must observe a different boot-UUID hash, rerun preparation against the same append-only ledger with zero upload POSTs, recollect all 267 durable rows, and require unchanged ledger/code/plan/profile/readiness and canonical reduced-terminal-row digests.

Before restart and again after restart, the owning acceptance identity performs at least one deterministic authenticated byte-stream download per company, selected from the durable PostgreSQL document/object lineage, and proves the streamed byte count and SHA-256 against the persistent-object receipt. This is a true bounded raw-byte streaming response and incremental client hash: the API and gate may not serialize, base64-encode, JSON-wrap, or buffer the complete object in memory. Finite read timeout, admitted maximum byte count, fixed-size chunks, exact terminal byte count, and end-of-stream SHA-256 are mandatory. Both companies must be represented and the two-company download-receipt digest must remain identical across restart.

The complete canary isolation matrix executes once after initial ingestion and before API termination, then executes again after restart; the initial proof command therefore also carries `--verify-tenant-isolation`. In both phases the canary identity proves denial without an existence oracle on every planned cross-tenant read surface (deal/package, document, span, content-integrity, and byte download) and every applicable cross-tenant write surface (package/membership mutation and document upload/update/delete against owner identifiers). The document mutation family explicitly includes an authenticated `DELETE` attempt against an owner document and proves that the row, spans, and object version remain unchanged. An unrelated validation, not-found shortcut, or foreign-key failure is not RLS/auth denial proof. Task 13 consumes this frozen ingestion proof later; it does not own or defer Task 4's upload/parser/object/span/restart/isolation execution.

The proof starts only after the dedicated local pgvector-capable PostgreSQL database is migrated base-to-`0035` by the strict migration role and the scoped strict API dependency admission is green. That scoped admission executes the real PostgreSQL/schema/RLS, durable audit, durable PostgreSQL idempotency, Redis, persistent object-store, and two-key auth/residency probes used by ingestion. It truthfully omits worker-claim and provider-budget execution because this process starts no worker and authorizes no provider call; omitted components are recorded as outside the scope, never reported green. Signed governance files and their trust root remain profile-bound inputs, but provider credential-lineage bootstrap, rights import, provider-budget import/probing, and all provider calls remain Task 9/Gate-A work. The API scope starts no pipeline, webhook-dispatch, or compliance background worker and permits only `GET /health`, `GET /v1/strict-readiness`, and the exact authenticated deal/document/upload/package/span/content-integrity/content/download/delete methods used by the Task-4 proof. Every run, enrichment, governance, prompt, provider, debate, scoring, deliverable, administrative, metrics, and unrelated API route is absent. An `acceptance-v1`/promoted API retains the complete production surface and complete Task-2 admission.

The frozen server inventory is exactly these 13 method/templates: `GET /health`; `GET /v1/strict-readiness`; `POST /v1/deals`; `GET /v1/deals/{deal_id}`; `POST /v1/deals/{deal_id}/documents/upload`; `GET /v1/deals/{deal_id}/documents/{document_id}`; `POST /v1/deals/{deal_id}/data-room-packages`; `GET /v1/deals/{deal_id}/data-room-packages/{package_id}`; `GET /v1/documents/{doc_id}/content-integrity`; `GET /v1/documents/{doc_id}/spans`; streaming-only `GET /v1/documents/{doc_id}/content`; `GET /v1/documents/{doc_id}`; and `DELETE /v1/documents/{doc_id}`. The last two are canary-denial surfaces and remain mounted so a router-level 404 cannot masquerade as tenant isolation. Server startup compares its observed routes to this literal inventory and fails on a missing, duplicate, or extra route. The external owner and canary clients enforce their own smaller literal method/path/transport sets before transport; the owner cannot delete, the canary cannot create/list/read general deals, and buffered access to the streaming content route is forbidden.

Task 4's first real API attempt exposed a pre-existing creator-bootstrap atomicity gap: `createDeal` committed a visible deal without the direct actor assignment required by deny-by-default ABAC, so the same authenticated creator could not perform the next deal-scoped operation. Deal creation therefore inserts the creator's existing `ACTOR` assignment on the same request-scoped PostgreSQL connection and transaction as the deal and audit writes for every role already authorized to call `createDeal`; it adds no role, bypass, route, table, environment variable, or flag. The in-memory twin writes through the shared assignment store and compensates the deal if assignment fails. Break-glass tests retain an unassigned ADMIN by using a distinct authorized creator, rather than treating the creator as unassigned.

The two deterministic Task-4 `createDeal` requests that first exposed this defect already committed their deals, successful immutable `deal.created` audits, and durable idempotency responses before any document upload, so replaying those same requests correctly bypasses the repaired handler. Task 4 therefore permits one narrow request-time compatibility repair on a retained PostgreSQL `POST createDeal` `201` replay, and only when the server's strict composition state is the admitted `task4-ingestion-v1` bootstrap scope; unrestricted/acceptance production composition never invokes it. It is not a migration, startup backfill, idempotency deletion, or legacy-deal repair. While holding the existing idempotency-scope advisory lock and request transaction, the repair re-locks and byte-for-byte rechecks the exact same-tenant/same-actor/raw-key idempotency row, locks the RLS-visible deal, requires an exact canonical cached response and matching deal state, requires exactly one immutable successful `deal.created` lineage whose tenant, actor/type, event/request IDs, request method/path/status, hashed idempotency key, resource, payload hash, and timestamps all agree, and rejects any later assignment-created/deleted lineage. An existing exact creator assignment makes the request a pure replay. Otherwise the exact `ACTOR` assignment and one `rbac.assignment.created` audit are written through the same request connection and commit atomically; audit failure rolls both back. Missing, duplicate, malformed, cross-tenant, drifted, revoked, or otherwise ambiguous evidence fails closed as `IDEMPOTENCY_REPLAY_INVARIANT_FAILED`, without logging the raw key or diagnostic bindings. A second replay must be mutation-free. This refinement introduces no schema, migration, table, environment variable, flag, route, provider call, or private-data output.

The scoped API creates exactly two isolated deals/packages, uploads all 267 files under generated names, and requires durable PostgreSQL document/span/audit/idempotency rows plus one persistent object-store version and authenticated content-integrity receipt per file. It then kills and restarts the API process through the strict launcher, re-collects all rows and authenticated object/download receipts, proves hashes/versions survive, and uses the configured canary tenant to prove the required read and write denial matrix cross-tenant.

The safe Task-4 report contains only aliases, counts, exact aggregate byte totals (overall and by safe company alias), content/contract/profile/readiness/boot/download/policy/exemplar/prescan/candidate/sample/receipt hashes, stable codes, derived media-track and watermark-classification counts, numeric classifier scores, restart booleans, review-required booleans, and isolation booleans. It never contains a source/reviewer path, private filename, OCR/stamp text, bitmap pixels, or signature. STT-positive and frame-OCR-positive video counts are derived from the 267 durable terminal rows on every proof run; the observed `6/2` split is never a literal acceptance constant or expected value. The proof performs no external provider, LLM, embedding, graph, prompt, debate, methodology, scoring, or deliverable call and does not queue a FULL run. A partial upload may resume only through the source-bound hash-chain ledger; a missing durable row, failed parser/classifier receipt, object mismatch, restart loss, unchanged API-boot hash, owner-download failure, unresolved runtime review, or canary access success fails the proof and never becomes a deferral.

This safety refinement uses only the already planned profile values, JSONB metadata, API surfaces, and gate flags. It introduces no table, column, migration, environment variable, feature flag, or durable API flag. Section 1.11 separately authorizes only the documented offline prescan CLI arguments; they cannot broaden the API, worker, or acceptance gate.

### 1.7 Approved 2026-07-17 Task 9 identity-before-rights amendment

Task 9 must evaluate each company/provider in this exact fail-closed order: **verified identity output/version -> immutable applicability rule/decision -> required provider identifier -> active rights version -> exact active encrypted credential version -> active signed budget policy/reservation -> rights-compatible cache decision -> provider request -> durable normalized persistence/indexing/consumer receipts**. An operator seed, deal UUID, absent ticker, missing registry match, or `UNKNOWN` listing state is not verified identity and cannot produce `NOT_APPLICABLE`. The identity output records a governed listing state (`LISTED`, positively verified `PRIVATE`, positively verified `UNLISTED`, or `UNKNOWN`), evidence/provenance IDs and hashes, rule version, and immutable decision hash.

When positively verified identity proves that Finnhub or FMP is inapplicable to a private/unlisted acceptance company, Task 9 persists a `NOT_APPLICABLE` provider outcome before touching rights. That outcome must bind the identity output/version and rule and prove **zero** rights-row selection, credential decrypt/use, budget reservation, cache lookup, HTTP attempt, provider fact, or downstream fact receipt. Therefore the currently signed `REVOKED` Finnhub/FMP rights snapshots are honest and do not block these two company runs once both identities independently prove inapplicability; they still block any listed/applicable execution. A missing ticker alone never establishes inapplicability, and `LISTED`/`UNKNOWN` plus revoked or absent commercial rights fails closed.

The Gate-A registry inventory still contains one derived row for each of all 18 providers. For an applicable provider the row requires its real rights-authorized probe and any failure is a hard blocker. For a provider proven inapplicable to both frozen acceptance identities, the row is satisfied only by the two durable zero-call `NOT_APPLICABLE` receipts and the registry rule hash; attempting a public-company or generic probe to bypass the revoked acceptance rights is forbidden. This changes no provider count and does not weaken the rule that a failed attempted probe blocks. RED must cover rights-before-identity, seed/UUID lookup, `UNKNOWN`/missing-ticker false N/A, wrong identity version, listed-plus-revoked, credential/budget/cache access on an N/A branch, and any provider/fact/receipt side effect on that branch. This amendment is design-effective now and implementation-authorized only in Task 9.

### 1.8 Task 4 real-PDF OCR deadline refinement

The first resumable real ingestion attempt stopped honestly after 52 committed rows when safe alias `company-001-document-00053.pdf` returned `DOCUMENT_UNREADABLE` with the sole parser code `ocr_timeout`. Safe read-only characterization proves that this is an unencrypted 5,200,948-byte, 72-page scanned PDF with zero native-text pages, so all 72 pages require real OCR. The admitted `IDIS_OCR_MAX_PAGES=72`, `IDIS_OCR_DPI=200`, and one 120-second wall-clock deadline remain unchanged; this is not an unreadable/corrupt disposition, file-specific bypass, timeout increase, or relaxed acceptance rule.

The production Tesseract PDF worker is refined to one `image_to_data` execution per page, using that single result as both the text-token and confidence authority, instead of the current duplicate `image_to_string` plus `image_to_data` executions. It reconstructs lines deterministically from ordered Tesseract block/paragraph/line/word coordinates, emits pages only in ascending source-page order, and fails closed on missing, duplicate, malformed, empty, or out-of-window page results. The strict ingestion path passes the already hash/size/stat-verified staged PDF path read-only to the isolated worker, so `convert_from_bytes` cannot orphan an additional private temporary PDF when the child is killed. Poppler renders deterministic ascending batches of at most eight page images with `thread_count=1`; every image is closed before the next batch and no unbounded raster set is resident. Each batch uses at most four fixed page worker threads, with `OMP_THREAD_LIMIT=1` for every Tesseract descendant. Every render and queued/running page receives only the remaining portion of the original parent monotonic deadline, never a reset. The outer process deadline and recursive process-tree termination remain authoritative and must kill Tesseract descendants and clean queues/images on timeout or failure. A safe eight-page real-data characterization observed 5.454 seconds for bounded rendering and 2.234 seconds for the four-worker single-pass OCR stage; that observation is diagnostic evidence only, never a per-file threshold or success constant.

RED must prove exactly one OCR call per page, maximum concurrency four with actual overlap, deterministic page/line order independent of completion order, batches no larger than eight, one shared decreasing deadline, malformed/missing hierarchy failure, timeout classification, and process-tree/queue/image cleanup compatibility. The stopped attempt has two acceptance deals with 52 `PARSED` documents plus two committed `FAILED` timeout documents. Because those rows were produced by the pre-refinement code/profile and the final receipt binds one code/profile closure, neither ledger resume, selective retry, deletion of only the failed rows, nor relabeling is acceptable proof. Before another live upload, the operator/reviewer must explicitly approve either a fresh dedicated acceptance database/namespace or a complete reset of the dedicated acceptance state; the old ledger and safe failure evidence are preserved. The final proof then starts from zero and ingests all 267 rows under one final closure. This refinement adds no environment variable, flag, table, migration, route, synthetic acceptance path, or external call.

### 1.9 Approved Task 4 clean-restart namespace mapping

The 2026-07-17 clean restart is explicitly versioned and never resumes or selectively edits the failed attempt. Logical `acceptance-001` denotes the preserved legacy namespaces: PostgreSQL database `idis_dev`, the previously configured `IDIS-objects` filesystem root, Redis logical database 0, and report/ledger root `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\.local_reports\slice100\task4-ingestion`. Its stopped preparation ledger is preserved at 616,959 bytes with SHA-256 `4e0c9554aba9fee377037dcd6057de161cb45cdc750e348d480dbcad6f2f3c56`; its safe reconciliation-log entry records 52 uploaded rows, two committed failed rows, and the fail-closed blocker without private names or content.

Logical `acceptance-002` uses the fresh PostgreSQL database `idis_acceptance_002`, filesystem object-store subdirectory `acceptance-002` under the same approved persistent parent, Redis logical database 1, and report/ledger root `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\.local_reports\slice100\acceptance-002`. Preflight must prove the database and paths absent and Redis DB 1 empty before creation/use. The strict dotenv changes exactly four entries: the database name in `IDIS_DATABASE_URL`, the database name in `IDIS_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL`, the `IDIS_OBJECT_STORE_BASE_DIR` suffix, and the logical database in `IDIS_REDIS_URL`. The report/ledger root is not a dotenv entry and changes only through the existing `--safe-report-dir`, `--prepare-ledger`, `--resume-ledger`, and `--pid-file` arguments; no new environment variable or flag is introduced.

The operator update clears only the strict dotenv file's read-only attribute, performs those four exact value substitutions without rewriting any other entry, restores read-only, and re-proves `path_acl_is_current_owner_only` for both the file and its parent before the launcher is invoked. The expected bootstrap-v1 base-profile HMAC change is recorded only through its safe digest. The `acceptance-001` database, objects, and ledgers receive no delete, reset, retry, relabel, or further acceptance write. Migration and every API/gate process target only `acceptance-002`, which must ingest all 267 files from zero under one code/plan/profile closure.

The template-zero database begins with no application-role table grants. After the strict launcher applies exactly `0035`, the existing migration authority grants the existing application role only `USAGE` on schema `public` and `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, and `DELETE` on every table then present in that schema, matching the proven `acceptance-001` application privilege shape. It grants no ownership, schema creation, DDL, `TRUNCATE`, role membership, superuser, or `BYPASSRLS`. Catalog verification derives the table count, requires complete CRUD coverage, and re-proves `NOSUPERUSER`/`NOBYPASSRLS`; the strict migration command is then rerun idempotently so its durable launch-audit emission must succeed. A schema-at-`0035` result accompanied by `AUDIT_EMIT_FAILED` is not accepted as a successful migration launch.

### 1.10 Approved Task 4 OCR rescue, verified-no-substantive-content evidence, and acceptance-003 restart

The `acceptance-002` run stopped honestly after 110 durable `PARSED` uploads and one durable `FAILED` upload, with 39,939 persisted spans. The sole blocker was `DOCUMENT_UNREADABLE`/`ocr_no_text_extracted` for safe alias `company-002-document-00053.pdf`. Its preserved preparation ledger has 113 physical rows, is 2,535,384 bytes, and has SHA-256 `88b1bea61df89be3af4aa40a6f801a386ee848e6cb29ab9caf01a140329a2175`. The append-only reconciliation entry records only those safe aggregates and blocker codes. The `acceptance-002` database, object subtree, Redis logical database, report root, and ledger are sealed against resume, deletion, relabeling, selective retry, or further acceptance writes.

OCR rescue is `pdf-ocr-rescue-v1` and is entered **only** when the admitted primary page OCR has completed successfully and its normalized token sequence is empty. A non-empty primary result is never replaced, rescored, or compared with rescue output. Rescue evaluates the complete fixed Cartesian matrix of exactly 24 candidates in this order: counter-clockwise rotation outer loop `[0, 90, 180, 270]`, photometric transform middle loop `[autocontrast-c0, binary-192, binary-224]`, and Tesseract page-segmentation mode inner loop `[6, 11]`. Rotation uses only the lossless Pillow transpose operation, with no interpolation, crop, resize, or pixel-count change. `autocontrast-c0` is grayscale `L` followed by `ImageOps.autocontrast(..., cutoff=0)`; each binary transform is grayscale `L` followed by the literal rule `pixel < threshold -> 0, else 255`. Every candidate calls the admitted Tesseract language with `--oem 1 --psm <6|11>` and `Output.DICT`. Deskew, inversion, morphology, adaptive thresholds, model selection, per-file tuning, early exit, and any other transform are forbidden.

The reviewer visually determined on 2026-07-17 that safe alias `company-002-document-00053.pdf` is a watermark-only page: deterministic contrast enhancement makes legible only a diagonal VDR confidentiality stamp describing a recipient email and timestamp, with no substantive business content. The actual email and timestamp values are never copied into source, plan, envelope, database, audit, ledger, report, or output; the content SHA-256 binds the inspected bytes. Re-verification through the sealed `acceptance-002` ledger binds the complete content SHA-256 `660891ceb200f7ea337e55377ab6d78beda9081e3358200d7cc18cb9d6b3cd5b` and unchanged 267-file source-tree fingerprint `dc29e07e7c605a7ba4bd3b84ad9ab481c32056e544f054a64f95946a460ef64d`; no private locator was returned or serialized. This finding does **not** authorize a 45-degree rotation, diagonal-watermark chase, or any expansion of the rescue matrix. Stamp tokens are evidentiary junk and must not enter spans, RAG, graph, claims, Sanads, agents, debate, scoring, or deliverables. The exact reviewer statement is `Diagonal VDR confidentiality stamp (recipient email + timestamp) only; no substantive content; determined visually by reviewer after deterministic contrast enhancement, 2026-07-17.`; its UTF-8 SHA-256 is `2444a956745c250d6e4189dc1d65d72ca79dd12eae9e60d43149dc40eb850b2a`. Only that hash, never the statement or stamp values, enters the signed payload and durable receipt.

Candidate normalization is NFKC plus case-folding, with Unicode letter/number runs as tokens and every other character as a separator. A candidate is a completed rejection when it has fewer than four tokens, fewer than 16 alphanumeric code points, mean word confidence below `0.60`, median word confidence below `0.55`, or fewer than `0.75` of words at confidence `>=0.50`. Every non-empty Tesseract word must have one valid finite confidence. More than 65,536 normalized code points, more than 4,096 tokens, malformed/incomplete output, or transform cleanup failure is `ocr_failed`, never a discarded candidate. All 24 candidates must finish before an acceptance decision; a timeout in any candidate makes the whole document `ocr_timeout`.

For normalized token sequence `x`, `U(x)` is its unigram multiset and `B(x)` its adjacent-token bigram multiset. Multiset Dice is `2 * sum(min(count_x(g), count_y(g))) / (sum(count_x) + sum(count_y))`, or zero if either side is empty. Cross-candidate agreement is the lesser of unigram and bigram Dice, compared without rounding. An agreement edge exists only at `>=0.800000`; persisted values use round-half-even to six decimal places. Rescue succeeds only when there is exactly one maximal pairwise clique of at least three quality-admitted candidates, all at the same rotation, spanning at least two photometric transform IDs and both PSMs. A second qualifying clique, including an overlapping or differently oriented near tie, fails closed. Zero qualifying cliques fails `OCR_RESCUE_NO_CONSENSUS`. The observed reference agreement range `0.077` through `0.25` therefore cannot pass. Inside the one admitted clique, the representative is the real candidate ordered by descending minimum agreement to the clique, mean agreement to the clique, mean confidence, and fraction of words at confidence `>=0.50`; the fixed matrix ordinal is only the representation tie-break after semantic consensus and never creates consensus.

The existing single 120-second monotonic PDF OCR deadline and at-most-0.25-second cleanup reserve cover primary OCR, every rescue transform/call, consensus calculation, result transfer, process-tree termination, and cleanup. A rescued page performs at most 25 Tesseract calls including primary; the 72-page ceiling therefore permits at most 1,728 rescue-matrix calls plus 72 primary calls, 1,800 total, while the existing page executor still permits no more than four active Tesseract calls. Candidates are sequential inside each page future, no nested executor is introduced, Poppler remains bounded to batches of eight with one rendering thread, and `OMP_THREAD_LIMIT=1` remains inherited. Boundedness is the fixed call ceiling plus the unchanged wall clock; inability to finish the matrix in the remaining time is `ocr_timeout`, not an incomplete success.

Every accepted rescued line is a real `PAGE_TEXT` span with `source=ocr_rescue`, never `source=ocr`. Its exact locator repeats the immutable page receipt: policy version and SHA-256, winner rotation/transform/PSM, winner word count, mean/median/minimum/maximum confidence, fixed confidence-histogram counts and confidence-values SHA-256, fraction at or above `0.50`, attempted-candidate count 24, supporting-candidate count, minimum and mean clique agreement, candidate-receipt SHA-256, and normalized-page-text SHA-256. The candidate receipt binds all 24 ordered candidate IDs, completed/rejected states, safe quality fields, normalized-text hashes, confidence-distribution hashes, and the complete pairwise agreement matrix; it never persists raw candidate text, a path, or a private name. All lines on a page repeat identical page-level fields. `ocr-terminal-evidence-v2` adds rescue page/span counts and a rederived rescue-receipt digest; standard OCR rows carry zero counts and the canonical empty digest. Terminal collection accepts only the exact standard or exact rescue locator schema, rederives every count/digest/range/allowlist, and hashes private locators before safe output. Downstream Sanad grading therefore sees the distinct `ocr_rescue` evidence source and cannot treat rescued text as clean OCR.

If a complete real primary-plus-rescue execution still produces no admitted substantive text, the document continues to fail closed unless an explicit, reviewer-approved `document-no-substantive-content-attestation-v1` envelope matches it. The renamed optional secret `IDIS_REAL_EXAMPLE_NO_SUBSTANTIVE_CONTENT_ATTESTATION_MANIFEST_PATH` points to one owner-only, read-only JSON file under an owner-only parent, with the same 64-KiB secure-read and duplicate-member protections as the password manifest. It reuses `IDIS_GOVERNANCE_TRUSTED_SIGNER_ID` and `IDIS_GOVERNANCE_TRUSTED_ED25519_PUBLIC_KEY_PATH`; the Ed25519 signature covers the canonical `{schema_version, signer_id, payload}`. The exact payload is one document only: `{tenant_id, source_tree_fingerprint, safe_alias, content_sha256, disposition, variant, attestation_text_sha256, reviewer_approval}`. `disposition` is exactly `DOCUMENT_NO_SUBSTANTIVE_CONTENT_CONFIRMED`; `variant` is exactly one of `blank`, `illegible`, or `watermark_only`; `reviewer_approval` is exactly `{decision: APPROVED, reviewer_id, approved_at, expires_at}`. Digests are lowercase SHA-256, the alias is the canonical generated upload name, timestamps are active UTC instants, and neither attestation text, source path, private filename, stamp email/timestamp values, nor signature is persisted or emitted. No compatibility alias accepts the superseded illegible-only schema, environment name, disposition, receipt, or error family. At approval time this v1 contract preceded any `acceptance-003` row; §1.11 records the later signed v1 result and the explicitly reviewed manifest-v2 extension without reinterpreting that row.

Attestation admission is deliberately narrower than parser failure: the whole PDF must have zero native-text spans, every page must require OCR, primary OCR and all 24 rescue candidates must have completed on every page under the admitted runtime, no page may have an accepted rescue clique, and the only parser outcome may be the complete no-consensus/no-text outcome. Timeout, unavailable runtime, malformed candidate output, corruption, unresolved encryption, missing page, warning, partial OCR, non-empty native/rescued text, tenant/tree/alias/content-hash/variant/text-hash drift, stale approval, untrusted signer, or invalid signature remains a hard failure. On an exact match, PostgreSQL retains `documents.parse_status=FAILED` and zero spans; no fake text or `PARSED` state is created. The same transaction persists a safe `document-no-substantive-content-receipt-v1` in document metadata, the exact admitted variant, the persistent artifact/object binding, the original parser failure, and canonical `document.no_substantive_content.confirmed` audit lineage. The API returns HTTP 201 only with explicit `terminal_disposition=DOCUMENT_NO_SUBSTANTIVE_CONTENT_CONFIRMED`; idempotent replay must reproduce that exact disposition and variant without mutation. Stable failures use the renamed `DOCUMENT_NO_SUBSTANTIVE_CONTENT_ATTESTATION_*` and `DOCUMENT_NO_SUBSTANTIVE_CONTENT_PROOF_*` families, the existing governance signature/signer codes, and admitted `DOCUMENT_NO_SUBSTANTIVE_CONTENT_CONFIRMED`.

The upload idempotency key additionally binds the verified attestation envelope hash when one is used. Because middleware can replay a cached HTTP 201 before route execution, every confirmed-no-substantive-content replay revalidates the current secure manifest, active approval window, durable failed row, exact zero-span receipt, tenant/tree/alias/content/variant binding, and cached response before returning it. A removed, replaced, expired, or drifted attestation fails `IDEMPOTENCY_REPLAY_INVARIANT_FAILED`; a stale 201 is never replayed. Attestation bytes/hashes are included in Task-4 readiness and final proof binding so changing the secure file cannot hide behind an unchanged dotenv-path/profile HMAC.

`DOCUMENT_NO_SUBSTANTIVE_CONTENT_CONFIRMED` is an evidence-backed terminal exclusion, not a parsed document, synthetic fact, intended deferral, or permission for downstream consumers to invent content. Package/preflight and final ingestion reconciliation admit only its exact signed receipt as the sole exception to the general failed/zero-span rejection: it counts toward 267 durable source objects, but not toward parsed/span-bearing/RAG/graph/claim/Sanad/agent/debate/scoring input. Safe reconciliation reports separate derived `parsed_span_bearing_file_count` and `confirmed_no_substantive_content_file_count` totals, a variant-count map, and the attestation receipt digest. Any other failed, partial, zero-span, unsigned, or unknown-variant row still fails. The source-bound admission census proves every zero-native-text PDF page is accounted for; the separate execution census proves real OCR for the remaining admitted PDF pages and both PNGs, while signed classifier receipts prove the two pre-OCR terminal pages. Durable-text counts separately reflect the exact confirmed exclusions.

The terminal reconciliation receipt is bumped to `slice100-terminal-ingestion-reconciliation-v3`, and the Task-4 durable proof receipt to `slice100-task4-durable-ingestion-proof-v3`; prior receipts are rejected rather than reinterpreted. Preparation/restart hashes, parser aggregates, authenticated downloads, and zero-reupload proof all bind the confirmed-exclusion count, variant counts, and receipt digest. Run document preflight reports it as `DOCUMENT_NO_SUBSTANTIVE_CONTENT_CONFIRMED`/non-usable with zero source spans, while remaining `COMPLETED` only when all other rows are usable and this exact signed exclusion is current; parsed-only repository loads and every downstream evidence consumer continue to exclude the failed row.

The operator-only locator is `python -m idis.tools.real_example_source_lookup --acceptance-namespace acceptance-002 --generated-upload-name <safe-alias> --acknowledge-private-terminal-output`. It pins `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\real_example`, derives the canonical sealed ledger path, rebuilds and reconciles the complete source contract and hash-chain ledger, checks the locator hash plus current file SHA/stat/containment/reparse state, and resolves exactly one row. It requires a controlling Windows console and writes the private source path once through a verified `CONOUT$` `WriteConsoleW` handle; stdout, stderr, reports, logs, exceptions, return values, clipboard, network, database, and API receive no path. Non-TTY/redirection fails `SOURCE_LOOKUP_TTY_REQUIRED`. The operator must visually inspect the page and obtain a reviewer-approved signed envelope before the manifest may be configured; the tool itself never creates or approves an attestation.

Logical `acceptance-003` uses fresh PostgreSQL database `idis_acceptance_003`, object-store subdirectory `acceptance-003`, Redis logical database 2, and report/ledger root `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\.local_reports\slice100\acceptance-003`. The clean profile update changes exactly the same four namespace values described in section 1.9 and, only after the explicit inspection/approval, adds the one approved `IDIS_REAL_EXAMPLE_NO_SUBSTANTIVE_CONTENT_ATTESTATION_MANIFEST_PATH`; no other value changes. Read-only and owner-only file/parent checks are re-proved before launch, and the new safe profile-HMAC digest is recorded. The database and paths must be absent and Redis DB 2 empty before use. Migration remains exactly through `0035`; no new schema is introduced.

Initial durable gate mode must use only the canonical absolute `<safe-report-dir>\prepare-ledger.jsonl` via `--prepare-ledger`; restart mode must use that exact same file only via `--resume-ledger`. The report leaf must match `acceptance-[0-9]{3}` and the ledger must be its direct child named exactly `prepare-ledger.jsonl`. Mixed flags, a relative/parent-traversal path, wrong filename, symlink/reparse alias, or cross-namespace report/ledger pair fails before client construction or source/API access. The strict-launcher admission matcher enforces the same mutually exclusive flag families.

RED fixtures include project-authored faint scans whose primary path is forced empty: one genuine phrase produces the sole cross-transform/both-PSM agreement clique and is rescued, while engineered noise remains at or below the reference agreement and is rejected. Boundary tests cover `0.799999` versus `0.800000`, low-confidence/short/oversize/malformed/missing candidates, one-transform/one-PSM/two-supporter sets, two disjoint or overlapping qualifying cliques, all fixed pixels/config/order, common deadline/concurrency/cleanup, exact rescue locator/metadata, terminal-v3 mutation rejection, signed-envelope schema/signature/tenant/tree/alias/hash/variant/time binding, exact failed-row terminal exclusion, rejection of the superseded illegible-only names, local lookup non-emission, and report/ledger namespace binding. The real blocker is then exercised through the actual authenticated API path in `acceptance-003`, from zero, under one final code/plan/profile closure.

### 1.11 Approved Task 4 exemplar-anchored watermark-page classifier, batch review, and acceptance-004 restart

The post-OCR classifier ordering and v1 receipt fields recorded in this section are the historical `acceptance-004` through `acceptance-006` contract and evidence lineage. They remain immutable facts about those sealed attempts, but they do not define the current runtime path: for `acceptance-007`, the classifier-first ordering, bounded candidate render, v2 pre-OCR receipt, and zero-primary/zero-rescue rule in §1.16 supersede only those historical execution-order and receipt-shape clauses. The signed exemplar, batch identity, reviewer dispositions, candidate membership, and sanity cap remain binding unless §1.16 explicitly refines them.

The `acceptance-003` stop is accepted and sealed. It durably uploaded 111 source rows: 110 `PARSED` rows and one signed `watermark_only` terminal exclusion, with 39,942 persisted spans. The next row, safe alias `company-002-document-00054.pdf` and content SHA-256 `d3a1bd7c9d3c7e117026208aafaedf41cbfa378a2b945146109b517d858d1670`, failed with outer code `DATA_ROOM_DURABLE_HANDOFF_INCOMPLETE`, underlying `DOCUMENT_NO_SUBSTANTIVE_CONTENT_ATTESTATION_MISMATCH`, and `OCR_RESCUE_NO_CONSENSUS`. Its sealed preparation-ledger SHA-256 is `878e814ee72f252710ea94befd48f2e4f7209eb023abe56896e37937280a73b6`; the append-only reconciliation-log file SHA-256 after recording the stop is `f57bb7181c982e6f14be4d229de211e0b843ea10294ad5686e4cabbe425c5848`. The `acceptance-003` database, object subtree, Redis DB 2, report root, and ledger remain immutable acceptance evidence: no resume, selective retry, deletion, relabeling, or further write is permitted.

The reviewer-attested `company-002-document-00053.pdf`, content SHA-256 `660891ceb200f7ea337e55377ab6d78beda9081e3358200d7cc18cb9d6b3cd5b`, is the sole signed class exemplar for code-pinned policy `exemplar-watermark-page-v1`. Its prior signed one-document envelope SHA-256 is `07c04e65b3475b6f8e9ec47f4cc4f6589fb1798498482b82a1bb0c9fe0942329`. The classifier is not a free-form watermark detector and cannot tune itself from the data room. A page reaches classification only after all of the following have completed in order:

1. the admitted source format is page-bearing and the page has exactly zero native-text spans;
2. the admitted primary OCR execution completed and normalized to zero tokens;
3. all 24 fixed `pdf-ocr-rescue-v1` candidates completed, with no admitted rescue text and terminal `OCR_RESCUE_NO_CONSENSUS`;
4. the original 200-DPI grayscale-`L` raster has a pixel-`>=245` near-white fraction in the inclusive interval `[0.970000, 0.985000]`;
5. `ImageOps.autocontrast(..., cutoff=0)` followed by literal full-resolution binarization `pixel < 224 -> 0, else 255` has a foreground-ink ratio in the inclusive interval `[0.015000, 0.030000]`; and
6. the autocontrasted grayscale page is resized directly, without crop or repositioning, to 128 x 128 with Pillow `LANCZOS`, re-binarized by the same literal threshold, and has position-aligned foreground-mask Dice similarity at least `0.800000` to the signed exemplar bitmap produced by the identical transform.

Foreground Dice is `2 * |foreground(candidate) intersection foreground(exemplar)| / (|foreground(candidate)| + |foreground(exemplar)|)` with exact integer numerator/denominator comparison; an empty foreground on either side scores zero. Policy decisions use unrounded values. Persisted near-white, ink, and similarity values use decimal round-half-even to six places. Exact inclusive boundaries pass; one unit below or above a faintness bound, or similarity `0.799999`, fails. The admitted Poppler/Pillow/Tesseract versions, render DPI, threshold values, resampler, page dimensions, policy ID, canonical policy JSON SHA-256, signed exemplar envelope SHA-256, exemplar content SHA-256, and exemplar canonical-bitmap SHA-256 are readiness-bound. A policy or exemplar drift fails before source/API access. Cropping, deskew, inversion, morphology, adaptive thresholding, 45-degree rotation, OCR-token matching, model inference, per-document tuning, early acceptance, or any additional transform is forbidden.

The existing secret `IDIS_REAL_EXAMPLE_NO_SUBSTANTIVE_CONTENT_ATTESTATION_MANIFEST_PATH` is reused; no new table, column, migration, environment variable, feature flag, route, or provider is added. Its broadened `document-no-substantive-content-attestation-manifest-v2` contains the unchanged verified v1 exemplar envelope, a 128 x 128 bit-packed canonical exemplar bitmap and hash, and one independently signed `document-no-substantive-content-watermark-batch-attestation-v1` envelope. The packed pixels stay inside the owner-only/read-only secret and never enter a safe report, API response, audit message, log, exception, or persisted document receipt. The new signature covers the canonical payload:

`{tenant_id, source_tree_fingerprint, disposition, variant, classifier_policy_id, classifier_policy_sha256, exemplar_safe_alias, exemplar_content_sha256, exemplar_envelope_sha256, exemplar_canonical_bitmap_sha256, prescan_receipt_sha256, candidate_set_sha256, candidates, sample_set_sha256, attestation_text_sha256, reviewer_approval}`.

`disposition` remains exactly `DOCUMENT_NO_SUBSTANTIVE_CONTENT_CONFIRMED`; `variant` remains exactly `watermark_only`. `candidates` is the complete strictly sorted, duplicate-free sequence of `{safe_alias, content_sha256, page_number, canonical_render_sha256, similarity_score}` from one sealed prescan receipt. Reviewer approval retains the existing signer/trust-root/time rules. Candidate removal, insertion, reordering, duplicate alias/page/hash identity, score drift, source-tree drift, policy/exemplar drift, prescan/sample digest drift, invalid signature, stale approval, or unknown member fails closed. The legacy v1 envelope remains a valid manually reviewed one-document proof; it is not silently reinterpreted as a batch.

Every admitted classifier page persists `watermark-page-classification-v1` provenance containing the document content SHA-256, page number, policy ID/hash, exact transform chain and bounds, measured near-white and foreground-ink values, similarity metric and score, exemplar safe reference/content/envelope/canonical-bitmap hashes, canonical render hash, complete primary/rescue terminal proof hash, and batch membership. `batch_membership=ATTESTED` requires exact membership in the active signed candidate sequence. A later runtime page that was not in that sequence may still classify only when the identical policy and signed exemplar pass every gate; it records `batch_membership=RUNTIME_UNSEEN` and safe status `WATERMARK_RUNTIME_CANDIDATE_REVIEW_REQUIRED`. Such a row may let ingestion continue but prevents a final Task-4 acceptance claim until post-run reviewer confirmation is durably signed. The classifier emits no text span. A page inside an otherwise substantive multi-page document is excluded at page level while the other real spans remain; a document with no remaining substantive page retains `parse_status=FAILED`, zero spans, and the explicit terminal disposition. Both forms remain excluded from RAG, graph, claims, Sanads, agents, debate, scoring, and deliverables for the classified page.

`document-no-substantive-content-receipt-v2` carries the page-level classification proof and reviewer source (`reviewer_batch`, `signed_exemplar`, or `signed_exemplar_runtime_match`) without raw text, bitmap bytes, source paths, private filenames, stamp values, or signatures. Manual v1 receipts remain valid only for their exact signed document. The terminal reconciliation receipt becomes `slice100-terminal-ingestion-reconciliation-v4` and the durable proof becomes `slice100-task4-durable-ingestion-proof-v4`; both derive classifier-page, batch-attested, manual-v1, runtime-unseen, per-company, and variant counts from durable rows and bind the ordered receipt digest. Duplicate page claims, a terminal row with spans, an excluded page with an emitted span, stale current-manifest proof, or a prior receipt version fails rather than being reinterpreted. Upload idempotency and cached-201 replay bind and revalidate the manifest-v2 hash, policy hash, candidate-set hash, batch envelope, durable page receipt, failed/parsed row semantics, and zero excluded-page spans.

The sanity cap is per company and counts every auto-classified page, including an unseen runtime match: `100 * auto_classified_page_count <= 15 * company_file_count`. It is evaluated with integers and no rounded percentage. Thus company 001 admits at most 8 classifier pages for 58 files and company 002 at most 31 for 209 files; 9 or 32 respectively fails `WATERMARK_PAGE_SANITY_CAP_EXCEEDED`. A manual v1 attestation is reported separately and does not weaken the auto-classifier cap. The cap is checked before persisting a newly classified page, again during terminal reconciliation, and again after restart.

Before `acceptance-004`, a local read-only `watermark-page-prescan-v1` walks and rehashes all 267 source files and the exact 58/209 membership under source-tree fingerprint `dc29e07e7c605a7ba4bd3b84ad9ab481c32056e544f054a64f95946a460ef64d`. It performs no API, database, Redis, object-store, audit, network, or acceptance-namespace write. Every page with zero native text runs real primary OCR and, when primary is empty, all 24 rescue candidates before the structural classifier. The safe receipt contains only aliases, content/page/canonical-render/proof hashes, numeric scores, format/page/outcome aggregates, the complete classifier-candidate sequence, the complete below-threshold unresolved sequence, policy/exemplar/source hashes, per-company cap calculations, and self/candidate/sample-set digests. Any source mutation, unreadable page, incomplete OCR/rescue result, prescan error, below-threshold no-consensus page, or missing document 00054 fails `WATERMARK_PRESCAN_INCOMPLETE`; the batch cannot be signed and `acceptance-004` cannot start.

The 2026-07-17 checkpoint prescan completed the read-only 267-file/858,207,454-byte census, 223 PDFs/2,595 pages, and all 284 zero-native-text pages with zero scan errors. Primary OCR produced text on 274 pages; ten pages completed all 24 rescue candidates with no consensus. Exactly two passed the structural classifier: `company-002-document-00053.pdf` page 1 at persisted Dice `1.000000`, and mandatory `company-002-document-00054.pdf` page 1 at `0.819404`. The other eight were below threshold: `company-002-document-00105.pdf` pages 4 and 7, `company-002-document-00109.pdf` page 54, `company-002-document-00110.pdf` page 60, `company-002-document-00113.pdf` pages 4, 27, and 51, and `company-002-document-00122.pdf` page 2. The sealed checkpoint report remains truthfully `BLOCKED_PENDING_REVIEW`: receipt SHA-256 `8199828bed9492b987b3e6c5aec282476303b2f2fd9a95084b1564120c3380d4`, file SHA-256 `5d5143742dec8f94f2f5c5928a30a6a3478120388b0140e0dc14b65c19eadbaa`, policy SHA-256 `8ee6b1f77f51aa51cdf382ee4c11e57a4f50c2d7b51a22ef0c88fb6c446c3467`, candidate-set SHA-256 `34f5e00d1308f2ee8e146806d9389da5bf3ccd4dbc8594a698e623ae6d9a8630`, and sample-set SHA-256 `633a6dadb06df1ce3cc27552704ccf85cb527d88afc68a71875b6f640a73cddb`; it is never edited or relabelled. The review disposition is a new hash-linked receipt, not a mutation of that evidence.

On 2026-07-17 the reviewer inspected all ten staged renders. The complete watermark batch is exactly document 00053 page 1 plus document 00054 page 1, with document 00053 as the sole exemplar. Its reviewer approval is exactly `{decision: APPROVED, reviewer_id: slice100-reviewer-agent-v1, approved_at: 2026-07-17T16:00:00Z, expires_at: 2026-10-15T00:00:00Z}`. The same ruling confirms all eight below-threshold pages contain substantive imagery and are not watermark-only. A new safe, derived `watermark-page-prescan-v1` resolution receipt binds the immutable blocked-prescan receipt hash, its complete candidate and unresolved sets, the two confirmed candidate identities, the eight confirmed image-only identities, the reviewer approval, and its own digest. It alone changes status to `COMPLETE`; the original checkpoint remains sealed and unchanged. The blocked prescan's diagnostic `canonical_render_sha256` representation is not copied forward as if it were the signed runtime bitmap format: the resolved receipt deterministically normalizes all ten reviewed renders to the current policy's 128 x 128 row-major one-bit packing (white bit `1`) and binds the former representation through the immutable prior receipt/file/unresolved-set hashes. The manifest-v2 batch envelope's `prescan_receipt_sha256` binds this resolved receipt and therefore the original prescan lineage; it cannot turn an omitted or unknown page green. Once the envelope verifies against the pinned public key, the ten staged review renders are deleted; their pixels and paths never enter a report or durable row.

The real signed result is now fixed: prior blocked receipt `8199828bed9492b987b3e6c5aec282476303b2f2fd9a95084b1564120c3380d4`, prior blocked file `5d5143742dec8f94f2f5c5928a30a6a3478120388b0140e0dc14b65c19eadbaa`, prior unresolved set `5126c58968a9ce9eca737af8787f5d2f9e6c4e0ccfd427b97e8d2fa02dc3696d`, policy `8ee6b1f77f51aa51cdf382ee4c11e57a4f50c2d7b51a22ef0c88fb6c446c3467`, exemplar packed bitmap `2b94839ca7d30c6cdc44ede2e0e66d06f9ffbb390b6da7816418afd1f0bf1dd0`, normalized two-candidate set `7d86b7169bbe74d278be99e1474dccbb42b73f96fb57d9f2aa8a6a75b18945bc`, reviewed eight-page image-only set `d67e9ae0e85660c1e7bdfa7e4cb0d6d64df204cf69c11b26368343be5c7707eb`, resolved receipt `7087faf56d1c539722c7a315159f9b6e86264726a3283b709359e126c583e0c0`, batch envelope `073e82574b98b905444a633546de1f83f60228e1b196672acd3a2b60b2879676`, and manifest-v2 `dc330f33cfd1ab6666907d1454ca2a1501933417e5559d200d412be4b4e102dd`. The four governance files are protected owner-only and read-only; production secure loading and both Ed25519 signatures re-verified against public-key hash `05b0e896e64b4f9344e65e1ec45309907a0ef4825c193a253877f522d216260c`. All ten staged render files and their now-empty staging root were then deleted exactly as approved.

`image_only_page` is page-level parser provenance for a zero-native-text, zero-primary-token, complete-rescue-no-consensus page that failed the watermark classifier and contains substantive imagery. It is not a no-substantive-content disposition, not a text span, and not evidence that the image was semantically understood. Each exact record is `image-only-page-receipt-v1` and contains only the parent document content SHA-256, one-based safe page locator, a deterministic page-content/canonical-render SHA-256, the completed OCR terminal-proof SHA-256, classifier policy/proof hash, `classification=image_only_page`, occurrence scope, and review status. `page_content_sha256` is exactly SHA-256 over the current classifier policy's 2,048-byte 128 x 128 row-major one-bit packed bitmap (white bit `1`), so the signed review identity is independently recomputable at runtime rather than copied from an earlier diagnostic hash. The eight pages inspected in this ruling use `occurrence_scope=REVIEWER_CONFIRMED`, `review_status=REVIEWED_SUBSTANTIVE_IMAGE`, and create zero post-run spot-check debt. A later deterministic occurrence not in the reviewed set uses `occurrence_scope=RUNTIME_UNSEEN`, `review_status=IMAGE_ONLY_PAGE_POST_RUN_SPOT_CHECK_REQUIRED`, and is included in the post-run spot-check aggregate. No raw pixels, OCR noise, source path, private filename, rendered file path, or reviewer signature is persisted or emitted. Duplicate page locators, missing hashes, a page with native or accepted OCR text, an incomplete rescue/classifier chain, or a page simultaneously claimed as watermark-only fails closed.

An `image_only_page` blocks nothing only when its parent document has at least one independently persisted non-empty substantive span from another page. That document remains `PARSED`; its real spans are retained, the image-only page emits zero spans, and its records/count/digest are persisted in document metadata and safe audit provenance. Conversely, a document whose every page is image-only has no evidence-bearing span and remains `FAILED` with stable blocker `IMAGE_ONLY_DOCUMENT_REVIEW_REQUIRED`; it cannot use page classifications to manufacture `PARSED`, `DOCUMENT_NO_SUBSTANTIVE_CONTENT_CONFIRMED`, or semantic evidence. Future runtime image-only occurrences use the identical deterministic contract, are admitted only in span-bearing parent documents, and are reported as safe per-company/page counts plus ordered receipt digests for post-run reviewer spot-checking. A future all-image-only document always stops the run for explicit reviewer disposition. The terminal v4 reconciliation, restart verifier, and isolation proof derive these states from durable documents and spans rather than trusting client counters. This refinement adds no table, column, migration, environment variable, feature flag, route, or provider.

Reviewer samples are deterministic. If the candidate sequence has five or fewer entries, every candidate is staged; otherwise document 00054 is mandatory and the remaining four are the lowest SHA-256 ranks of canonical `{policy_sha256, source_tree_fingerprint, safe_alias, content_sha256, page_number}`. Renders are generated through the pinned transform, named only with safe aliases/page numbers, and placed outside the repository and `.local_reports` under an owner-only/read-only review directory. The safe receipt contains only their ordered identities and `sample_set_sha256`; it contains no local path or pixels. The reviewer spot-checks at most five candidate samples including document 00054 and issues one signed batch attestation over the complete enumerated candidate set. Below-threshold pages are staged separately for explicit reviewer disposition; they never become candidates by sampling or status editing and may become a separately signed exemplar only through a later approved amendment.

Logical `acceptance-004` uses fresh PostgreSQL database `idis_acceptance_004`, object-store subdirectory `acceptance-004`, Redis logical database 3, and report/ledger root `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\.local_reports\slice100\acceptance-004`. The strict-profile edit follows the established owner-only/read-only procedure and changes only both database DSNs, object-store base directory, ledger/report namespace, Redis URL, and the approved manifest-v2 path/content binding; no provider or semantic setting changes. The database and paths must be absent and Redis DB 3 empty before use.
