Migration remains exactly through `0035`. `acceptance-004` starts all 267 rows from zero only after the prescan is complete, every below-threshold page is resolved, the reviewer sample check is recorded, and the batch envelope verifies against the pinned public key. Any failure seals the new namespace and records its safe aggregates; no prior namespace is resumed or erased.

### 1.12 Acceptance-004 production-wiring stop (2026-07-17)

`acceptance-004` stopped fail-closed at the first signed watermark exemplar, not at a newly discovered page class. The production API durably accepted 110 source rows (company aliases 001/002: 58/52; 531,747,614 bytes), persisted 110 `PARSED` documents and 39,938 spans, and then persisted safe alias `company-002-document-00053.pdf` (content SHA-256 `660891ceb200f7ea337e55377ab6d78beda9081e3358200d7cc18cb9d6b3cd5b`) as one `FAILED` document with `OCR_RESCUE_NO_CONSENSUS`. The row has no terminal receipt, watermark classification, or image-only receipt. No package or manifest was created, and restart/zero-reupload/isolation verification was not reached. The API was stopped and port 8000 was confirmed closed before sealing.

The validated append-only preparation ledger has sequence 112, tip SHA-256 `1143b14fce27b92f7c0423282ac3db729448200028b087c0d6bcb67e7795d66c`, and file SHA-256 `3ff06af4aeb88d2524b8a56069a9f655a3672ecea352e9d1c5c1e9f166619e13`. The safe reconciliation entry is preserved beside it; its file SHA-256 is `522e7c4215700469fd03f9b93ecfc93d4b492689c13c4072ad964da6c87a69fd`. Every acceptance-004 report file is read-only. Its database, object namespace, Redis DB 3, ledger, and logs remain the versioned failed-attempt evidence and must not be resumed, selectively edited, relabeled, or deleted.

Read-only code tracing proves a production composition gap. The strict launcher loads the profile and the default ingestion builder verifies manifest v2 and supplies it to `IngestionService`, but `build_default_ingestion_service` derives only the eight reviewed `image_only_page` admissions for parser configuration. `classify_watermark_page` has no production caller; `OcrConfig`, `ParserRegistry.parse_bytes`, and `parse_pdf` carry no typed watermark runtime configuration or signed batch identity. Consequently the PDF no-consensus branch can emit only an image-only admission or the observed OCR failure, while `IngestionService` can persist v2 watermark provenance only when parser metadata was already populated. Direct classifier tests and persistence tests with prebuilt metadata did not cover this end-to-end construction seam. This stop records the defect only: no acceptance-005 namespace, implementation, migration, environment name, table, flag, live call, or retry is authorized without the next reviewer ruling.

### 1.13 Approved Task 4 production-composition fix and acceptance-005 restart

The explicit configuration threading and composition-root anti-masking rule in this section remain binding. Its post-OCR classifier ordering and full-resolution classifier-worker details describe the historical `acceptance-005`/`acceptance-006` implementation target; §1.16 supersedes those two execution details for the current path without weakening the composition-root requirement.

The 2026-07-17 reviewer ruling authorizes the narrowly scoped production-composition repair. RED must first reproduce the sealed `acceptance-004` failure through both real composition roots: (1) `build_default_ingestion_service`/`IngestionService` with a configured, cryptographically verified manifest-v2 and a watermark-only fixture PDF and (2) the production API upload route over the same contract. Before implementation, both tests must expect the watermark-only terminal receipt yet fail with the actual `OCR_RESCUE_NO_CONSENSUS`/HTTP 422 behavior. The repair then derives one immutable typed watermark runtime configuration from the already verified manifest and passes it explicitly through `defaults.py` -> `ParserRegistry.parse_bytes` -> `parse_pdf` and its OCR page execution. No parser may read the environment, global state, manifest path, trusted key, or admission set ambiently. The classifier runs only after the existing zero-native-text, zero-primary-token, complete-rescue-no-consensus eligibility proof and returns text-free provenance; every existing below-threshold, cap, mixed-document, all-watermark, and fail-closed rule remains unchanged.

Standing anti-masking rule: every parser- or ingestion-level acceptance capability must have at least one test that enters through a production composition root (`IngestionService` built by the default builder or the actual API upload route). Direct unit tests, injected parser metadata, repository tests, and receipt replay tests remain necessary but cannot establish that a capability is implemented for acceptance. A capability with no composition-root test is incomplete and blocks the acceptance namespace. This rule applies retroactively in this diff to standard OCR rescue, reviewer-confirmed `image_only_page` in a mixed document, and the all-image-only `IMAGE_ONLY_DOCUMENT_REVIEW_REQUIRED` branch. Their composition tests must exercise real builder/parser routing and may not pre-populate the result metadata that they are intended to prove. Any other missing construction seam found by those tests is repaired within this same narrow parser-composition scope before a new acceptance attempt.

The same repair keeps the post-OCR watermark work inside the original configured OCR wall clock and resource boundary. Page geometry is preflighted deterministically at the pinned render DPI against the existing `MAX_OCR_IMAGE_PIXELS` admission bound before rendering. Poppler rendering plus Pillow classification executes in a killable child process, never as unbounded image work in the API process, and any timeout, oversized page, malformed worker result, or incomplete page result fails closed. This reuses the existing bound and timeout; it adds no environment variable or feature flag.

Unresolved pages are partitioned exactly once across the two authorized terminal page classes. A page cannot be both `watermark_only` and `image_only_page`; signed image-only admissions may cover only below-threshold pages, and the union of accepted OCR pages plus the two disjoint terminal classes must cover every OCR-required page. A mixed document retains only independently extracted substantive spans. If no substantive span remains while any page is substantive imagery, the document remains fail-closed for explicit reviewer disposition rather than being mislabeled as wholly watermark-only.

Durable classifier provenance uses the literal executed chain: `poppler-200dpi`, then the governed policy operations `convert-grayscale-L`, `measure-near-white-ge-245`, `autocontrast-cutoff-0`, `measure-foreground-lt-224`, `resize-grayscale-128x128-LANCZOS`, `binary-foreground-lt-224`, and `foreground-dice`. The similarity label is exactly `foreground_dice`, meaning position-aligned foreground-mask intersection. The earlier runtime labels that claimed a pre-resize binary transform and a multiset metric were inaccurate diagnostics and are rejected rather than persisted for acceptance-005.

Only after the two watermark RED tests are observed failing for the sealed production reason, all new composition-root tests pass, and the full declared-environment verification set is green may logical `acceptance-005` be created from zero. It uses PostgreSQL database `idis_acceptance_005`, object-store subdirectory `acceptance-005`, Redis logical database 4, and report/ledger root `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\.local_reports\slice100\acceptance-005`. The strict-profile update changes only the same approved namespace values as prior clean restarts; the verified manifest-v2 path/content binding remains unchanged. `acceptance-004` and its ledger/reconciliation hashes stay sealed. Migration remains exactly through `0035`. The next stop is either a complete 267-row proof followed by API kill/restart, authenticated zero-reupload/download survival and tenant-isolation verification, or the next real blocker sealed under the same evidence rules.

### 1.14 Approved classifier-domain validation repair and acceptance-006 restart

This section records the historical domain-validation repair admitted for `acceptance-006`. Its separation of signed canonical-exemplar validation from candidate-raster validation remains binding; its full-resolution runtime candidate acquisition is superseded by the bounded 512 x 512 candidate contract in §1.16 and must not be treated as the current `acceptance-007` classifier path.

`acceptance-005` stopped during strict API composition before Uvicorn listened or the durable gate began. The database reached migration `0035` and contains four durable startup-audit rows but zero tenants, deals, documents, document spans, or stored objects. Source rows attempted/uploaded and uploaded bytes are all zero. Redis logical database 4 contains one recorded startup-residue key. No `prepare-ledger.jsonl`, proof receipt, package, or manifest exists; the absence of a preparation ledger is explicit evidence that the gate never began, and an empty ledger must never be fabricated. The safe reconciliation file SHA-256 is `b279f62420af98f8124e857518faa7a7b449ce3fd1a2213e73e917f1970c8898`; it records blocker `WATERMARK_PAGE_EXEMPLAR_INVALID` at `api_startup_composition`/`watermark_runtime_config`. The `acceptance-005` report directory and its four evidence files remain read-only and hash-verifiable, but this is not represented as owner-only NTFS immutability: its inherited ACL was not changed by this ruling. Its database, empty object namespace, Redis DB 4 residue, reports, and logs remain untouched.

The 2026-07-17 reviewer ruling authorizes one RED-first classifier-domain repair. An operator-backed test must load the already configured strict profile, verify the actual signed manifest-v2 against the pinned public key through the production loader, and reproduce the current construction failure before implementation. Ordinary offline CI skips that test only when `IDIS_STRICT_DOTENV_PATH` is absent; a configured but unreadable, unverified, or invalid operator artifact fails secret-safely. No additional environment variable, test switch, runtime flag, table, column, migration, or route is introduced.

The classifier has two non-interchangeable typed validation domains. A full-resolution raster is the real page rendered at the policy's pinned 200 DPI. Only that type may be measured against the full-resolution near-white and foreground-ink bounds and reduced through grayscale, deterministic autocontrast, 128 x 128 LANCZOS downscale, and pinned binarization. A signed canonical exemplar is the resulting row-major 128 x 128 one-bit bitmap: it must have the exact derived packed length, unpack to the exact policy dimensions and binary pixel domain, contain both foreground and background, match its signed canonical-bitmap SHA-256, and remain bound to a valid exemplar reference and the exact classifier-policy SHA-256. The verified batch must contain exactly one self-candidate with the exemplar alias, exemplar document SHA-256, page number 1, the same signed canonical-bitmap SHA-256, and exact similarity `1.000000`; wrong page, canonical drift, non-self score, or duplicate identity fails the signed-manifest boundary. Canonical material is never re-rendered, autocontrasted, resized, or tested against full-resolution faint-raster fractions. Runtime comparison validates the signed canonical type once, prepares only the full-resolution candidate raster, and performs the pinned foreground-Dice calculation between their canonical masks. The prescan/full-exemplar path may still apply the full-resolution bounds to an actual full-resolution exemplar before constructing its canonical type; neither validator accepts the other type.

Fixture realism is part of the standing anti-masking rule. Every valid synthetic exemplar used as positive test evidence must be created through the same production sequence that creates an operator exemplar: create a source page/PDF, render it with the pinned renderer and DPI, execute the governed grayscale/autocontrast/downscale/binarize/pack constructor, bind those results into the manifest, and sign the manifest with a test Ed25519 key before loading it through production verification. A hand-assembled 128 x 128 bitmap or arbitrary 2,048-byte string cannot establish valid behavior. A negative validator test may mutate a valid production-constructed base only to prove that the mutation is rejected. The RED set must include both the actual operator artifact and a deterministic source raster whose full-resolution faintness is valid while its canonical foreground fraction is outside the full-resolution range, plus a boundary assertion that runtime never submits 128 x 128 signed material to the full-resolution preparation/faint validator.

Only after those REDs turn green and the complete declared-environment verification set passes may logical `acceptance-006` be created from zero. ### 1.16 Approved classifier-first and deterministic OCR render-admission repair

The 2026-07-18 reviewer ruling authorizes the narrow RED-first repair for the resource-bound failure recorded in §1.15. Classification ordering changes only for pages whose native extraction produced zero text: before any full-resolution OCR admission calculation or OCR worker call, the parser renders the page once through the fixed `watermark-runtime-candidate-render-v1` contract into an exact 512 x 512 grayscale candidate, with no crop, adaptive sizing, retry, OCR, or content-dependent transform, then deterministically reduces that bounded raster to the governed 128 x 128 binarized comparison bitmap. Its canonical governance payload pins Poppler `size=(512, 512)` (literal `-scale-to-x 512 -scale-to-y 512`, not the aspect-preserving scalar form), PNG, grayscale, one page, one thread, exact output dimensions 512 x 512, then the existing grayscale, near-white measurement, deterministic autocontrast, foreground measurement, 128 x 128 LANCZOS, binary threshold, and foreground-Dice chain. The fixed 512 acquisition is the smallest validated bounded power-of-two raster that preserves the unchanged signed faintness bounds on the real exemplar: 128 produced near-white/foreground fractions `0.959534/0.048462`, 256 produced `0.968918/0.034744`, and both correctly failed; 512 produced `0.973351/0.028603` and foreground-Dice `0.982112`, all inside the existing bounds. This is design-time evidence only, not a runtime candidate ladder or per-document tuning: every runtime page receives exactly one 512 x 512 prepass. `watermark-runtime-candidate-render-v1` has its own canonical-JSON SHA-256 persisted in every pre-OCR classification; it is code/plan-closure governance and is never substituted for the signed `exemplar-watermark-page-v1` hash. Actual output other than exactly 512 x 512, more or fewer than one image, timeout, or renderer drift fails closed.

This is an explicit versioned dual-policy bridge. The bounded runtime candidate is compared with the existing signed canonical exemplar and the existing signed faint-raster/similarity bounds, while the signed exemplar remains the production-constructed 200-DPI source-page render sealed in manifest v2. Its canonical bitmap, classifier policy hash, and reviewer batch signature are neither regenerated nor claimed to govern the new Poppler acquisition command. Runtime provenance carries both `signed_exemplar_policy_id`/`signed_exemplar_policy_sha256` and `runtime_candidate_policy_id`/`runtime_candidate_policy_sha256`, and must never label the bounded candidate as `poppler-200dpi`. A qualifying match terminates that page as `watermark_only`, records `schema_version=watermark-page-classification-v2`, `classification_phase=pre_ocr`, `full_resolution_ocr_attempted=false`, the literal bounded-render transform chain, actual candidate dimensions, and the existing exemplar/similarity bindings, and never submits that page to the OCR adapter. The pre-OCR receipt has no `ocr_terminal_proof_sha256` and must not fabricate primary-token or 24/24 rescue facts. The latest ruling supersedes the prior post-OCR runtime-eligibility ordering only; historical acceptance-006 diagnostics and receipts remain immutable, and the manifest's signed canonical exemplar remains an identity/evidence binding rather than false proof that OCR ran in acceptance-007.

Batch membership is version-aware. For v2, exact signed candidate identity is the tuple of safe alias, document content SHA-256, and one-based page number; because document bytes and page identity are cryptographically fixed, a matching signed identity remains `ATTESTED` after the new runtime classifier independently passes, while the receipt persists the signed 200-DPI candidate hash/score separately from the runtime 128 x 128 candidate hash/score and never asserts that they are equal. A matching signed identity with content or page drift fails the manifest boundary. A passing exemplar match whose identity was not enumerated remains `RUNTIME_UNSEEN`, is terminal as previously approved, and carries the existing post-run reviewer-confirmation debt. A below-threshold candidate proceeds to OCR and does not become a terminal receipt. Any timeout, malformed or incomplete bounded render, policy/exemplar mismatch, or classifier internal failure fails closed; only the governed below-threshold outcome admits OCR.

For each remaining zero-native-text PDF page, OCR raster admission uses the immutable descending ladder `200, 150, 120, 96, 72`, identified as `ocr-dpi-ladder-v1`. The ladder is PDF-page-only: standalone PNG OCR and video-frame OCR retain their existing admission and raster contracts. Projection is computed before rendering as `ceil(width_points * dpi / 72) * ceil(height_points * dpi / 72)`, including the effective PDF page box and `/UserUnit`, with checked positive finite integer arithmetic. The chosen DPI is the first and therefore highest ladder member whose projected pixel count is at most the unchanged `MAX_OCR_IMAGE_PIXELS=20_000_000`; equality is admitted. Selection is per page, arithmetic-only, and occurs before rendering. Only the selected rung renders, and its actual width, height, and pixel count are revalidated against the admitted projection and bound before Tesseract. No adaptive search, intermediate DPI, crop, tiling, render-and-retry, limit change, environment override, or retry at a different DPI is allowed. A page that does not fit at the 72-DPI floor terminates before Poppler/Tesseract with stable parser code `ocr_image_too_large`; its secret-safe details contain the one-based page number, `ocr-dpi-ladder-v1`, floor DPI, floor projected pixel count, and maximum pixel count. It is never silently skipped, converted to an image-only receipt, or represented as parsed.

Every OCR text span produced under this admission path persists one exact nested locator object named `ocr_render_admission`: `{schema_version: "ocr-render-admission-v1", ladder_version: "ocr-dpi-ladder-v1", configured_dpi: 200, chosen_dpi: <integer>, projected_pixel_count: <integer>, max_pixel_count: 20000000}`. Document metadata persists the same records in ascending page order as `ocr_render_admissions`, each augmented only with its one-based `page`, plus `ocr_render_admissions_sha256`, the SHA-256 of their canonical JSON array. The parser derives these records before worker launch, passes the typed immutable plan explicitly to the OCR adapter, validates returned page membership against that plan, and attaches only the matching record to returned primary or rescue spans. Duplicate pages, unplanned pages, missing records, a rendered size that differs from the admitted projection, or provenance drift fails closed. Native-text spans have no fabricated OCR admission object. Watermark-only pages have the bounded classifier receipt instead and cannot also have OCR admission provenance. This adds no table, column, migration, environment variable, feature flag, provider, transform candidate, or resource-limit change.

Fresh PDF collection emits only `ocr-terminal-evidence-v3`. It records the exact sorted, duplicate-free `ocr_page_numbers`, exact sorted, duplicate-free `preclassified_page_numbers`, source-bound `ocr_required_page_count`, page-bound `ocr_render_admissions`, and `ocr_render_admissions_sha256`; the OCR and preclassified sets must be disjoint and their union must cover every source-bound required page exactly once. The admission-page list must equal the OCR-page list, its digest must rederive, and every OCR span must carry the identical matching page admission. Terminal reconciliation receives the immutable-source content-SHA-to-required-page and content-SHA-to-render-admission maps from the read-only census and compares each row to its own exact map entry; aggregate-equivalent cross-file page swaps, an allowed but non-highest DPI rung, or a fictional projected-pixel count fail closed. A classifier-first page therefore contributes only to `preclassified_page_numbers`, never to OCR counts, admissions, confidence, primary, or rescue evidence. `ocr-terminal-evidence-v2` remains compatibility evidence for pre-amendment rows and the unchanged PNG OCR surface; it cannot satisfy a fresh `acceptance-007` PDF proof.

RED evidence is mandatory before production edits. First, the exact read-only source artifact `company-002-document-00053.pdf` must enter both `IngestionService` through the production default builder and the real API upload route using the verified signed operator manifest. Both tests must reproduce the current resource-bound failure, then require a durable `watermark_only` terminal receipt and prove the full-resolution OCR adapter/worker was called zero times. Second, a synthetic poster-size PDF containing real text must be generated through the same production fixture pipeline used for PDF construction/rendering and must exceed the 200-DPI projection while fitting a lower ladder member; through a production composition root it must produce non-empty spans whose locator and document metadata carry the exact chosen-DPI provenance and digest. Third, a production-constructed synthetic page whose projection exceeds the bound even at 72 DPI must enter a production composition root, return `ocr_image_too_large`, and prove zero OCR worker calls. Direct unit contracts remain additional evidence, not replacements for these composition-root tests. The standing fixture-realism and anti-masking rules remain binding.

Only after those RED failures are captured for the intended missing behavior, the implementation turns them green, and the complete declared-environment verification set passes may logical `acceptance-007` be created from zero. It uses PostgreSQL database `idis_acceptance_007`, object-store subdirectory `acceptance-007`, Redis logical database 6, and report/ledger root `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\.local_reports\slice100\acceptance-007`. The strict-profile update changes exactly the database component in both approved DSNs, the existing object-store base directory, and the existing Redis URL; the CLI report/ledger root is not a dotenv value. All other profile bytes, including the manifest-v2 binding, source root, API URL, `bootstrap-v1`, promoted-prompts-off setting, providers, and semantic configuration remain unchanged. The owner-only/read-only edit and ACL/HMAC verification procedure is identical to prior clean restarts.

Before any `acceptance-007` write, PostgreSQL database `idis_acceptance_007`, object namespace `acceptance-007`, and report root `acceptance-007` must be absent, Redis logical database 6 must be empty, port 8000 must be closed, and all seven sealed `acceptance-006` evidence files must match their recorded member set and hashes. The report directory is created without `-Force` only after that absence proof. Migration remains exactly through `0035` via the strict launcher. The run starts all 267 rows from zero; `acceptance-006` is never resumed. The next stop is either the complete 267-row durable proof followed by forced API kill/restart, a different boot identity, zero upload POSTs, authenticated artifact download/hash survival, exact 58/209 reconciliation, and the complete tenant-isolation denial matrix, or the next real blocker with its namespace stopped, reconciled, hashed, and sealed.

### 1.18 Approved exact Poppler render-rounding repair and acceptance-008 restart

The 2026-07-18 reviewer ruling authorizes one narrow RED-first correction to §1.16. OCR admission and render verification use two distinct quantities that must never be conflated. The **admission ceiling** remains the existing conservative per-axis round-up projection over the effective PDF page box, `/UserUnit`, rotation, and selected ladder DPI. It is used only to select the highest permitted `ocr-dpi-ladder-v1` rung under the unchanged `MAX_OCR_IMAGE_PIXELS=20_000_000` resource bound. The **expected render dimensions** are separately computed by a typed, pure contract that reproduces the deterministic dimension-rounding rule used by the pinned Poppler 25.12.0 file-backed render path for the same page geometry and DPI. The worker compares actual output with those expected dimensions, never with the admission ceiling. Expected or actual dimensions cannot retroactively admit a rung rejected by the conservative projection; every actual raster must independently remain at or below the unchanged pixel bound.

Exact replication is the required implementation. Runtime provenance pins the literal Poppler version `25.12.0`, the expected-dimension policy identifier and canonical policy SHA-256, and the comparison mode. Strict readiness resolves the executable actually used by the file-backed render, verifies that exact version before admission, and passes the same resolved binary identity into worker execution; a later ambient `PATH` lookup may not select another installation. A runtime Poppler version or executable-identity mismatch fails closed before OCR. If reproducible RED evidence proves that one exact formula is unstable across relevant code paths of that pinned version, the only approved fallback is a documented maximum absolute delta of one pixel per axis between expected and actual dimensions. That fallback may not relax the actual-pixel resource check, may not admit any different DPI, and must persist `comparison_mode=bounded-plus-minus-one`, expected-to-actual width and height deltas, and `tolerance_used=true` on every affected page. Exact matches persist `comparison_mode=exact-poppler-25.12.0`, zero deltas, and `tolerance_used=false`. A delta outside the selected contract, a version mismatch, an executable-identity mismatch, or any actual pixel count above the resource bound fails closed. Tolerance is not implemented merely for convenience: absent captured pinned-version instability, the exact mode is mandatory.

The completed RED characterization selects the exact path; no tolerance fallback is activated. Policy `poppler-pdftoppm-25.12.0-dimensions-v1` (canonical SHA-256 `483ee7542fbcb6a69dc3949072ef2897471130e409cc41318395e72fec3e0253`) reproduces Poppler 25.12.0's PDF-number lexer by accumulating each fractional decimal digit into binary64 in lexical order, taking the absolute difference of the independently parsed `MediaBox` endpoints, applying `extent * dpi / 72.0` in that order, then `ceil`, with axes swapped only for rotations 90/270. `/UserUnit` remains part of the conservative admission ceiling but is not applied by the pinned Poppler renderer's expected-dimension model. This formula matched 100 five-rung boundary cases and all 31 real corpus pages where admission and render dimensions differ, with zero mismatches. Because pypdf may normalize unusually long legal numeric lexemes, the model makes no universal lexical-recovery claim: any resulting expected/actual mismatch remains a hard failure, never a tolerance or silent acceptance.

The resolved runtime identity binds the implementation as well as the thin executable. On Windows, strict resolution explicitly selects co-located `pdftoppm.exe` and `pdfinfo.exe`, never same-name command shims, verifies both as version `25.12.0`, and hashes both `pdftoppm.exe` and sibling `poppler.dll`, where the dimension logic resides. Their path-free canonical runtime-identity SHA-256 is `8ebfb07d9a8e5e01c12cc2994837a122cc6c94a930c657e10ffc655db8f0cc72`. The worker rechecks version, co-location, executable hash, and library hash immediately before render and passes the admitted directory explicitly through `pdf2image`; paths are never persisted or emitted in safe evidence.

Fresh OCR provenance is versioned as `ocr-render-admission-v2` and `ocr-terminal-evidence-v4` so the three dimensions cannot be mistaken for one another. Each OCR span and its matching document/terminal receipt persist the page, chosen DPI, `ocr-dpi-ladder-v1`, maximum pixel count, admission-projected width/height/pixel count, Poppler-expected width/height/pixel count, actual rendered width/height/pixel count, pinned Poppler version and resolved-executable identity hash, expected-dimension policy identifier/hash, comparison mode, signed width/height deltas, and tolerance-used boolean. The admission projection remains pre-render arithmetic; expected dimensions remain renderer-model arithmetic; actual dimensions remain observed worker output. Their canonical page-ordered array and digest must rederive exactly across span, document metadata, durable receipt, collection, and reconciliation. A fresh `acceptance-008` PDF proof requires those v2/v4 schemas; legacy `ocr-render-admission-v1` and `ocr-terminal-evidence-v3` records remain honest historical `acceptance-007` evidence but cannot satisfy the new run.

RED evidence is mandatory before production edits. First, the exact read-only artifact `company-001-document-00004.pdf` with content SHA-256 `732a18b4543c5c7db3838f0a87d14816e896b41f9d09b3c9177c177561655b5e` must reproduce the current one-pixel rejection through the real file-backed OCR worker and separately through the real API upload composition root with the signed operator manifest configured. The green contract requires successful non-empty OCR, admission projection 1,655 x 2,339, Poppler-expected and actual dimensions 1,654 x 2,339, unchanged resource admission, and identical durable provenance at every surface. Second, a fixture matrix must cover page sizes engineered on both sides of Poppler rounding boundaries, including half-pixel cases, at every pinned ladder DPI 200, 150, 120, 96, and 72. Every fixture is built through the production PDF fixture pipeline, rendered by the real file-backed worker, and proves exact expected/actual dimensions, conservative admission, highest-fitting-rung selection, and the unchanged bound. At least one boundary fixture also enters a production composition root under the standing anti-masking rule. If and only if the exact formula is shown unstable, additional REDs must capture each unstable pinned-version path and prove the constrained fallback provenance and >1-pixel rejection. Synthetic hand-assembled rasters or metadata are invalid test evidence.

Only after those REDs fail for the intended old behavior, the implementation turns them green, and the complete declared-environment verification set passes may logical `acceptance-008` be created from zero. It uses PostgreSQL database `idis_acceptance_008`, object-store subdirectory `acceptance-008`, Redis logical database 7, and report/ledger root `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\.local_reports\slice100\acceptance-008`. The owner-only/read-only strict-profile procedure changes exactly the database component in both approved DSNs, the existing object-store base directory, and the existing Redis URL; the CLI report/ledger root is not a dotenv value. All other profile bytes remain unchanged, and the expected profile-HMAC change is reverified without secret output.

Before any `acceptance-008` write, database `idis_acceptance_008`, object namespace `acceptance-008`, and report root `acceptance-008` must be absent, Redis DB 7 must be empty, port 8000 must be closed, the immutable source fingerprint must remain exact, and all seven sealed `acceptance-007` evidence files must match the member set and hashes in §1.17. Migration remains exactly through `0035` via the strict launcher. All 267 rows start from zero; `acceptance-007` is never resumed. The next stop remains either the complete durable proof followed by forced API kill/restart, a different boot identity, zero upload POSTs, authenticated download/hash survival, exact 58/209 reconciliation, and the complete tenant-isolation denial matrix, or the next real blocker with its fresh namespace stopped, reconciled, hashed, and sealed.

### 1.19 Recorded acceptance-008 mixed-page census blocker and approved acceptance-009 repair

`acceptance-008` is sealed failed-attempt evidence, not a completed Task-4 proof. The exact Poppler repair in §1.18 passed its RED and full declared-environment verification, and the fresh run uploaded all 267 immutable source files with HTTP 201: company-001 58 files and 496,782,706 bytes, company-002 209 files and 361,424,748 bytes, for 267 files and 858,207,454 bytes total. The preparation ledger has 272 physical rows, tip sequence 271, 267 upload rows, and 858,207,454 uploaded bytes. PostgreSQL contains 267 artifacts, 182,094 spans, 265 `PARSED` documents, and the two approved watermark-only terminal exclusions. Persistent object storage contains 1,068 files and 858,346,623 bytes. The API emitted no error or traceback marker, but durable collection stopped after 163 document triplets with `DATA_ROOM_RECONCILIATION_INCOMPLETE`; therefore no 267-row success receipt, forced-restart proof, different-boot proof, zero-reupload proof, authenticated restart download, or final isolation proof exists.

The blocker is safe alias `company-002-document-00105.pdf`. It is a valid eight-page mixed document with 263 durable native-text spans across six text-bearing pages and valid reviewer-confirmed `image_only_page` receipts for pages 4 and 7. OCR was performed on those two pages and correctly produced no text. The document-level evaluation is `PARSED` with `may_proceed=true`, but the durable collector incorrectly requires every `ocr_performed=true` page to have an OCR-text locator and raises `A PDF OCR locator census is incomplete`. This is a terminal-evidence census defect, not a source-document, OCR, or data-room defect.

The API was stopped, port 8000 was confirmed closed, and Redis DB 7 was reduced from one ephemeral residue key at failure to zero keys before sealing. The immutable source fingerprint remains `dc29e07e7c605a7ba4bd3b84ad9ab481c32056e544f054a64f95946a460ef64d`. The safe reconciliation evidence binds Task-4 code-closure SHA-256 `2ac29f75aed892d0fc01fe22702306ff5505e568fa8ccee68dc0fc0667c8dd62`, plan SHA-256 `c5a6146c9d872e28979891abf9d7046f4e921e1698197152150b6b72f5ff9dd9`, and the accepted bootstrap profile-HMAC digest `6feb34c336767cc5181476efcdee7db077442bca7c7975afeaa0ff9ff15391e1`.

The sealed report member set and SHA-256 values are exact: `.api.pid.lock` and `.prepare-ledger.jsonl.lock` each `6e340b9cffb37a989ca544e6bb780a2c78901d3fb33738768511a30617afa01d`; `api.pid` `d786bb2bc967b77b2e2698f4e49b711179bb42a1aaf0ebc089976b1f27340678`; `api.stderr.log` `15b7667d52d5c2411035a6916f44ad97cf213cdf383d0c27d69b6532ae8980e5`; `api.stdout.log` `2ba19ace86aa64456c19019a654c9acc00a66e0f55c02b53101bbc2ecafd4c18`; `prepare-ledger.jsonl` `a742a7f61619435e78dd510fcad6484afeed3257a3f55fca7341881730e99265`; and `reconciliation_log.jsonl` `f9cc8fc6937e40dae7add56ced1aaeaf3c3d17f1a2fc28e869ebbd20f2669029`. All seven files and the report directory are read-only. Database `idis_acceptance_008`, object namespace `acceptance-008`, Redis DB 7 history, ledger, logs, and report remain immutable and may not be resumed, selectively retried, deleted, relabeled, or written further.

The approved repair introduces one versioned, durable, page-complete PDF disposition census in terminal reconciliation evidence. Fresh rows emit `ocr-terminal-evidence-v5`, preserving the complete v4 render/admission lineage and embedding exactly one `pdf-page-disposition-census-v1` object. That object is `{schema_version, page_count, page_dispositions, native_text_page_numbers, ocr_text_page_numbers, image_only_page_numbers, watermark_only_page_numbers, class_counts, census_sha256}`. `page_dispositions` is the exact one-based page-ordered sequence of `{page_number, disposition, evidence_binding_sha256}`; the four page-number arrays are strictly increasing and duplicate-free; `class_counts` rederives from those arrays; and `census_sha256` is SHA-256 over canonical JSON of every preceding member in that order, excluding the digest member itself. Every one-based page of every parsed PDF belongs to exactly one of four classes: `native_text`, `ocr_text`, `image_only_page`, or `watermark_only`. The collector must derive the classes and evidence-binding digests from durable spans and their page locators, signed classifier/image-only receipts, OCR render/terminal provenance, and the source-bound page count; it may not trust an aggregate client counter. It validates **completeness** by proving that the four sets' union is exactly every page from 1 through the durable page count, and **disjointness** by proving that every page has cardinality one across those sets. Missing pages, out-of-range pages, duplicate membership within or between classes, a receipt/text contradiction, aggregate-equivalent page swaps, or a source/durable page-count mismatch fails `DATA_ROOM_RECONCILIATION_INCOMPLETE`. The exact ordered per-page census, contract version, canonical digest, class counts, and derivation bindings are persisted in the safe reconciliation evidence and must rederive identically after restart. Legacy `ocr-terminal-evidence-v4` remains honest sealed `acceptance-008` evidence but cannot satisfy a fresh `acceptance-009` proof.

The OCR locator rule is corrected, never relaxed. For a page with `ocr_performed=true`, the durable collector requires either at least one non-empty OCR-text span carrying a valid OCR locator, or one valid `image_only_page` receipt for that same page. The image-only alternative is valid only when its receipt carries the complete, matching render-admission/observed-render and primary-empty/rescue-no-consensus provenance required by the approved image-only contract. Zero OCR-text locators without that exact receipt remain a hard failure. A watermark-only page remains classifier-first, carries no fabricated OCR execution, and cannot overlap any OCR or image-only class. A native-text page must have at least one durable native-text span and cannot be reclassified merely because OCR metadata exists elsewhere in the document.

The document-level invariant is unchanged: a `PARSED` PDF has at least one text-bearing page in `native_text` or `ocr_text`. One or more properly proven image-only pages may coexist with those text-bearing pages, but an all-image-only document remains failed with `IMAGE_ONLY_DOCUMENT_REVIEW_REQUIRED` pending explicit reviewer disposition. Page receipts cannot manufacture a parsed document or substantive evidence, and the two approved whole-document watermark exclusions remain explicitly non-consumable terminal rows rather than parsed documents.

RED evidence is mandatory before production edits. First, operator-backed collection of real `company-002-document-00105.pdf` through the durable collector and the gate must reproduce the current `DATA_ROOM_RECONCILIATION_INCOMPLETE` result, then turn green only with an exact eight-page census of six `native_text` pages and two `image_only_page` pages, including the valid render/no-consensus provenance and persisted contract version/digest. Second, production-pipeline fixtures must independently prove failure when one page belongs to two classes, when one page belongs to none, and when an `image_only_page` receipt lacks render provenance. Additional contracts preserve failure for an all-image-only parsed claim, an OCR-performed zero-text page without a receipt, and any restart rederivation drift. Fixtures follow the standing production-constructor realism rule, and at least the real-artifact path enters the actual durable gate composition root under the standing anti-masking rule.

Only after those REDs fail for the intended missing behavior, the implementation turns them green, and the complete declared-environment verification set passes may logical `acceptance-009` be created from zero. It uses PostgreSQL database `idis_acceptance_009`, object-store subdirectory `acceptance-009`, Redis logical database 8, and report/ledger root `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\.local_reports\slice100\acceptance-009`. The owner-only/read-only strict-profile procedure changes exactly the database component in both approved DSNs, the existing object-store base directory, and the existing Redis URL; the CLI report/ledger root is not a dotenv value. All other profile bytes remain unchanged, and the expected profile-HMAC change is reverified without secret output.

Before any `acceptance-009` write, database `idis_acceptance_009`, object namespace `acceptance-009`, and report root `acceptance-009` must be absent, Redis DB 8 must be empty, port 8000 must be closed, the immutable source fingerprint must remain exact, and all seven sealed `acceptance-008` evidence files must match the exact member set and hashes above. Migration remains exactly through `0035` via the strict launcher. All 267 rows start from zero; `acceptance-008` is never resumed. The acceptance bar is the original complete Task-4 target: 267/267 durable rows and exact 58/209 reconciliation, followed by forced API kill/restart, a different boot identity, zero upload POSTs, authenticated artifact download/hash survival, and the complete tenant-isolation denial matrix. The only permitted earlier stop is the next real blocker with its fresh namespace stopped, reconciled, hashed, and sealed.

The approved repair is narrow and RED-first. A test must enter through the production composition root with real authentication, strict tenant context, real `RateLimitMiddleware`, and the durable content route, obtain HTTP 200 with the six production headers, then pass that response through the actual gate verifier and reproduce the current `DATA_ROOM_RECONCILIATION_INCOMPLETE`. The green contract compares header names case-insensitively and requires the observed `x-idis-*` set to equal exactly the four existing durable stream names plus exactly `X-IDIS-RateLimit-Limit` and `X-IDIS-RateLimit-Remaining`. Each rate-limit value is a canonical non-negative base-10 integer (`0` or a nonzero ASCII digit followed only by ASCII digits); signs, whitespace, leading-zero alternatives, comma-joined duplicate values, and non-decimal forms fail closed. `Remaining` must be less than or equal to `Limit`. To make that relationship truthful under the existing burst-capable token bucket, the middleware reports the effective bucket capacity (`limit_rpm * burst_multiplier`) as `Limit` and the actual post-request token count as `Remaining`; the configured sustained refill rate and gate pacing remain `limit_rpm` and are not changed. A missing required header or any seventh/unknown `x-idis-*` header remains a hard `DATA_ROOM_RECONCILIATION_INCOMPLETE`. The existing stream content type, cache, byte length, hashes, object version, chunk bound, and authenticated ownership checks remain unchanged. No environment variable, flag, table, migration, proxy, or middleware bypass is added.

RED coverage includes the exact production-composition failure, mixed-case header-name acceptance, missing either rate-limit header, malformed/negative/noncanonical integer values, `Remaining > Limit`, duplicate/comma-joined values, and an unknown `x-idis-*` header. Unit-seam cases supplement but do not replace the production-composition test. Only after those REDs fail for the intended old behavior, the implementation turns them green, and the complete declared-environment verification passes may logical `acceptance-010` be created from zero. It uses PostgreSQL database `idis_acceptance_010`, object-store subdirectory `acceptance-010`, Redis logical database 9, and report/ledger root `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\.local_reports\slice100\acceptance-010`. The owner-only/read-only strict-profile procedure changes exactly the database component in both approved DSNs, the existing object-store base directory, and the existing Redis URL; every other profile entry remains byte-identical and the new safe profile-HMAC digest is reverified.

Before any `acceptance-010` write, database `idis_acceptance_010`, object namespace `acceptance-010`, and report root `acceptance-010` must be absent, Redis DB 9 must be empty, port 8000 must be closed, the immutable source fingerprint must remain exact, and all seven sealed `acceptance-009` evidence files must match the exact member set and hashes above. Migration remains exactly through `0035` via the strict launcher. All 267 rows start from zero; `acceptance-009` is never resumed. The terminal Task-4 bar is the complete initial proof plus forced API kill/restart, a different boot identity, zero upload POSTs, authenticated owner downloads with exact byte/hash survival for both companies, and the complete pre- and post-restart isolation matrix. The only permitted earlier stop is the next real blocker with `acceptance-010` stopped, safely reconciled, hashed, and sealed.

The approved repair establishes `task4-terminal-snapshot-v1`. Every multi-row SQL read that feeds terminal collection must carry an explicit `ORDER BY` whose final key is a unique row identifier; ordering by a non-unique timestamp, sequence, or page alone is insufficient. Point reads remain bound to one canonical identifier and must reject multiplicity. Repository/API serialization must preserve neither database plan order nor mapping insertion order as semantic input. Before hashing, the canonicalizer recursively normalizes every mapping key and scalar, sorts every unordered collection by its complete normalized semantic tuple plus a unique digest/identifier tie-breaker, and emits UTF-8 JSON with sorted keys, no insignificant whitespace, Unicode NFC strings, lowercase canonical UUIDs and SHA-256 values, finite canonical base-10 numbers with negative zero normalized to zero, and booleans/null as JSON literals. Every timestamp is converted to UTC and emitted as exactly `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.ffffffZ`; naive, non-finite, locale-dependent, or otherwise noncanonical scalars fail closed. Ordered sequences whose order is itself evidence retain that explicitly validated order and include their unique tie-breaker. No Python set/dict iteration, SQL planner order, locale, process hash seed, or HTTP mapping order may influence a digest.

The Task-4 durable proof receipt advances from `slice100-task4-durable-ingestion-proof-v5` to `slice100-task4-durable-ingestion-proof-v6`. Its exact new `terminal_snapshot_digests` member has `schema_version=task4-terminal-snapshot-digests-v1`, one top-level `terminal_rows_sha256`, and `component_sha256` with exactly five SHA-256 fields: `documents`, `artifacts`, `spans`, `censuses`, and `audit_counts`. Each component is derived independently from the same normalized terminal snapshot, contains hashes/counts only, and excludes private identifiers, text, paths, filenames, object keys/URIs, and timestamps not required for immutable semantics. The top digest binds the exact complete component mapping. The initial proof persists the mapping before post-isolation comparison; a mismatch fsyncs both safe component mappings and the exact differing component-name set, then raises `DATA_ROOM_TERMINAL_SNAPSHOT_DRIFT`, so the section is diagnosable without private data. Restart frozen-digest comparison includes the complete `terminal_snapshot_digests` mapping, not only its top digest; any missing, extra, malformed, unbound, or changed component fails closed.

RED begins before implementation. First, an operator-backed test performs at least three back-to-back terminal collections through the production composition root, authenticated API, and real disposable PostgreSQL with no intervening write and requires exact equality of the top and all five component digests; it must reproduce the current instability before the repair. Second, `tests/slice100_task4/test_strict_durable_api_gate.py` removes the line-1412 replacement of `_canonical_reduced_terminal_rows_sha256` with a constant and exercises the real reducer/digest path. Third, a production-shaped fixture injects different SQL/API/list/mapping iteration orders over the same rows and proves the canonical snapshot and all component digests remain byte-for-byte identical. The complete Task-4 test tree is swept for monkeypatched, stubbed, or constant-return verification hashes/digests: no test may replace a canonicalizer, collection digest, file/code/plan/profile/boot hash, or frozen comparison value with a fixed literal. Test-only fixtures may control immutable input bytes or identities at their external boundary, but the production hash and comparison code must execute. The sweep must be zero before green is claimed.

### 1.24 Approved shared receipt-whitelist amendment and acceptance-013

The manifest-v2 safe-summary contract must have one production definition consumed by both the final durable receipt validator and the dependency readiness validator. That single immutable contract object owns the exact base-v2 field set, the exact paired reviewed-image field set, and every common validation rule. `real_example_gate.py` and `dependency_probes.py` import and invoke that same object; neither retains a private field-set copy or parallel reviewed-image validation branch. A RED test asserts object identity across the shared definition and both consumers so future drift cannot hide behind equivalent-but-separate literals.

The shared contract admits exactly two shapes: the base v2 safe summary, or the base fields plus both `reviewed_image_only_page_count` and `reviewed_image_only_set_sha256`. When present, the pair is atomic; the count is a positive integer and not a boolean, and the digest is exactly one lowercase SHA-256. A partial pair, zero, negative, boolean, float, string count, malformed digest, unexpected field, wrong fixed identity/count/status value, or malformed existing hash remains a hard rejection. Each consumer preserves its own established outer fail-closed code while delegating the common shape and semantic decision to the shared object.

RED must first load the actual operator-configured signed production manifest through the production strict-profile and governance loaders, place its real `safe_summary()` into an otherwise valid v6 durable proof, and enter through `_write_safe_durable_ingestion_receipt`; the pre-fix result must be the reproduced `DATA_ROOM_RECONCILIATION_INCOMPLETE`. The test may persist only the final safe receipt under its temporary test root and must not expose the manifest path, signature, bitmap, tenant secret, source locator, or private content. Additional RED cases cover shared-object identity, both accepted shapes, and every paired-field rejection branch. Synthetic manifest material used outside the operator-backed case must be built through the production constructor/signing pipeline rather than hand-assembling an impossible artifact.

The approved amendment was implemented RED-first. Before production changes, the shared-contract suite failed all 15 cases because no common singleton existed, and the operator-backed test loaded the actual signed production manifest and failed at the final receipt writer with exactly `DATA_ROOM_RECONCILIATION_INCOMPLETE`. After the repair, those 15 cases pass and the actual signed-manifest writer case passes. Declared-environment verification is green: the focused receipt/readiness/governance set is 337 passed and 2 skipped; the complete Task-4 set is 1,168 passed and 15 skipped; and the full repository is 7,345 passed and 322 skipped with zero failures. Ruff reports all 1,091 files formatted and a clean check, no-cache mypy reports all 481 source files clean, all 19 forbidden-pattern scans are clean, and `git diff --check` is clean apart from line-ending notices. Independent review also confirmed identical singleton identity under both import orders, no import cycle, no remaining private v2 whitelist in either consumer, and a passing operator-backed production-writer case. These results authorize the fresh namespace only; they do not make an acceptance claim by themselves.

Only after the focused tests and complete declared-environment verification are green may fresh `acceptance-013` begin. Its exact namespaces are PostgreSQL database `idis_acceptance_013`, persistent-object namespace/subdirectory `acceptance-013`, Redis logical database 12, and report/ledger root `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\.local_reports\slice100\acceptance-013`. Before rotation, all nine sealed `acceptance-012` members and hashes in §1.23 must reverify exactly, port 8000 must be closed, the source fingerprint must remain fixed, and the new database, object namespace, report root, and Redis DB 12 must be absent/empty. Migration remains exactly through `0035` via the strict launcher.

### 1.26 Approved native-process wrapper amendment and acceptance-014

The run wrapper has exactly one success signal: the launched native process exit code. It captures the child's complete stdout and stderr into separate caller-supplied report files, but it never promotes, parses, classifies, filters, or otherwise interprets stderr content as process failure. A child that writes a warning to stderr and exits zero succeeds with that warning durably captured; a child that writes nothing to stderr and exits nonzero fails from that exit code. Output paths must be distinct, their parent directory must already exist, and existing output files are rejected so evidence cannot be overwritten. The wrapper emits no child output to the invoking PowerShell streams and exposes only safe process metadata.

One checked-in PowerShell wrapper is the shared contract for every documented native strict-launcher process. Its approved parameters are `FilePath`, `ArgumentList`, `WorkingDirectory`, `StandardOutputPath`, `StandardErrorPath`, and `Background`. `FilePath` is the repository's declared interpreter `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\.venv\Scripts\python.exe`, never bare `python` or an ambient `Get-Command` resolution. Foreground mode waits, closes both capture streams, returns safe process metadata on exit zero, and throws a stable exit-derived failure on any nonzero exit. Background mode is permitted only for the long-running API and worker; it returns the spawned PID without claiming service success, and the existing PID-file, liveness, port, strict-readiness, boot-identity, and shutdown checks remain mandatory. Migration, governance, and every gate invocation use foreground mode. The section-16 sweep must remove direct PowerShell-native exposure for all API, worker, gate, governance, and migration launches in one pass; Python `subprocess` calls internal to parsers/tests are outside this Windows PowerShell error-conversion defect and are not changed.

RED-first regression covers the production wrapper itself: an actual child writes a warning to stderr and exits zero, with exact stdout/stderr capture and success; a second child has clean stderr and exits nonzero, with complete stdout capture and a hard exit-derived failure. A background child additionally proves that service stderr is isolated from the caller while the spawned PID remains the only start result. A static runbook contract rejects any direct `python -m idis.runtime.strict_launcher` launch in section 16 after the sweep. No new environment variable, feature flag, table, column, index, migration, provider, parser bound, or Task-7 schema is introduced.

Only after focused RED/green proof and the complete declared-environment verification may fresh `acceptance-014` begin. Its exact namespaces are PostgreSQL database `idis_acceptance_014`, persistent-object namespace/subdirectory `acceptance-014`, Redis logical database 13, and report/ledger root `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\.local_reports\slice100\acceptance-014`. The protected-profile rotation changes only the database component of `IDIS_DATABASE_URL` and `IDIS_DATABASE_ADMIN_URL`, the terminal `IDIS_OBJECT_STORE_BASE_DIR` namespace, and the logical database in `IDIS_REDIS_URL`; the complete key set and every other value remain byte-identical, then read-only and both owner-only ACL checks are re-proved. Before rotation, the exact seven-member sealed `acceptance-013` evidence set and hashes in §1.25, its dead API PID, closed port 8000, immutable source fingerprint/totals, and the fresh database/object/report/Redis-13 state must all reverify. Migration remains exactly through `0035` via the strict launcher.

The full terminal bar remains binding: the initial phase must persist the complete v6 proof after all 267 uploads, both durable collections, both authenticated downloads, stable component digests, and all 18 isolation denials; the API is then force-killed and proved dead; the identical HMAC-bound profile launches a distinct boot; restart verification performs zero upload POSTs, proves both downloads survive, reproduces the frozen receipt and all 267 terminal rows, and completes the post-restart isolation matrix before persisting the restart-verified receipt. The only permitted earlier stop is the next production-derived blocker with the API stopped and `acceptance-014` reconciled and sealed. `acceptance-013` is never resumed or modified.

The wrapper amendment's declared-environment verification is complete. The initial RED checkpoint produced four expected failures because the shared wrapper did not yet exist and section 16 still contained direct native invocations. Independent review then found that background spawn metadata alone left an API-startup race; the follow-up RED checkpoint produced two expected failures for the absent bounded PID/port/authenticated-readiness admission and the incomplete direct-launch inventory. After implementation and the complete runbook sweep, all seven wrapper/runbook contracts pass, including warning-on-stderr/exit-zero success, clean-stderr/nonzero-exit failure, background isolation, overwrite rejection, path separation, exhaustive strict-launcher wrapping, and both API-boot admissions. The combined wrapper/durable-gate/fresh-migration/strict-launcher set is `103 passed, 2 skipped`; the complete Task-4 set is `1,175 passed, 15 skipped`; and the complete repository is `7,352 passed, 322 skipped` with zero failures from 7,674 collected tests. The exact 11-member report set has canonical member-set SHA-256 `2ba311f4fa3efca8a8972387e9be039434890f6f81fb6859cae96a8c13738d06`: `.api.pid.lock` `6e340b9cffb37a989ca544e6bb780a2c78901d3fb33738768511a30617afa01d`; `api.initial.stderr.log` `ae25b68ebc7e476788a0a8edcc96ba3b2a15601036145e4127b0519dcaf9cc61`; `api.pid` `4e3b40474e2048eff584dcbb2d4a729f1e6e07f48ff664986e6c9fa9aa9f85c4`; `preflight.stdout.log` `83bf38d0ff0f5d24907e8d09c5c82bc8ac20c620fe9fe8a16394a416c7816126`; `reconciliation.stdout.log` `7d6d7a56408e151caa2a7a86122485e844dfe058e64dfd875355d2626513e752`; and `reconciliation_log.jsonl` as above. `api.initial.stdout.log`, both migration logs, `preflight.stderr.log`, and `reconciliation.stderr.log` each have the empty-file SHA-256 `e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855`. The directory and every member are read-only; database/object/Redis/report evidence is sealed against resume, deletion, relabelling, or further writes.

The approved `strict-process-identity-v1` contract distinguishes two safe integers: `launcher_process_id`, returned by `Start-Process`, and `service_process_id`, written atomically by the strict launcher to its existing `--pid-file`. Background mode of the native wrapper returns only schema-v2 spawn metadata containing `LauncherProcessId`; the checked-in, code-hash-bound `scripts/strict_process_identity.psm1` composition helper reads the already structured pid-file path supplied by the caller and returns the validated relationship plus both PIDs. This adds no wrapper parameter, product flag, or environment variable. Within the existing fixed 60-second startup bound, the pid-file service PID must be live, its bounded acyclic parent chain must contain the launcher PID, and its executable must resolve to the expected interpreter. The pid-file publication time must be no earlier than the launcher's creation time, and its PID plus file metadata must remain identical across the relationship validation and a second read. Every lineage node is snapshotted with PID, parent PID, and creation time; every parent must predate its child, and the complete chain must requery unchanged before admission so intermediate PID reuse cannot validate a false relationship. For the declared Windows virtualenv, expected resolution is the unique absolute `executable` entry in the co-located `pyvenv.cfg`; otherwise it is the resolved `FilePath` itself. Missing/duplicate/malformed virtualenv metadata, a missing/invalid/stale pid file, a dead service, an unrelated process, an executable mismatch, an ancestry cycle/depth overflow, or process-inspection failure is terminal. Exact stable failures include `IDIS_NATIVE_PROCESS_SERVICE_PID_FILE_INVALID`, `IDIS_NATIVE_PROCESS_SERVICE_PID_FILE_TIMEOUT`, `IDIS_NATIVE_PROCESS_SERVICE_PID_UNRELATED`, `IDIS_NATIVE_PROCESS_SERVICE_NOT_LIVE`, `IDIS_NATIVE_PROCESS_SERVICE_EXECUTABLE_MISMATCH`, and `IDIS_NATIVE_PROCESS_EXPECTED_EXECUTABLE_INVALID`.

The composition helper returns safe identity schema v1 containing both PIDs, `PidRelationship=launcher_ancestor`, `PidRelationshipValidated=true`, `ServiceExecutableValidated=true`, and the expected launcher's and service interpreter's content SHA-256 values; neither it nor the wrapper emits an executable path. Each initial/restart API or worker launch persists that exact identity object in a fresh process-receipt JSON file before authenticated readiness. HTTP `/health` plus `/v1/strict-readiness` and constant-time profile-HMAC equality remain mandatory for the API; PID lineage only corroborates that proof. RED uses a real shim-spawned child process through the checked-in wrapper, accepts launcher-to-descendant ancestry, rejects an unrelated live PID through the same relationship validator, and proves service-PID kill/restart behavior. The shared relationship implementation is `scripts/strict_process_identity.psm1`, imported by the runbook composition root and added to the Task-4 code closure.

Restart semantics are service-PID-specific. Immediately before each forced shutdown, the current pid-file is reread through the same freshness, lineage, creation-time, executable, and stability validator and must reproduce the persisted identity; forced shutdown then targets that service PID and proves both recorded launcher and service PIDs dead and port 8000 released before restart. The second launch must return a service PID different from the first, persist a distinct process receipt, pass the same lineage/executable/authenticated-readiness checks, and still satisfy the existing distinct API boot-UUID, zero-reupload, download-survival, and post-restart isolation contracts. The final shutdown again proves both processes dead and the port released before evidence sealing. PID inequality never replaces the stronger API boot-UUID proof.

The dual-PID amendment is verified RED-first in the declared repository environment. The initial checkpoint had five expected failures for the absent shared relationship module, old PID-equality Task-4 runbook, and missing code-closure member while the six pre-existing wrapper contracts stayed green. Adversarial review then added two expected behavioral failures for a stale live pid-file predating the new launcher and an intermediate ancestor PID with impossible parent/child creation ordering. Green now proves the real shim-to-descendant relationship, unrelated and executable-mismatch rejection, stale-file replacement, complete lineage snapshot/requery, exact 11-field safe receipt, service-PID restart, and all initial/final Task-4 plus future API/worker shutdown contracts. The final wrapper/identity/code-closure set is 68 passed; all 19 PowerShell plan blocks and both implementation files parse under Windows PowerShell 5.1. The complete Task-4 suite is 1,182 passed and 15 operator-backed skips; the complete repository is 7,359 passed and 322 skips with zero failures from 7,681 collected tests. Ruff reports all 1,093 files formatted and a clean check, no-cache mypy reports all 481 source files clean, all 19 forbidden-pattern scans are clean, import identity resolves to this worktree, and `git diff --check` is clean. The strict profile and every `acceptance-015` namespace remained untouched throughout verification; no Task-7 schema, live provider call, commit, push, PR, or merge occurred.

Fresh `acceptance-015` uses PostgreSQL database `idis_acceptance_015`, persistent-object namespace `acceptance-015`, Redis logical DB 14, and report/ledger root `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\.local_reports\slice100\acceptance-015`. Before the exact four-value `acceptance-014 -> acceptance-015` protected-profile rotation, all 11 sealed prior members/hashes, dead processes, closed port, source aggregates, profile ACL/read-only state, absent database/object/report, and empty Redis DB 14 must reverify. Migration remains exactly through `0035`. The next stop remains the complete 267-file initial proof plus distinct-service-PID restart, zero reupload, both download survivals, and both 18-denial isolation matrices—or the next production-derived blocker, safely reconciled and sealed. The standing acceptance-hardening authorization permits further RED-first harness/wrapper/launcher/verification-shape repairs and fresh namespaces without per-blocker approval, but none of the product-data, schema, auth/RLS/crypto, private-content, bound-relaxation, Task-7, or priced-provider stop triggers is waived.

### 1.29 Task 4 acceptance, database housekeeping, and approved Task 5 schema boundary

The reviewer accepted Task 4 after independently verifying the proof, database end-state, attestation chain, sealed evidence, and consolidated history. The accepted implementation is preserved in six local-only commits: `4a9e8f9`, `857f771`, `e375eae`, `f0d3bec`, `b76a8c8`, and `b9280ed`. No push or PR occurred. The acceptance-hardening autonomy in section 1.27 is expired.

The reviewer separately authorized deletion of the superseded PostgreSQL databases in the literal range `idis_acceptance_002` through `idis_acceptance_014`, after their existence and sealed report-set hashes were recorded. That inclusive range contains 13 databases, not 14, and the authorization is not expanded to `idis_dev`, `idis_acceptance_001`, `idis_acceptance_015`, or any other database. Before deletion, all 13 existed with zero active connections and every report set reproduced under the name-sorted `name + NUL + decimal_size + NUL + lowercase_sha256 + LF` contract. The authoritative safe root reconciliation log records 13 `DATABASE_CLEANUP_ADMITTED` entries before deletion and 13 `DATABASE_CLEANUP_COMPLETE` entries after it; its post-operation SHA-256 is `cf62f8e3b60331fb0089b14f1de5072a2d36f7958287a26397975a67afeff6d5`. Post-operation verification found all 13 target databases absent, `idis_acceptance_015` present, and all 13 filesystem report sets byte/hash unchanged. The sealed per-attempt reconciliation files were not modified.

Task 5 owns one linear migration, `0036`, after `0035`, and exactly four new tenant-bearing tables: `methodology_versions`, `methodology_questions`, `run_methodology_selections`, and `methodology_coverage_records`. This is the concrete Task-5 subset of the methodology state named in sections 6 and 13. Task/extraction attempts/results, canonical-output links, semantic receipts/edges, and every Task-7 table remain deferred to their separately authorized tasks. Runtime tenant-bearing inventory becomes exactly 46 tables; migration `0034` remains the immutable historical 42-table normalization and must not be edited.

`methodology_versions` stores the tenant-scoped methodology identity/type/version label, honest source kind, actual source-artifact SHA-256, canonical registry SHA-256, exact question count, `IMPORTED|FROZEN` state, actor/audit provenance, and import/freeze timestamps. Only `IMPORTED -> FROZEN` is allowed; a frozen row and all of its questions are immutable and undeletable. `methodology_questions` stores the stable question ID and ordinal, version/type/section, exact source sheet-or-section and Excel row where applicable, canonical question payload plus payload SHA-256, and the version's source-artifact binding. It enforces unique version/question, version/ordinal, and FDD version/sheet/row identities. An FDD question always carries its actual sheet and physical Excel row.

`run_methodology_selections` is one immutable tenant/deal/run row with two mandatory foreign keys: one frozen 21-question CDD version and one frozen 79-question FDD version.
