### 1.31 Approved Task 6 durable extraction and canonical-materialization amendment

Task 5 was independently approved and preserved in the five local commits recorded at the top of this document. Task 6 is authorized RED-first under the per-task cadence. Its implementation constructs the real strict Anthropic methodology extractor, while Task-6 verification injects a hermetic transport below that extractor: the production request serializer, response parser, validation, provenance, and budget-vector construction all execute, but no provider credential is read, no network request or priced call occurs, and the attempt is recorded honestly as hermetic/non-live. A hermetic attempt may exercise repository behavior in tests but can never satisfy strict semantic completion or Gate A.

Task 6 authors `METHODOLOGY_QUESTION_EXTRACTION_V1@1.0.0` as a governed DRAFT artifact: prompt text, metadata, input JSON schema, output JSON schema, and the checked-in prompt inventory entry. It creates no staging or production pointer, evaluation pass, approval, or promotion record. The production composition root resolves only the PROD pointer and therefore remains fail-closed until Task 12 evaluates, approves, and promotes this exact content-addressed artifact. Task 12 promotes rather than creates the runtime prompt.

The Task-6 extractor integrity review adds the following binding correction without expanding schema, environment, or Task-7 scope. Prompt and admitted source text are preserved byte-for-byte; the exact frozen question string is likewise preserved from the durable question row through the worker request and provider wire view, including leading/trailing whitespace, line endings, and Unicode. Nonblank validation observes these raw strings but never normalizes them. The prompt artifact validates its own raw UTF-8 content hash and canonical input/output-schema hashes before use. The internal typed request retains all tenant/run/task/version/question identities, while a minimized provider wire view contains only the ten governed evidence-extraction fields in the input schema; runtime validates that exact view against the checked-in input schema, and `prepared_call_sha256` additionally binds the complete internal request. One immutable Anthropic wire-request object owns model, exact prompt, messages, strict output tool, forced single-tool choice, controls, and schemas. The identical object is canonical-serialized without text normalization for the reservation byte/token ceiling and request hash, then passed unchanged to the zero-retry SDK transport. `tools[0].strict=true`, one-tool-only choice, and configured JSON-mode proof are therefore factual rather than inferred.

Preparation requires the exact verified `budget_policy_id`; the deterministic reservation identity includes tenant/run/task/input **and** policy ID, and that same ID is immutable across prepared call, committed admission, durable attempt, terminal proof, and final receipt. A policy-A admission cannot finalize under policy-B. The terminal boundary reloads the exact catalog row, recomputes observed USD from returned token counts, and rejects catalog, price, reservation, or consumption drift. Provider latency is measured monotonically around the sole SDK call, stored as a non-negative millisecond value, and bound into the dispatched-call hash.

The provider may not choose a partial search proof. `methodology-searched-scope-v1` hashes the complete deterministic admitted document/span/ordinal/content/locator identity without raw text; returned searched document/span sets and hash must equal that complete scope or fail `METHODOLOGY_SEARCH_SCOPE_MISMATCH`. Accepted citations are canonicalized and hashed as paired `(span_id, locator_sha256)` tuples, and every pair must equal the admitted mapping. Returned value domains must match the frozen question's declared answer type (`numeric`, `boolean`, `table`, or `narrative`); `period=none` is an explicit valid period alongside `as_of` and `range`. Table duplicate detection uses question-record ID plus the canonical `methodology-table-contract-v1` row core, so changing prose or citations cannot disguise an identical row. Boolean inputs remain strict JSON booleans only.

The transport accepts exactly one `methodology_result` tool-use block, requires the pinned terminal `tool_use` stop reason, validates the raw payload against the governed output schema, then performs typed/domain/source validation. Missing or multiple matching blocks, extra ambiguous content blocks, truncation/max-token stops, schema violations, partial scope, or answer-domain mismatch fail closed before terminal persistence. The production composition-root tests cover CDD and FDD wire views, raw prompt/source preservation, policy swapping, exact wire/reservation equality, response ambiguity/truncation, answer domains, and restart proof. These corrections are Task-6 integrity work only and create no live call or prompt promotion.

The locally installed execution protocols are binding for Task 6 and later work: `using-superpowers` for capability routing; `brainstorming` satisfied by this already-approved Slice100 design plus the task amendment; `receiving-code-review` when applying reviewer rulings; `writing-plans` before implementation; `test-driven-development` with watched RED before production changes; `subagent-driven-development` with specification review followed by code-quality review; `supabase` and `supabase-postgres-best-practices` for migration, transaction, indexing, privilege, and RLS work; `using-git-worktrees` for isolation verification; `requesting-code-review` before checkpoint; and `verification-before-completion` before any passing or completion claim. A Task-6 checkpoint lists the protocols actually used. The user's local-commit prohibition before checkpoint approval overrides any generic skill instruction to commit after a subtask.

#### Migration `0037` and exact schema boundary

Task 6 owns one linear migration, `0037`, directly after `0036`, and exactly nine new tenant-bearing relations:

1. `methodology_extraction_tasks`
2. `methodology_extraction_task_spans`
3. `methodology_extraction_attempts`
4. `methodology_extraction_results`
5. `methodology_result_factual_outputs`
6. `methodology_result_claim_links`
7. `methodology_result_evidence_links`
8. `methodology_result_sanad_links`
9. `methodology_result_defect_links`

The runtime tenant-bearing RLS inventory therefore becomes exactly 55. Migration `0037` adds no output root, source-lineage root, run-input binding, run-step-output, expected edge, consumer receipt, provider/enrichment fact table, Task-7 relation, environment variable, or feature flag. The ninth relation is normalized Task-6 canonical-defect lineage, not a Task-7 receipt or edge. Supporting constraints, columns, indexes, and guards named below are part of this amendment; no other schema surface is authorized.

`methodology_extraction_tasks` stores an internal UUID primary key, exact tenant/deal/run/selection/coverage/version/question scope, deterministic external `extraction_task_id`, document/classification identity and classification hash, planner/contract version, immutable canonical task payload and SHA-256, candidate-span-set SHA-256, and creation time. It accepts executable `READY` plans only: blocked, skipped, partial, deferred, evidence-missing, synthetic, or empty task rows are not persistable. It has composite foreign keys to the exact run selection, coverage row, selected methodology question, and tenant/deal document plus unique `(tenant_id, run_id, extraction_task_id)` and one immutable task-input identity. Planning loads the exact selected frozen 21+79 pair from PostgreSQL; the checked-in CDD-only loader is forbidden in strict composition. All 100 Task-5 `NOT_STARTED` rows are applicable in Task 6 and each must have at least one persisted ready task. A `NOT_APPLICABLE` row is not accepted without a separately governed durable applicability proof; Task 6 creates none.

`methodology_extraction_task_spans` stores the scoped task UUID, candidate document/span UUIDs, positive deterministic ordinal, content SHA-256, canonical locator SHA-256, and created time. Composite foreign keys bind it to the scoped task and exact tenant/deal/document/span. Unique task/span and task/ordinal constraints make the set disjoint and ordered. The repository rechecks the live span's document, content hash, and canonical locator hash immediately before provider admission, again in the fenced terminal-result transaction, and again before EvidenceItem materialization. A missing, cross-deal, or drifted locator fails `METHODOLOGY_SOURCE_LOCATOR_REJECTED`; no result or canonical object may bind to content other than the admitted bytes. Migration `0037` may add only the supporting scoped unique constraints needed by these foreign keys to `runs`, `documents`, `document_spans`, Task-5 methodology tables, and the canonical claim/evidence/Sanad/defect tables.

`methodology_extraction_attempts` stores a scoped UUID attempt, exact task/input identity and positive ordinal, lifecycle `STARTED|SUCCEEDED|FAILED|OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`, provider/backend and `LIVE|HERMETIC_TEST` transport kind, configured and observed model, prompt ID/version/content hash plus input/output schema hashes, complete request-payload, prepared-call, and billable-payload SHA-256 values, sanitized provider request ID, distinct raw provider-response and composite dispatched-call SHA-256 values, derived real-call boolean, start/completion time and latency, configured max tokens/temperature/JSON mode, observed input/output usage and stop reason, signed budget policy/component/reservation vector and terminal budget state, immutable prospective/final receipt SHA-256, and safe failure code. Prepared-call, provider-response, and dispatched-call hashes are separate domains and are never compared for equality; the terminal proof binds all applicable identities. The extractor derives transport kind and real-call truth; a test transport cannot self-assert `real_call=true`. It stores no raw prompt, response envelope, exception, credential, source text, or secret. Unique `(tenant_id, run_id, task_record_id, task_input_sha256)` admits at most one provider call for an approved input. Identity/provenance is immutable and lifecycle transitions are monotonic.

`methodology_extraction_results` stores one scoped immutable terminal result envelope per admitted task/input. Its only outcomes are `EVIDENCE_FOUND` and `EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND`; both require a non-empty validated structured envelope, result SHA-256, searched document/span scope and hash, and provider-provenance hash. Strict completion additionally requires every result to bind to a successful live attempt; hermetic results are test evidence only. `EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND` requires positive searched scope and a non-empty governed gap finding and has zero factual-output children. Empty, generic completed, partial, failed, skipped, blocked, deferred, mixed accepted/rejected, or locator-outside-candidate results are rejected.

`methodology_result_factual_outputs` is the normalized immutable parent for every accepted fact; factual outputs are not hidden solely as JSON members of a result. It carries tenant/deal/run/result/task scope, positive output ordinal, deterministic UUIDv5 `factual_output_id`, the validated canonical typed-output payload and SHA-256, accepted locator/span set and set hash, and creation time. The ID is computed over the complete canonical typed output under a fixed Task-6 namespace; link uniqueness is scoped by result, while equivalent content remains stable across retries and rehydration. Unique `(tenant_id, deal_id, run_id, result_id, factual_output_id)` and `(tenant_id, deal_id, run_id, result_id, output_ordinal)` reject duplicates. `EVIDENCE_FOUND` requires one or more exact factual-output children; `EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND` requires zero. Every canonical link uses a composite foreign key to this relation, never an ID embedded only inside JSON.

The four typed link tables avoid polymorphic object-kind/object-ID pseudo-foreign-keys. `methodology_result_claim_links` binds one normalized factual-output row and coverage row to exactly one canonical claim and records its canonical-key hash and whether an existing claim was reused. `methodology_result_evidence_links` binds that factual-output and claim link to the exact EvidenceItems and admitted source spans; its locator set must equal the factual output's accepted-locator set exactly. `methodology_result_sanad_links` binds the factual output and claim link to exactly one canonical Sanad and its primary EvidenceItem. `methodology_result_defect_links` binds each governed canonical defect to that exact Sanad link with a scoped composite foreign key; zero defects is valid, but any emitted governed defect must have exactly one link and no unlisted link is allowed. Each relation carries tenant/deal/run/result/factual-output scope, deterministic immutable link identity, real composite foreign keys, uniqueness preventing duplicate mappings, and creation time. Database semantic guards also prove the joined canonical identity, not merely same-deal ID existence: every linked EvidenceItem's `claim_id` equals the exact claim link and its `source_span_id` equals the exact evidence link span; every linked Sanad's `claim_id` and `primary_evidence_id` equal that claim link and an evidence link for the same factual output; and every linked defect's non-null `claim_id` equals the claim reached through that Sanad link. A different same-deal claim, span, primary evidence, Sanad, or defect therefore fails at the write boundary. These guards add no new table or column and are revalidated by the exact snapshot collector before completion and restart.

Step 8, Step 9, and Step 10 insert their respective canonical rows and links at fenced completion boundaries; restart loaders reconstruct from these rows, never from `RunStep.result_summary` or process memory. For every `EVIDENCE_FOUND` result, set equality is mandatory: factual-output IDs equal claim-link factual-output IDs, equal Sanad-link factual-output IDs, equal the distinct factual-output IDs represented by EvidenceItem links; per output, accepted locator IDs equal EvidenceItem-link locator IDs, and governed defect IDs equal defect-link defect IDs. A failed run may retain a wholly committed earlier step, but no step may expose a partial row set, and Step 10 cannot complete while any accepted output is absent, duplicated, rejected, or mapped to the wrong scoped object.

All nine relations use `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, and exactly one `FOR ALL TO PUBLIC` policy with the canonical null-guarded tenant predicate from section 1.2 in both `USING` and `WITH CHECK`. The app role is re-proved `NOSUPERUSER` and `NOBYPASSRLS`; same-tenant CRUD and unset/cross-tenant read plus insert/update/delete denial are tested for every table. The internal transaction-local current-owner/current-token context described below is mandatory for every Task-6 write, not only attempt recovery. Step-6 task/span persistence therefore accepts the explicit lease owner/token, proves the active run fence, and sets that context before insertion. The immutable/semantic guards for task, span, attempt, result, factual-output, and canonical-link writes validate the same context against the exact active `runs` row; an unfenced, expired, stale-owner, stale-token, or cross-run planning/materialization write fails with `WORKER_LEASE_FENCED`. Test fixtures establish legitimate context explicitly and cannot justify a production guard bypass. All nine are `erased_with_deal`; deletion order is defect links, Sanad links, EvidenceItem links, claim links, factual outputs, results, attempts, task spans, tasks, then Task-5 coverage/selection and the existing canonical child-first order. Immutable guards permit only the established requested-erasure transaction to delete. Empty `0036 -> 0037 -> 0036 -> 0037` is mandatory on disposable PostgreSQL; downgrade refuses while any Task-6 row or any populated Task-6 canonical extension would be lost.

#### Canonical claim and EvidenceItem repairs

Task 6 adds nullable legacy-safe fields to `claims`: `normalization_rule_version`, `normalized_predicate`, `normalized_subject`, `normalized_period`, `normalized_unit`, and lowercase `value_sha256`. A partial unique index over rows with all six fields present enforces the planned key `(tenant_id, deal_id, normalized_predicate, normalized_subject, normalized_period, normalized_unit, value_sha256)`. It adds scoped uniqueness for claims, EvidenceItems, Sanads, and defects and scope-safe claim foreign keys from EvidenceItems, Sanads, and defects; migration aborts on an existing mismatch and never silently repairs data. It validates every existing non-null `sanads.primary_evidence_id` as UUID and as an existing same-tenant/same-deal EvidenceItem, then changes the column to UUID and adds a composite `(tenant_id, deal_id, primary_evidence_id)` foreign key. Any malformed, missing, or cross-scope legacy reference aborts migration rather than being repaired. It adds the exact canonical model fields currently discarded by `evidence_items`: `source_system`, `upstream_origin_id`, `retrieval_timestamp`, `verification_status`, `source_rank_subgrade`, `rationale`, and `updated_at`, nullable for legacy rows but mandatory where the model requires them for Task-6-linked evidence.

`claim-normalization-v1` is exact and deterministic. Text identity components are Unicode NFKC normalized, stripped, internal whitespace collapsed to one ASCII space, and Unicode case-folded. The provider result supplies a non-empty subject, predicate, typed value, and an explicit period object. Period canonicalization is exactly `none`, `as_of:YYYY-MM-DD`, or `range:YYYY-MM-DD/YYYY-MM-DD`; a reversed/incomplete/unknown period fails. Unit canonicalization is uppercase ISO-4217 for monetary values, `ratio` for percentages, `date` for dates, normalized explicit unit for counts/ranges, `boolean` for booleans, `table_row` for decomposed table rows, and `text` for text values. Boolean values extend `ValueStruct` with an exact JSON boolean; strings and integers are rejected rather than coerced.

Table answers extend `ValueStruct` with `TableRowValue` and are decomposed before canonical insertion under code-owned `methodology-table-contract-v1`. The contract is derivable without inventing metadata in a frozen question: every table response uses the exact ordered columns `row_label`, `metric`, `value`, `unit`, and `period`. `row_label` and `metric` are non-empty normalized text, `value` is a validated scalar ValueStruct, `unit` is a non-empty normalized unit, and `period` uses the period contract above. The prompt output schema and response validator pin this exact shape. Source order governs deterministic decomposition, but row identity is the question-record UUID plus canonical compact sorted-key row JSON, so key ordering cannot change identity. Two identical rows are rejected as ambiguous duplicates rather than distinguished by an unstable occurrence counter. An undeclared/missing column also fails. Each row becomes one factual output, never one lossy whole-table text claim. `value_sha256` is SHA-256 over compact sorted-key JSON of the validated typed value after Decimal values are rendered in non-exponent canonical form and textual values use the same NFKC/whitespace rule. Every component is non-empty after normalization.

The canonical claim UUID is deterministic UUIDv5 over the complete tenant/deal canonical key. Creation uses one conflict-safe upsert and then reloads the winner. A collision is reusable only when the normalization version, full canonical key, claim class, and typed value match exactly; it never overwrites existing text, value, grade, verdict, or provenance. The same predicate/subject/period/unit with a different value hash is a distinct claim for later conflict analysis. Equivalent formatting converges to one UUID claim and receives additional EvidenceItems. Methodology and generic claim IDs are sorted, de-duplicated, and unioned before `EXTRACT` returns; downstream grade, calculation, graph, RAG, Court/VEP, debate, analysis, scoring, Layer 2, and deliverable seams receive that one canonical set.

#### Provider admission, budget, and crash semantics

Task 6 introduces one non-optional `MethodologyTaskExtractor` protocol and a narrower `AnthropicMethodologyTransport` below the concrete production extractor. Its request contains the selected version/question hashes, a discriminated immutable source trace, exact question, expected answer schema, required evidence, admitted candidate spans/locators/hashes, predicates/validation rules, and calculation requirements. A CDD template trace requires source hash plus template section and forbids a fabricated row/anchor; an FDD workbook trace requires source hash, sheet, positive physical row, and anchor cell. Its typed response carries the validated outcome plus immutable provider, prompt, usage, request/response, and budget proof. Strict composition rejects `extractor=None`, the in-memory methodology executor/repositories/materializers, deterministic answer clients, `external_calls_enabled=False`, missing PROD prompt resolution, a hermetic transport, or any fallback. Tests construct the real extractor with a hermetic typed transport, so the production Anthropic serializer/parser runs while the SDK/network is provably never invoked.

The extractor API is explicitly three-phase. `prepare(request)` is pure and performs zero transport I/O; it returns an immutable content-addressed `PreparedMethodologyCall` containing the complete serialized billable request and conservative reservation vector. The worker commits that exact call's fenced `STARTED` attempt and signed `RESERVED` budget admission, then supplies the matching durable admission proof to `dispatch(prepared, admission_proof)`. Dispatch rejects an absent, mismatched, non-reserved, or tampered proof before transport and makes exactly one request only after proof validation. It returns a typed content-addressed `DispatchedMethodologyCall` with provider response/usage while the reservation is still honestly `RESERVED`; it cannot emit a final `CONSUMED` receipt before the terminal transaction.

The fenced terminal micro-boundary validates the dispatched-call hash, rechecks source identity, consumes or refunds the exact reservation, and durably terminalizes the attempt/result. It returns a content-bound durable terminalization proof only after commit. `finalize(dispatched, terminalization_proof)` rejects any scope/hash/budget-state mismatch and only then exposes the final response and `CONSUMED` provider receipt. A convenience `extract()` may compose the phases only for an honestly hermetic test transport and produces `NOT_BILLABLE` proof; a live transport cannot bypass either durable boundary through it. A typed `MethodologyProviderRequestNotAccepted` signal is the sole refund authority: it may be raised only by a transport path carrying explicit nonacceptance proof and causes one fenced transaction to refund the immutable reservation, mark the attempt and Step 7 `FAILED` with `METHODOLOGY_PROVIDER_REQUEST_NOT_ACCEPTED`, and emit durable audit with zero result/canonical row. Generic SDK/network/time-out/connection exceptions are not that proof and remain `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`. A returned provider response is never a pre-response failure: if its tool envelope, schema, search proof, citation pairing, answer domain, semantic content, provider identity, stop metadata, or usage metadata is invalid, the attempt atomically becomes `FAILED` with `CONSUMED` budget, persisted sanitized response/request/hash/model/usage/latency provenance, no terminal result or canonical row, and the Step-7 failure code. The SDK adapter hashes the complete returned JSON before domain-field validation. Valid returned metadata is persisted exactly; an absent or malformed returned model/request/stop/usage field is persisted as null rather than coerced or replaced with an invented sentinel, while the raw-response hash durably binds the malformed envelope. Valid non-negative token values above the admitted ceiling are also persisted exactly on the failed attempt while the immutable reserved vector is consumed. These paths use `METHODOLOGY_PROVIDER_PROVENANCE_INVALID` or `METHODOLOGY_PROVIDER_USAGE_INVALID`; success continues to require complete, well-formed, configured-model-matching metadata within every admitted ceiling. Only that explicit transport proof may use `REFUNDED`; an ordinary transport exception after committed admission immediately and atomically marks the admitted attempt `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`, retains its reservation, fails Step 7, and never redispatches.

The Task-6 Anthropic request owns exactly one HTTP attempt: hidden SDK/client retries are disabled. Step 7 processes the deterministic task order through explicit per-task micro-boundaries while its `RunStep` remains `RUNNING`. For each task, a short tenant-scoped admission transaction renews and validates the exact worker lease, validates the task/span/input hashes, atomically inserts the unique `STARTED` attempt, reserves the complete signed Anthropic call/USD/input/output-token vector, emits safe durable admission audit, and commits. This is a narrow, explicit exception to section 5.1's provider-phase no-output-write rule: a `STARTED` attempt plus budget reservation is admission control, not a success output, and is required to prevent a crash from causing a second paid call. No transaction remains open during the HTTP call; periodic heartbeats use the existing independent short transactions.

The reservation uses the active signed policy, construction-bound global call cap, one call, zero embedding tokens, and a deterministic reservation UUID bound to task/input/policy. The extractor first constructs the complete canonical billable Anthropic request, including system/prompt text, all message content and admitted source text, output schema/tool or JSON-mode material, model controls, and configured output `max_tokens`; its UTF-8 byte count is the conservative input-token ceiling. A versioned code-owned catalog, `anthropic-standard-price-catalog-2026-07-20-v1`, permits only exact configured model IDs and standard non-batch, non-cached, <=200K-context pricing. Its `claude-sonnet-4-20250514` row is USD 3.00 per million input tokens and USD 15.00 per million output tokens, sourced from Anthropic's official pricing table. Unknown models, caching, batch mode, beta long context, or a request above the 200K ceiling fail before dispatch.

Worst-case reserved USD is `ceil_8(input_token_ceiling * input_price / 1_000_000 + max_tokens * output_price / 1_000_000)`, not `component max_usd / component max_calls`. The complete one-call/USD/input/output vector must fit the signed policy before dispatch. For success, returned observed input/output tokens and the catalog-computed observed USD must each be non-negative and no greater than the reservation, and the observed model must equal the configured model. A valid non-negative observed value above an admitted ceiling instead fails `METHODOLOGY_PROVIDER_USAGE_INVALID`, persists the exact returned value, and consumes only the immutable reserved vector; it never enlarges the signed budget after dispatch. Malformed or negative usage follows the null-provenance failure contract above. The immutable conservative vector is consumed after any returned response, whether semantically accepted or terminally invalid, and is refunded only after a confirmed pre-response failure; the budget ledger never trusts provider estimates or silently releases an unproven delta.

After each dispatch, a fenced per-task terminal transaction revalidates the complete admitted span identity and persists `SUCCEEDED` attempt proof, validated terminal result plus normalized factual outputs, budget consumption, and audit while Step 7 remains `RUNNING`. A confirmed pre-response provider failure atomically finalizes the attempt as `FAILED`, refunds the exact reservation, audits, and fails Step 7 with a safe code; it creates no terminal result or canonical row. A post-response validation failure uses the same fenced atomic failure boundary but consumes rather than refunds the reservation and persists the safe response provenance enumerated above. After the last task, a separate fenced final boundary proves: selected applicable question IDs equal the distinct methodology-question IDs projected from planned tasks; every selected question has at least one task; and planned task IDs equal admitted-attempt task IDs equal terminal-result task IDs. It then validates every result's non-empty/structured outcome and only then completes Step 7. It never compares a question-ID set directly with a task-ID set. This permits restart to rehydrate committed task results without either holding 100 responses in memory or completing the step after the first call.

If the process dies or ownership changes after the request could have been accepted but before terminal proof commits, the surviving `STARTED` row becomes `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`; restart never calls the provider again and atomically fails Step 7 with `METHODOLOGY_ATTEMPT_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` unless a future explicitly approved provider-reconciliation contract resolves it. The attempt's admission `lease_owner_id` and `lease_fencing_token` remain immutable provenance. Every Task-6 write boundary sets an internal transaction-local current-owner/current-token context only after the repository has proved the exact unexpired run lease. The database attempt guard validates that context against the current `runs` row. Ordinary insert and `STARTED -> SUCCEEDED|FAILED` terminalization additionally require the context to equal the attempt's immutable admission fence. The sole recovery exception is `STARTED -> OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`: the stored admission fence remains unchanged, while the context may be either that still-active admission fence or a strictly higher active successor token after reclaim. Therefore the new worker can close the old ambiguous admission without rewriting provenance, while the prior worker's stale context cannot write any terminal state. This uses transaction-local database context only and adds no column, table, environment variable, or feature flag. A stale worker cannot admit, finalize, materialize, or mark recovery and receives `WORKER_LEASE_FENCED`.

#### Durable coverage projections

Task 6 updates the existing `methodology_coverage_records` rows at two ordinary fenced completion boundaries; it adds no coverage table, column, environment variable, flag, or Task-7 surface. Per-task micro-boundaries never mutate coverage. Both projections are derived only from tenant/deal/run/selection-scoped durable rows, lock the complete 100-row coverage set in `coverage_record_id` order, validate an exact and disjoint source set, update coverage, emit durable audit, and complete the owning `RunStep` in one transaction. A missing, duplicate, extra, cross-row, semantically invalid, or nonterminal task/result/link aborts the boundary and leaves all 100 coverage rows unchanged.

The final Step-7 boundary groups the authoritative terminal results by the exact `(coverage_record_id, methodology_question_record_id)` lineage after proving that every selected question has one or more planned tasks and every task has exactly one accepted terminal result. All `EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND` results project to `EVIDENCE_MISSING` with reason `METHODOLOGY_EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND_ALL_TASKS_V1`. One or more `EVIDENCE_FOUND` results and no not-found result project to `EXTRACTED` with reason `METHODOLOGY_EVIDENCE_FOUND_ALL_TASKS_V1`. A mixed found/not-found task set projects to `EXTRACTED` with reason `METHODOLOGY_EVIDENCE_FOUND_WITH_TASK_GAPS_V1`, preserving the task-level gap rather than hiding it. Step 7 writes empty `evidence_links`, null `answer`, and empty conflict/defect IDs; the result and gap tables remain the authority until canonical materialization. Task 6 never emits `PARTIALLY_ANSWERED`, `NOT_APPLICABLE`, or `BLOCKED` from this projection.

Steps 8 and 9 do not mutate coverage. At the Step-10 completion boundary, after the complete canonical snapshot passes the factual-output/claim/EvidenceItem/Sanad/defect set-equality checks, the transaction recomputes the projection from committed Task-6 source/result/link/canonical rows. A found question with no required calculation becomes `ANSWERED`, retains its all-found or mixed-gap reason, receives sorted unique `(claim_id, evidence_id)` links, and receives a deterministic answer whose text is the newline join of non-empty canonical claim texts ordered by canonical claim ID, whose claim/evidence IDs are the exact sorted unique link sets, whose calculation IDs are empty, and whose `requires_calculation` value is false. Its defect IDs are the exact sorted unique governed defect-link set and conflict IDs remain empty because Task 6 has no conflict producer. An all-not-found question remains `EVIDENCE_MISSING` with empty canonical payloads and must have zero canonical links. A question that explicitly requires calculation remains `EXTRACTED` with no answer until a later calculation step can supply governed calculation IDs; Task 6 never fabricates them.

The claim link itself binds the complete coverage lineage `(tenant_id, deal_id, run_id, selection_id, coverage_record_id, methodology_version_record_id, methodology_question_record_id)` through a composite foreign key and a matching index; a caller cannot substitute a coverage row from another selected methodology question. Coverage projection is deterministic, order-independent, fenced, RLS-scoped, and replay-safe. An exact replay is a no-op, including `updated_at`; a partial or tampered predecessor is rejected rather than repaired, downgraded, or overwritten. Restart rehydration recomputes and validates the expected Step-7 or Step-10 projection from durable rows according to the completed step state and never trusts `RunStep.result_summary`. For Step 10, the coverage proof and canonical snapshot load occur on one connection in one database transaction/snapshot; a separately validated coverage view can never be paired with a later canonical view. A crash before either completion commit rolls back the whole projection; a crash after commit rehydrates the exact committed projection.

#### Task-6 specification-review correction

The fresh Task-6 specification review makes the existing integrity requirements explicit before corrective implementation. The governed prompt metadata and registry pin the raw prompt UTF-8 SHA-256 plus both canonical schema SHA-256 values; the PROD resolver validates the typed `prod` pointer, registry and metadata identity/path agreement, strict UTF-8 bytes, and all three pins. Frozen `question_text` and admitted span text use whitespace only for nonblank validation and otherwise preserve exact bytes. For Task-6 admission, `document_spans.content_hash` means SHA-256 of the exact UTF-8 `text_excerpt`; planning, request construction, admission, terminalization, and materialization rehash those bytes and fail on drift.

Migration `0037` adds one supporting attempt-proof field, lowercase `price_catalog_row_sha256`, inside the already authorized `methodology_extraction_attempts` relation. It is immutable from admission, is included in the terminal proof, and must equal the freshly resolved exact catalog row on every returned-response terminal path. This correction adds no table, environment variable, feature flag, provider, Task-7 relation, receipt, edge, or live call. The migration also tightens only already-planned keys and guards: attempt/result task-input identity and one-result-per-attempt; task/task-span document identity; claim-link coverage-to-factual-task identity; exact admitted `(span_id, locator_sha256)` evidence identity; one run-scoped link per governed defect; and required Task-6 EvidenceItem fields, including rejection of later invalidating updates.

Strict restart rehydration reloads the selected frozen 21+79 pair and exact 100-row coverage set, then recomputes every durable task payload and its complete ordered task-span row set before returning any executable task. Missing, extra, reordered, cross-document, hash-drifted, or incomplete task/span rows fail before transport dispatch. Distinct spans may share one canonical locator because citation identity is the paired tuple, never the locator hash alone. Every Step-7 micro-boundary locks and requires the exact canonical Step-7 row to remain `RUNNING`. Fresh canonical claims are accepted only at the independently recomputed UUIDv5. Step-10 completion requests `REPEATABLE READ` before its first SQL statement so its coverage proof and canonical graph share one PostgreSQL snapshot. Populated-downgrade refusal, all of these join substitutions, and the production durable-loader/wire paths require watched PostgreSQL or composition-root RED proofs.

#### Task-6 genuine CDD/FDD planning-composition correction

The fresh post-implementation specification review found one remaining Critical blocker: the real preflight summary has no authenticated `evidence_tags` producer, drops secondary CDD/FDD classifications, and the legacy planner rejects a task before extraction when fabricated tags are absent. The checked-in 21-question CDD source also uses a free-form target vocabulary that the private alias dictionary does not fully or honestly normalize. A real CDD/FDD import, freeze, selection, 100-row coverage initialization, production preflight summary, and durable planner therefore cannot create the required complete ready graph. Synthetic tags and uniform constructed registries are explicitly non-evidence and are removed from the strict composition proof.

Task 6 resolves this as one governed admission contract, with no Alembic change, table, column, RLS policy, environment variable, feature flag, provider call, Task-7 surface, or live call:

- Add the checked-in, strict-UTF-8, duplicate-key-rejecting artifact `src/idis/methodology/mappings/commercial_dd_v1_question_categories.json` and its one canonical packaged schema beside it at `src/idis/methodology/mappings/commercial_dd_v1_question_categories.schema.json`; both load through fixed `importlib.resources` package identities and are contract-locked, so source and built-wheel bytes are identical. The artifact is `cdd-question-category-map-v1`, mapping version `1.0.0`, is valid only for `commercial_dd:v1`, raw methodology source SHA-256 `57cfec98436514cae717e359fa872504a7b9e1115c7bc12a4561e0989c4281fc`, imported registry SHA-256 `13f38ef7afa9e96828ff723d32b650a16a9d4817f4dd6b5fab1bf0aafcdde2db`, classifier contract `cdd-document-classifier-v2`, and the complete ordered 21-question inventory. Its canonical payload hash is pinned in code and the contract lock; any byte/schema/identity/version/source/registry/question/category/reachability drift fails closed with `METHODOLOGY_CDD_CATEGORY_MAP_UNAVAILABLE`, `METHODOLOGY_CDD_CATEGORY_MAP_INVALID`, or `METHODOLOGY_CDD_CATEGORY_MAP_DRIFT`.
- The governed question-to-category sets are exact: `market_size=[market_research]`; `market_growth=[business_plan_assumptions,market_research]`; `market_structure=[market_research]`; `customer_segments=[customer_evidence,sales_pipeline]`; `customer_concentration=[commercial_contracts,customer_evidence]`; `customer_quality=[customer_evidence]`; `churn_retention=[customer_evidence,sales_pipeline]`; `sales_pipeline=[sales_pipeline]`; `pricing=[commercial_contracts,pricing_unit_economics,sales_pipeline]`; `unit_economics=[business_plan_assumptions,pricing_unit_economics,sales_pipeline]`; `gtm_model=[gtm_distribution,sales_pipeline]`; `distribution=[gtm_distribution,sales_pipeline]`; `competition=[competitive_landscape,market_research]`; `pmf=[customer_evidence,product_technology]`; `technology_diff=[product_technology]`; `contracts=[commercial_contracts]`; `revenue_drivers=[business_plan_assumptions,sales_pipeline]`; `expansion=[customer_evidence,sales_pipeline]`; `management=[management_team]`; `risks=[commercial_risks]`; and `growth_assumptions=[business_plan_assumptions]`. Each key is the corresponding full `mq_commercial_dd_<key>_0001` question ID, category arrays are sorted, unique, and nonempty, and every row binds the imported question hash.
- Complete `cdd-document-classifier-v2` with narrowly token-bounded, negative-tested production rules for `gtm_distribution` (`go to market`, `gtm`, `distribution channel`, `channel performance`, `sales plan`, `marketing report`), `management_team` (`management team`, `founder profile`, `leadership team`, `executive team`, `management reference`), and `commercial_risks` (`commercial risk`, `commercial risks`, `risk register`). Generic `team`, `risk`, `channel`, or `management presentation` alone never activates these rules. The classifier emits a deterministic primary plus sorted unique secondary CDD/FDD category set whose exact productive reachability set and hash are bound by the mapping artifact.
- `RunDocumentPreflightDecision`, `SafePreflightClassificationInput`, and the persisted run-step summary carry typed ordered secondary CDD/FDD arrays. Missing, malformed, duplicate, enum-invalid, or noncanonical arrays fail closed; old summaries do not silently default. Raw span text and evidence tags remain absent from the safe preflight summary. Strict matching considers the full primary-plus-secondary category set and records the deterministic matched category, classifier version/reachability hash, and category-map version/hash inside the existing canonical task JSON payload.
- Strict durable planning uses a typed injected category-and-span admission policy. It requires an exact governed question mapping, a matching real primary or secondary classification, and one or more admitted durable spans with exact document/span/content/locator/text identity. It never uses `SourceSpanReference.evidence_tags` as authority and never fabricates tags. `required_evidence` remains mandatory and byte/canonical-preserved in the frozen question, task payload, extractor request, and provider input. Only the real extractor decides whether the admitted searched scope yields cited `EVIDENCE_FOUND` or nonempty structured `EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND`; absence can never block planning or create a free fact.
- Advance the strict selected-pair planner, task-payload, and classifier contracts to v2. Recompute classification hashes, task-input hashes, deterministic extraction-task IDs and record UUIDs, and restart expectations. Candidate-span-set identity remains the exact ordered real span, document, content, and locator identity. Resume reloads the exact map and classifier contracts and rejects missing secondaries or mapping/classifier drift rather than repairing or falling back to `_CDD_CATEGORY_ALIASES`.

The correction is RED-first at the production composition root: (1) the actual CDD artifact must prove all 21 question mappings and every mapped category classifier-reachable; (2) adversarial mapping/classifier drift and broad false-positive phrases must fail; (3) real preflight objects must preserve secondary categories through safe summary serialization while carrying no fabricated tags; (4) real CDD plus genuine FDD import/freeze/select, 100-row coverage, real preflight summaries, and the durable PostgreSQL planner must persist complete ready task/span coverage for all 100 applicable questions with zero provider calls; (5) secondary-only matches must record their selected category; and (6) restart must reproduce the exact mapping, task IDs, payloads, and graph or fail closed on drift.

Add the DRAFT prompt artifact/schemas, typed one-request Anthropic adapter with its lower hermetic transport seam, PROD-only runtime resolver, governed price-catalog budget admission, per-task terminal boundaries, immutable provenance validation, and durable attempt/result path. Empty, hermetic-as-live, or invalid provider/result semantics fail with stable Task-6 codes and zero false success.

Run focused Task-6 suites, Task-1 lease/restart regressions, Task-5 methodology regressions, the full declared-environment repository suite, Ruff format/check, no-cache mypy, contract lock, forbidden scan, import-identity check, disposable-PostgreSQL proofs, and `git diff --check`. Stop uncommitted at the Task-6 checkpoint with hashes and declared-environment numbers. No live calls, Task-7 schema, commit, push, or PR.

### 1.34 Proposed Task 9 provider-real contract correction (pending checkpoint review)

The hermetic Track-B review found that the first provider-surface draft could pass fixtures that are not executable against the documented provider schemas. The following correction is implemented only in the uncommitted new-files Track-B boundary and returns with that checkpoint for explicit review. It introduces no environment variable, flag, table, migration, active registry wiring, live call, or spend.

- **EPO OPS:** the OAuth normalizer accepts the documented required token fields while tolerating bounded extra metadata and validates decimal-string `expires_in`; it never persists or reports token response metadata. The patent operation is `GET /3.2/rest-services/published-data/search/biblio?q=pa%3D<verified-assignee>&Range=1-1`, because the unqualified `/search` result does not carry the title/applicant fields required by the contract. The XML validator requires the documented biblio-search/query/range/count hierarchy, a unique complete DOCDB record, deterministic applicant/title selection, exact selector/query binding, and a globally scoped publication identifier composed from country + document number + kind. A schema-valid zero-result envelope is `MISS`; a structurally empty root is not.
- **Semantic Scholar:** the fixed Gate-A probe remains the known public `CorpusId:215416146` paper-detail request, but the production connector exposes a typed selector dispatcher rather than treating `company_name` as a paper ID. Authorized selector alternatives are: exact paper identifier; exact Semantic Scholar author ID; topic query plus a closed Semantic Scholar field of study; or verified ROR organization ID plus exact Semantic Scholar author ID. The paper, author-paper, topic-search, and organization-author verification branches share the same versioned builder/normalizer dispatcher and one bounded deadline. Every `HIT` is bound to the requested selector; list/search `MISS` requires a valid zero-count/empty-data envelope; 404, ambiguity, mismatch, or malformed count/data is failure. `externalIds.CorpusId` is a positive integer, while the remaining external identifiers retain their governed string types. The legacy connector interface may not guess a selector type; the later active Task-9 composition must call the typed entry point from the admitted selector.
- **Probe receipt integrity:** the generic runner enforces the contract's exact normalized-field set, duplicate/unknown-field rejection, ordered operation closure, and a top response hash independently derived from the ordered operation hashes. Empty normalized values fail except for the contract-declared Perplexity `supported_synthesis=()` tuple, which records that unsupported prose was dropped while the separately required citations stay non-empty; `None`, empty citations, and every undeclared empty collection remain blockers. The transport checks the shared monotonic deadline after response EOF and after normalization, and request-construction/type failures map to stable secret-safe blockers. Hermetic observations remain `HERMETIC_TEST` and `acceptance_eligible=false`.
- **Admission and persistence seam:** every rights, credential, budget, and cache snapshot is bound to the exact tenant/run/provider/catalog/identity scope that can authorize it. `NOT_APPLICABLE` carries a positive listing decision and a content-bound admission proof; an applicable execution carries exact provider/selector/request/builder lineage; cache reuse is revalidated from typed cached outcome/fact provenance and emits fresh current-run intents plus an explicit unused-reservation disposition. Rights class is persisted, Perplexity synthesis is revalidated at the persistence boundary, and malformed collaborator values always fail with stable secret-safe codes.
- **B6 identity and execution proof refinement:** `verified-company-identity-v1` rederives domain-separated evidence/provenance set hashes and a complete tenant/deal/version/rule/listing/identifier identity hash, retaining the IDs and hashes through admission and persistence intents. Budget/cache collaborators bind deal and run; noncommercial rights rows cannot carry a credential-version link; collaborator exceptions map to constant payload-free codes. Catalog-pinned connector/builder lineage and ephemeral per-operation request/response bytes independently ground the persisted hashes. Safe source references reject local/user-info/signed or credential-bearing forms. `external-context-fact-v2` binds citation/Sanad tier/usage/eligibility, cache reuse revalidates the complete source fact and retrieval time, and a rights indexing denial removes both Graph and RAG edges. Exact-lineage cache reuse remains conservative: renewed-rights or new-decision compatibility is not inferred and degrades to a fresh call until a separately reviewed compatibility proof exists.

### 1.38 Task-7 section-20.2 semantic authority ruling and SQL-body RED gate

The 2026-07-22 reviewer ruling approves the dashboard equation and the binding principles for
the remaining guards. Tests still precede bytes. The corrected v3 preimage adds one closed
`SqlAuthoritySpecV1` registry whose ordered rows contain an ordinal, versioned authority ID,

ordered registry receives its own domain-separated hash. Historical
`FutureNativeAuthoritySpecV1`/`FutureReferenceAuthoritySpecV1`/`FutureChildAuthoritySpecV1` stay
byte-unchanged; explicit V2 shapes add an ordered `sql_authority_id_tuple` before their schema
hash, so every relation binds the exact bodies that govern it and no trigger body can live as
unbound prose.

The dashboard root-nullability equation is exact. `GAP` has a structured Task-6
`EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND` result root with non-empty search-scope/provider provenance and zero factual
outputs, but no evidence, Sanad, or calculation roots. `ANSWERED` and `RED_FLAG` require result,
evidence, and Sanad roots; RED_FLAG has the same evidentiary burden. Calculation-root presence is
the frozen Task-5 question's `any(required_calculations[*].required)` over the canonical payload:
the field must be an array, every member must be an object with an explicit boolean `required`
key, and malformed/missing/non-boolean values fail closed before `bool_or` is evaluated. Task 6's
missing-key-as-true Python fallback is defensive only and does not define this database guard.
The calculation decision is never runtime opinion. All currently admitted CDD/FDD rows declare
false, so their calculation roots are null. The existing calculation table has no coverage/question ID,
meaning the database can prove required root presence but not exact row-to-calculation lineage;
adding that lineage remains a separately reviewed schema amendment, not a v3 invention.

The resolved body tests require the literal expanded 18-provider Graph/RAG namespace equation;
null-safe RAG-query/index equality over namespace/provider/domain; READY plus exact zero
blocker/deferred/default counts and required/observed projection equality; the exact
`EXTRACT:rag_context` parent; three bidirectional Muhasabah guards; DB-level finite-binary64
checks and negative-zero normalization; finite native-root exact-one dispatch; and the two
coordinate-only generic child guards. Every resolved body has paired legal/illegal/raw-SQL cases.
Direct PostgreSQL tests load the artifact's exact float authorities, accept the inclusive
`0`/`1` boundaries, reject finite values outside them, reject NaN and both infinities in both
float columns, and prove `float8send` persists negative-zero input as positive-zero bits.

The tests-first audit leaves the following semantic choices explicit rather than letting the
materializer invent them:

1. **Grading cardinality.** Sections 4.5.1/19 require one physical row per distinct
   methodology/generic source claim-link root, while the new wording says one per canonical
   claim/run. The recommended reconciliation retains every source-root row for provenance and
   enforces one semantic `(sanad_id,grade,grading_sha256)` tuple per canonical claim/run across
   those rows. A literal one-physical-row-per-claim design needs another normalized source-root
   mapping relation.
2. **Erasure request states.** The application deliberately retries `FAILED` erasures, while the
   existing Task-5/6 database guards admit only `REQUESTED`. The recommended closed status set is
   `REQUESTED|FAILED`, with the request row locked and revalidated inside the same transaction.
3. **Erasure principal representation and authority.** A hermetic artifact cannot embed the
   deployment-specific application role name. The recommended representation hash-binds a
   logical `CONFIGURED_APP_ROLE` ACL principal, resolves and safely quotes it only during
   migration/readiness, and keeps the SECURITY-DEFINER body, secure
   `search_path=pg_catalog,pg_temp`, qualified relations, PUBLIC revoke, signature, owner, and
   overload census literal. This still lets any holder of the shared application database
   credential invoke the function directly, bypassing API ADMIN/hold/audit sequencing. The
   reviewer must either accept that boundary explicitly or authorize a dedicated NOLOGIN owner
   plus separately credentialed erasure-executor role/capability and its required config/readiness
   amendment. A forgeable custom GUC is never sufficient.
4. **Dashboard ANSWERED/RED_FLAG lineage.** The approved nullability equation does not say how a
   singular result/evidence/Sanad root represents a Task-6 question that can own multiple factual,
   evidence, and Sanad roots, nor what durable adverse signal distinguishes `RED_FLAG` from
   `ANSWERED`. The recommended complete design replaces representative roots with a normalized
   per-dashboard-row root-link set and makes `RED_FLAG` depend on an exact same-coverage governed
   adverse/contradiction relation; choosing a representative would lose evidence and is not
   recommended.
5. **Calculation lineage.** Presence can be checked from the frozen methodology payload, but the
   existing calculation row cannot prove that its root belongs to that coverage/question. The
   reviewer must classify this as a current-data-safe v3 shape limitation plus blocks-live item
   (all current `required` flags are false), or authorize a coverage/question lineage amendment
   now. The no-gap recommendation is the lineage amendment.
6. **Child projection digests.** Identity and scoring specify deterministic row order but not the
   canonical hash domain and complete parent/row preimage. Their positive executable cases remain
   intentionally absent. Recommended tuples are
   `H(["company-identity-identifier-projection-v1",tenant_id,deal_id,run_id,identity_output_id,run_output_object_id,count,ORDERED[(identifier_kind,identifier_hash,identity_identifier_id,verification_provenance_sha256)]])`
   and
   `H(["scoring-dimension-projection-v1",tenant_id,deal_id,run_id,scoring_output_id,run_output_object_id,count,ORDERED[(dimension_ordinal,dimension_key,hex(float8send(score)),hex(float8send(confidence)),rationale_sha256,confidence_justification_sha256,support_projection_sha256,muhasabah_projection_sha256,scoring_dimension_id)]])`.
7. **Deferred-guard mutation events.** The approved native-root exact-one guard is explicitly
   `AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE` on the root and every mapped child. The three CHECK
   authorities have no event tuple, negative-zero is exactly `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE`, and the
   append-only guard is exactly `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE`. The reviewed prose does not
   select exact event tuples for the other eleven deferred constraint authorities: dashboard,
   RAG-parent, EXTRACT-parent, three Muhasabah guards, grading, identity/scoring projections, and
   generic claim/evidence projections. The generator records these as unresolved and refuses the
   registry with `TASK7_SQL_AUTHORITY_EVENT_SET_UNRESOLVED`; it must not infer INSERT-only from the
   authority kind. The no-gap recommendation is `AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE` for all eleven,
   with ordinary mutation still denied by the append-only authority and coordinated erasure
   validated only at the deferred transaction boundary.

The SQL authority shape additionally declares every parent relation read by a guard and closes
its target/parent inventory over current and future relations; this is mechanically required by
the universal no-undeclared-mutable-state condition and is not a new semantic choice. Until the
seven rulings above are returned, explicit failing latches block v3 bytes, fixture generation,
migration `0038`, commits, and live calls.

The admission grammar is itself fail closed. Each decoded tuple item is one trimmed LF-only
statement; comments, top-level separators, nested dollar quoting, dynamic SQL, unknown or utility
forms, noncanonical quoted identifiers, and data-modifying CTE writes outside the target closure
are rejected. Every durable relation reference is exactly `public.<relation>`; unqualified,

approved parent. Trigger helpers are new (never `OR REPLACE`) exact `public.name()` functions with
empty signature, `RETURNS trigger`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, and
`search_path=pg_catalog,pg_temp`; trigger DDL has a closed authority-kind-specific timing/event/row

literal trigger and has exactly one PUBLIC revoke; execution grants are an empty closed set until
the erasure-principal ruling supplies an amended literal principal contract. Session state is
otherwise forbidden. The unresolved erasure authority may use only exact
`pg_catalog.current_setting`/`pg_catalog.set_config` calls for literal
`idis.erasure_context` with literal transaction-local `true`, plus the exact
`current_user<>session_user` predicate; arbitrary keys, session-wide state, replication-role
changes, time values, and extension `digest` calls are rejected. The hermetic generator repeats

### 1.39 Task-7 section-20.2 final semantic rulings and dedicated erasure authority

The 2026-07-22 final review resolves all seven latches in section 1.38. This section supersedes
the unresolved recommendations there without rewriting that review history. Tests remain before
artifact bytes, and the append-frozen full-strict-v3 artifact remains the sole normative byte
source.

1. **Grading cardinality.** `run_claim_grading_links` retains one physical row for every distinct
   methodology or generic source claim-link root. Across every such row for one canonical
   `(tenant_id,deal_id,run_id,claim_id)`, there must be exactly one semantic
   `(sanad_id,grade,grading_sha256)` tuple; a divergent tuple fails closed. `grade` remains in
   `A|B|C|D`. No additional mapping relation is introduced.
2. **Executable erasure states and attempt lineage.** The database erasure callable admits only
   `REQUESTED|FAILED`. It locks the exact request row and revalidates request ID, tenant, deal,
   state, and the approved erasure conditions inside the same short transaction. A FAILED retry is
   a new attempt, not a rewritten history item. Existing `audit_events`, not a new table or status,
   carry `request_id`, `attempt_id`, `attempt_ordinal`, `prior_status`,
   `prior_attempt_event_id`, `prior_attempt_event_hash`, and `outcome` under the exact event names
   `task7.erasure.attempted`, `task7.erasure.failed`, and `task7.erasure.succeeded`.
3. **Dedicated erasure authority.** The fixed database roles are
   `idis_erasure_function_owner` (`NOLOGIN NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS`) and
   `idis_erasure_executor` (`NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS`, separately credentialed). They are
   provisioned before migration; `0038` does not create cluster principals and instead refuses to
   proceed unless their exact posture is present. The owner owns only the approved DB-local
   function/support objects. The only public entry point is
   `public.idis_task7_execute_approved_erasure(uuid,uuid,uuid)`, with positional meanings
   `(request_id,tenant_id,deal_id)` and a JSONB result containing safe database deletion counts.
   It is SECURITY DEFINER with the reviewed fixed search path, PUBLIC has no EXECUTE, only
   `idis_erasure_executor` has EXECUTE, and the application role has none. The executor has no
   standing table privilege. A transaction-local `idis.erasure_context` is set only inside the
   callable and is a trigger marker, never authority; direct app SQL or a forged GUC still fails.
4. **Database/object-store boundary.** The callable atomically locks/revalidates the approved
   request and deletes the database graph in the exact child-first erasure order. Persistent
   object deletion cannot share that PostgreSQL transaction and remains an explicitly separate,
   ordered, idempotent operation under the existing erasure service and audit protocol. Reports
   must never claim cross-system ACID. Retriable partial outcomes are represented by the existing
   FAILED state and the append-only attempt lineage above; no schema or status is added.
5. **Lossless dashboard lineage.** Representative root columns are removed from the v3 future
   dashboard shape. `methodology_truth_dashboard_rows` binds the complete normalized root set by
   `root_projection_sha256`, and the new future-only child
   `methodology_truth_dashboard_root_links` is future-child ordinal 19 and erasure phase 65,
   executed before dashboard parent phase 41. Each child points to the exact dashboard parent and
   a scoped `run_output_objects` root. Its closed kinds preserve every Task-6 result, evidence,
   Sanad, applicable calculation, and governed `METHODOLOGY_DEFECT_LINK` root. `RED_FLAG` requires
   at least one such defect root whose Task-6 result lineage resolves to the same coverage record;
   `ANSWERED` forbids it. `GAP` remains the structured EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND result-only case. The
   deferred guard proves exact set equality, not representative membership or count equality.
6. **Calculation-to-question lineage.** `deterministic_calculations` gains nullable
   `coverage_record_id TEXT` and `methodology_question_record_id UUID`. They are both non-null for
   `producer_step_name='METHODOLOGY_DETERMINISTIC_CALCULATION'` and both null for historical rows
   and `producer_step_name='CALC'`. A scoped FK to the frozen Task-5/6 coverage-question parent
   proves the calculation belongs to the exact selected methodology row. The current real rows all
   declare calculation `required=false`, but RED fixtures exercise the required=true branch now.
7. **Projection preimages and deferred events.** The exact child digests are
   `H(["company-identity-identifier-projection-v1",tenant_id,deal_id,run_id,identity_output_id,run_output_object_id,count,ORDERED[(identifier_kind,identifier_hash,identity_identifier_id,verification_provenance_sha256)]])`
   and
   `H(["scoring-dimension-projection-v1",tenant_id,deal_id,run_id,scoring_output_id,run_output_object_id,count,ORDERED[(dimension_ordinal,dimension_key,hex(float8send(score)),hex(float8send(confidence)),rationale_sha256,confidence_justification_sha256,support_projection_sha256,muhasabah_projection_sha256,scoring_dimension_id)]])`.
   All eleven formerly unresolved deferred constraint authorities use exactly
   `AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE`; the generator must clear
   `TASK7_SQL_AUTHORITY_EVENT_SET_UNRESOLVED` only for those literal tuples.

The normalized dashboard child changes future child rows `4 -> 5`, all future relations

34, migration `0038` still creates exactly nine tables, and the forced-RLS inventory remains 64.
The 28 semantic vector, 47-rule vector, 1,756-route vector, 19-extractor vector, and their count

### 1.40 Approved Task-7 complete erasure-runner and parser-correction amendment

This section, including its executability refinement, supersedes section 1.39 items 2-4 wherever
they describe the runner body, read closure, privileges, hold sequencing, authority boundary, or
downgrade. The earlier text remains review provenance, not a competing active contract.

The reviewed v3 contract census remains exactly 72 physical relations and 66 erasure phases.
The executable erasure callable has a separate, hash-bound 86-relation deletion surface:
`RUNNER86 = REVIEWED72 UNION (PRODUCTION_ERASER_TARGETS MINUS REVIEWED72)`. The test derives
`REVIEWED72` by flattening the v3 `erasure_materialized.rows` tuples and derives the production
set directly from `PostgresErasureExecutor`'s `_CHILD_TABLE_DELETES`,
`_DIRECT_DELETE_ORDER`, and its explicit `deals`/`strict_preflight_proofs` deletes. The current
production set has 39 relations, of which 25 are already reviewed. Its exact 14-relation delta is
`break_glass_grants`, `claims`, `deal_assignments`, `deals`, `defects`, `evidence_items`,
`human_gate_actions`, `human_gates`, `methodology_extraction_attempts`,
`methodology_extraction_task_spans`, `overrides`, `run_methodology_selections`, `sanads`, and
`strict_preflight_proofs`.

Admission proves that the 72 relations are a strict subset of the 86; that the runner's set
difference from the reviewed census equals the production eraser's corresponding set
difference; and that the latter equals exactly the 14-relation set above. A future production
eraser target added without a reviewed runner amendment therefore fails the derivation instead
of being silently omitted. This executable superset does not change the independently bound
72/66 census or its activation semantics. Migration `0038` still owns exactly nine new tables and
the RLS inventory remains 64 tables.

Executable installation is split without weakening the reviewed whole. `FUTURE38` is the exact
ordered union of the 18 future-native relations (`methodology_truth_dashboard_rows`,
`company_identity_outputs`, `enrichment_provider_outcomes`, `enrichment_normalized_facts`,
`graph_projection_results`, `rag_index_results`, `rag_query_results`,

`run_claim_grading_links`), the 15 future-reference relations
(`company_identity_input_references`, `generic_extraction_input_references`,
`graph_result_input_references`, `rag_index_input_references`, `rag_query_input_references`,
`conflict_input_references`, `evidence_trust_court_input_references`,
`validated_evidence_package_input_references`, `debate_role_input_references`,
`analysis_agent_input_references`, `scoring_input_references`,
`layer2_readiness_input_references`, `layer2_role_input_references`,
`deliverable_artifact_input_references`, and `step_receipt_input_references`), and the five
future-child relations (`company_identity_identifiers`, `scoring_output_dimensions`,
`generic_extraction_claim_links`, `generic_extraction_evidence_links`, and
`methodology_truth_dashboard_root_links`). `CURRENT48` is the ordered `RUNNER86` projection after
removing exactly `FUTURE38`. Admission proves cardinalities `48 + 38 = 86`, disjointness, and exact
set equality: `CURRENT48 UNION FUTURE38 = RUNNER86` and
`CURRENT48 INTERSECT FUTURE38 = EMPTY`. Migration `0038` installs only the hash-bound CURRENT48
callable, returning exactly 48 safe per-relation count keys including zeroes. The
FUTURE38-dependent RUNNER86 replacement body, grants, and complete 86-key result remain
hash-frozen and inactive. Each table-creating migration
must validate its literal census contribution, and only the migration that completes the entire
FUTURE38 catalog may atomically replace CURRENT48 with the hash-checked RUNNER86 callable and its
ACL. Partial activation, runtime catalog branching, conditional DELETE, and dynamic SQL are
forbidden; the activated callable returns exactly 86 safe count keys.

The v3 artifact represents this in a dedicated normalized `erasure_execution_registry`, not by
overloading the future-table target tuple of `approved-erasure-append-only-v1`. The latter remains
the append-only guard authority for FUTURE38 tables. The execution registry contains literal
CURRENT48 active body/result/ACL tuples, literal RUNNER86 replacement body/result/ACL tuples, the
exact FUTURE38 activation census and hashes, the six-relation read projections, the advisory-lock
tuple, and the exact activation/downgrade predicates. Every SQL/ACL body is a literal byte field;
runtime source inspection or generated identifier substitution is not a hashing input.

The runner's complete declared read closure is exactly six relations: `erasure_requests`,
`deterministic_calculations`, `runs`, `human_gates`, `legal_holds`, and `document_artifacts`.
The last two provide the in-transaction active-hold census for the selected deal plus the exact
deal-scoped document/artifact target projection. All literal DELETE statements carry both the
exact tenant and deal predicates. Indirect children use their own tenant coordinate and the
declared parent's tenant/deal-scoped join; unscoped, tenant-only, undeclared-parent, or weakened
predicates fail admission. A disposable PostgreSQL proof builds and introspects the complete
86-relation FK graph, proves every child precedes every parent, and executes the order against
seeded rows. Mandatory witnesses include `run_steps -> runs`,
`calc_sanads -> deterministic_calculations`, and `human_gate_actions -> human_gates`.

Every runner and every durable legal-hold create/lift/update/delete transaction takes the same
transaction-scoped tenant advisory lock before reading or mutating hold state. The hash-bound
derivation `erasure-tenant-advisory-lock-v1` is exactly
`pg_catalog.hashtextextended('idis-task7-erasure-tenant-lock-v1:' || tenant_id::text, 0)` and is
acquired with `pg_catalog.pg_advisory_xact_lock`; no session lock or alternate key is admissible.
Concurrency RED proves both directions: a hold transaction that acquires the lock and commits an
active hold makes the waiting erasure recheck it and delete zero rows, while an erasure holding
the lock blocks a concurrent durable hold mutation until the erasure commits or rolls back.

`idis_erasure_function_owner` receives `USAGE` on the application schema, DELETE only on the
active runner surface (CURRENT48 at `0038`, RUNNER86 only after FUTURE38 activation), and only the
predicate-column SELECT privileges PostgreSQL requires: the six declared read projections,
`(tenant_id,deal_id)` on direct targets, and the exact tenant/parent-key columns on the three
indirect children. Its sole UPDATE grant is `UPDATE(request_id)` on `erasure_requests`, required
only for the row's `FOR UPDATE` lock; the admitted callable contains no UPDATE statement. It has
no INSERT, broader UPDATE, TRUNCATE, schema-create, role membership, SUPERUSER, or BYPASSRLS
authority. The append-only guard helper and runner have the identical fixed NOLOGIN owner.
`idis_erasure_executor` retains no table privileges and only the active callable's EXECUTE grant;
the application role and PUBLIC have none. Current guards admit only locked
`REQUESTED|FAILED` requests. A real PostgreSQL composition test proves FORCE RLS remains active
under transaction-local tenant and erasure context and that an unset, forged, cross-tenant,
cross-deal, held, or otherwise out-of-context invocation deletes zero rows.

The database function proves the exact request/status/hold state. ADMIN authorization, durable
audit emission, and invocation through the dedicated credential are the API composition
boundary; readiness continues to prove that the app role has no EXECUTE and cannot shortcut it.
The optional in-function audit precondition was evaluated and not added: current `audit_events`
has no immutable attempt-level relational binding to the exact request execution, so a text/JSON
match would not cleanly prove that sequence. No schema or seventh read relation is invented.
Downgrade restores the exact pre-`0038` helper/guard definitions, owners, grants, revocations, and
catalog state; the disposable proof remains upgrade -> downgrade -> upgrade.

The SQL parser corrections are RED-first. Named legal fixtures cover `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM`,
`FOR UPDATE`, `ROW(...)`, and the approved negative-zero assignment, while the complete existing
forbidden-form matrix remains green. Accepting reviewed-correct SQL must not make comments,
statement separators, dynamic SQL, undeclared relation access, unsafe session state, name
smuggling, or any previously rejected shape legal.

The complete normative details are in Task-7 design section 20.4. No real
`IDIS_ERASURE_DATABASE_URL` value is needed for hermetic artifact/parser work; the operator must
be notified immediately before the first operator-backed migration-0038/readiness proof and then
provision the credential for exactly `idis_erasure_executor` on the acceptance database.

### 1.41 Task-7 erasure executable-byte completion amendment

The pre-freeze audit found that the reviewed runner body was hash-bound but three required
execution surfaces were not yet represented in the normative bytes: direct legal-hold locking,
the retained `0031`/`0036`/`0037` guard replacements and exact restoration, and the later
FUTURE38 activation/inverse choreography. Those fields were subsequently materialized and the v3
artifact/fixture were append-frozen, but the post-freeze semantic audit in section 1.42 rejected
their admission. Migration `0038` remains unwritten.

`ErasureExecutionSpecV1` gains exactly nine normalized fields before its first freeze:
`hold_lock_contract_tuple`, `guard_authority_contract_tuple`,
`legacy_guard_replacement_tuple`, `hold_lock_upgrade_sql_utf8_hex_tuple`,
`hold_lock_downgrade_sql_utf8_hex_tuple`, `legacy_guard_upgrade_sql_utf8_hex_tuple`,
`legacy_guard_downgrade_sql_utf8_hex_tuple`, `future_activation_sql_utf8_hex_tuple`, and
`future_restoration_sql_utf8_hex_tuple`. All are literal bytes in the single execution row and
are independently reconstructed by the hermetic generator. This changes only the tuple-schema,
erasure-execution component, design-document, and final contract hashes; semantic, route,
extractor, provider, relation-count, and erasure-schedule vectors remain unchanged.

The legal-hold database guard is a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE` row trigger. It validates
the transaction-local tenant coordinate, rejects a tenant-changing update, and itself acquires
the exact PostgreSQL-16 transaction lock
`pg_catalog.pg_advisory_xact_lock(pg_catalog.hashtextextended('idis-task7-erasure-tenant-lock-v1:' || tenant_id::text,0))`
before returning the row. The durable repository still acquires the identical lock before its
first hold-state read or mutation; lift reads and updates in one transaction. Thus direct SQL is
made transaction-locked by construction without `pg_locks` introspection, session unlock probes,
try-locks, or any session-scoped lock primitive.

The exact retained replacement inventory is the strict-preflight guard; Task-5 selection and
coverage guards; and the Task-6 erasure-request helper, immutable guard, attempt guard, and
deferred result-output validator. Every replacement has a safe pinned search path, fully
qualified application relations/helpers, the dedicated executor/function-owner check before the
ungranted Task-7 context helper, transaction-local tenant and erasure context, and the closed
`REQUESTED|FAILED` state set. The deferred validator returns before reading deleted parents only
for that authenticated delete context. The runner sets all constraints deferred before the first
DELETE and forces all constraints immediate after the last DELETE while still under the fixed
owner; no deferred trigger escapes the authenticated boundary. Downgrade restores the literal
pre-`0038` function bodies, owner/proconfig expectations, and ordinary enabled trigger catalog
shape before dropping only the new helper and hold-lock objects.

The `0031` source body contains three `--` comments. One-line normalization removes those
comments before collapsing whitespace; retaining them would comment out the rest of the
dollar-quoted PL/pgSQL body and make downgrade non-executable. The executable comment-free
canonical restore digest is
`1a05ce8af35fe5102cba13e3198cdf92e82b966a15ea88552024fc7244c7e139`; the earlier
`936e51dc99b3e149216dfe9ec471597753aa40e42967ea718e18f855ff3174d4` normalization is
pre-freeze rejected evidence and is never admitted to the v3 artifact or fixture. The other six
legacy restore digests remain unchanged.

Future activation and its inverse are also literal: drop the one exact runner signature, create
the reviewed RUNNER86 or CURRENT48 body respectively, then apply its complete reset ACL. There is
no `CREATE OR REPLACE`, conditional/catalog branch, dynamic SQL, overload, `IF EXISTS`, or
unhashed migration-side rewrite. Activation remains gated on the complete hash-checked FUTURE38
schema census in the same table-creating migration transaction.

complete multi-required arrays, and missing-member rejection; all existing parser, ACL, hold,
scope, lineage, corruption, and vector gates remain. V4 must also preserve Task-9 fact persistence
and all permitted Layer-2-side consumers while making only Graph/RAG expectation derivation depend
on hash-bound immutable rights/indexability lineage. The proposed exact representation adds an
immutable `external_context_index_allowed BOOLEAN NOT NULL` provider-outcome field derived from
the governed Task-9 rights row, leaves E029's non-index consumers and E030 unconditional, and moves the

`47 -> 48`; E029 changes `269 -> 219`; E048 contributes 50; the overall static route count remains
