1.43 controls the RED-first materialization order; migration `0038` remains gated on the admitted
v4 artifact and fixture.

### 1.43 Task-7 full-strict-v4 approval and execution order

`full-strict-v4` is approved. The three frozen-v3 identifiers (artifact, fixture, and contract
hashes) are permanent void/blocklist entries. The append-frozen v3 artifact remains immutable
historical evidence; the v3 fixture is preserved by its sealed reconciliation hash and removed
from the active fixture tree. Active fixtures, registry/source bytes, migrations, contract locks,
and admission proofs may not contain any v3 blocklisted hash.

The v4 outcome authority adds immutable `external_context_index_allowed BOOLEAN NOT NULL`. Its
value is derived at outcome creation from the exact provider rights row—rights class/requirement,
no-store policy version, attribution policy version, rights-version hash—and the catalog's
external-context capability. It participates in outcome/native hashes and restart rehydration.
All 18 providers are covered against the rights registry, including a later-run policy/version
change that flips the value without mutating the earlier outcome.

The normalized provider outcome therefore persists the applicable rights-approval ID, rights
version hash, rights class, no-store policy version, attribution policy version, derived flag, and
the literal `enrichment-provider-outcome-sha256-v1` recipe identifier. `NOT_APPLICABLE` persists
all rights coordinates as null and the flag as false. The outcome recipe hashes the immutable
outcome coordinates, including that rights tuple and flag, before any output-root ID exists. A
separate `provider-outcome-native-output-v1` recipe then binds that outcome hash into
`native_output_sha256`; only then does the reviewed UUIDv5 name tuple derive the output-root ID.
The provider-outcome census requires both recipe/hash pairs, so completion and restart rederive an
acyclic outcome -> native output -> root chain and cannot recover the branch from an out-of-band
opinion or accept an arbitrary 64-hex placeholder.

For precision, the rights-row derivation applies to applicable `HIT`/`MISS` outcomes.
`NOT_APPLICABLE` remains identity/applicability-before-rights and therefore selects no rights row;
its immutable hash-bound flag is exactly `false`. The effective rights registry ultimately covers
all 18 providers, including explicit GREEN rows. Task 7 migration `0038` consumes the immutable
outcome flag only. The current four-row durable rights/envelope expansion to all 18 is a Task-9
integration migration handoff, not an unreviewed Task-7 schema change.

E029 keeps all non-index decisioning consumers unconditional; E030 keeps domain deliverables
unconditional; E048 alone gates the two Graph/RAG external-context routes on the immutable true
value. The v4 vector is exactly 48 rules and 1,756 routes: E029 `269 -> 219`, E048 `+50`, net zero.
Rights-denied facts remain durable and require every conflict/scoring/readiness/Layer-2/analysis/
deliverable receipt while producing zero external Graph/RAG rows or receipts. Both branches must
pass direct derivation, restart, and final three-way equality.

The same v4 bytes correct the null-URI erasure bypass and dashboard calculation equation: empty or
false-only requirement arrays are legal, while every `required=true` member needs its own exact
calculation lineage. Order is fixed: RED contracts, v4 bytes, append-frozen artifact, hermetic
fixture, complete proofs, then and only then migration `0038` RED. No commit, push, PR, live call,
or operator erasure credential is permitted before its existing checkpoint.

Canonical v4 materialization crossed the append-freeze boundary once on 2026-07-22. The frozen
artifact SHA-256 is `3705168991242fb49166f3b29404d35cd0775f5675771ae300366f131b4d9ff8`;
the embedded fixture SHA-256 is
`ea2471058ee1df38976af61c8b220b7413cea303d390a744a0d92b6366013b80`; and
`FULL_STRICT_V4_CONTRACT_HASH` is
`3fb3497c437b3d28ea208f0960a02c7b89fcac5510c7dc1ba0086173378b15b5`. The artifact contains
26 ordered sections, 28 semantic steps, 48 edge rules, 1,756 usage routes, 19 usage extractors,

orchestrator, and the Muhasabah validator must round-trip through the literal frozen schemas on
disposable PostgreSQL. A mismatch rejects the schema; production writers are not reshaped to make
the admission test pass.

The manifest member digest is exactly
`H(["deliverable-manifest-reference-digest-v1",tenant_id,deal_id,run_id,pipeline_contract_version,pipeline_contract_hash,manifest_deliverable_id,member_kind,catalog_ordinal_or_null,member_step_output_id,member_output_object_id,member_identity_sha256,member_value_sha256,member_content_sha256])`.
Root IDs are deliberately excluded to prevent circularity, and a regression must prove the digest
is stable when only root assignment changes. The exact current NK/P mapping and its static
rederivation helper are literal SQL inside the existing current-output authority row; dynamic SQL,
a new table, environment variable, flag, or authority registry is forbidden.

V9 preserves the 28-step, 48-rule, 1,756-route, 19-extractor, 18-provider, physical-census, rights,
and erasure-order vectors. Its artifact remains `append_frozen=false` until the constructor
round-trips and complete per-target legal, illegal, and direct-SQL PostgreSQL census pass. Only
then may the fixture be emitted and migration `0038` consume v9. V9 remains `INACTIVE` throughout
Task 7 and requires the existing separate activation review.

### 1.48 Task-7 v9 pre-freeze PostgreSQL executability stop-trigger

The required revision-`0037` plus exact-artifact-SQL census rejected the unfrozen v9 candidate
before fixture generation. The candidate artifact SHA-256 is
`a02cc1e892487e3bf80ce6a9255477bbeb8297706f81d51774c26312191186fe`; its SHA-512 is
`5b51205be91b013c6804f4f186a5f5413764a33c243457ff293617680772d6fa3a02e2ca1a455171ab9f2e8d08ebbc7ea0c08dd354eb93ed03a76d7de0e25878`.
It remains `append_frozen=false`; no v9 fixture or pipeline-contract hash exists, and migration
`0038` has not consumed these bytes.

Fresh disposable PostgreSQL at revision `0037` reached
`sql_authority_registry.rows[18].body_sql_utf8_hex_tuple[2]` (zero-based), authority
`current-output-exact-one-v1`, then rejected
`pg_catalog.unnest(f.accepted_source_span_ids,f.accepted_locator_sha256s)` with SQLSTATE `42883`
because PostgreSQL does not expose the multi-array `unnest(anyarray, anyarray)` overload as a
schema-qualified `pg_catalog` function. A byte census found three `pg_catalog.unnest` calls and
exactly one multi-array occurrence, isolating the defect.

An independent database probe proved that unqualified multi-array `unnest(uuid[],text[])`
succeeds and that the fully qualified, pairwise equivalent form
`ROWS FROM (pg_catalog.unnest(f.accepted_source_span_ids), pg_catalog.unnest(f.accepted_locator_sha256s)) AS zipped(span_id,locator_sha256)`
produces the same ordered pairs. The latter is the narrow recommended correction because it
retains explicit function qualification and changes only the invalid call shape. No artifact
byte is changed under this section: the stop-trigger requires a reviewer ruling on voiding and
blocklisting the v9 candidate and authorizing the corrected successor version.

and `cf3fd67c98341fe7373a88f4f5f2d72463b194ce7247541ed4c38d4e41c11900`;

The independent production-role review then found a distinct pre-freeze blocker. Each of the three

ordinary schema/table privileges therefore receives SQLSTATE `42501` on all three legal paths:

This is a broader existing class, not merely a three-helper v11 defect. A legal native-output
operation through the row-18 constraint trigger also fails at deferred-constraint flush with

helper has the same invoker/revoked-helper shape, and ordinary delete paths that reach the

V11 is therefore held, not frozen and not yet voided, pending a reviewer-approved executable-call

may start.

V12 equals the exact SHA-512-authenticated v11 bytes plus one hash-bound executable-authority
model and the four helper corrections it enables. The fixed role is
`idis_task7_guard_function_owner`, created as
`NOLOGIN NOSUPERUSER NOCREATEDB NOCREATEROLE NOINHERIT NOBYPASSRLS`. It has no role
memberships and never owns a tenant table. Every current trigger guard executes as
`SECURITY DEFINER` with exactly `search_path=pg_catalog,pg_temp`; every relation access remains

`PUBLIC` and the application role receive no direct helper `EXECUTE`.

The complete function/helper call graph, function signature, owner, execution security, locked
search path, target/event coordinates, direct parent closure, exact column-level `SELECT` grants,
and negative `INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE/REFERENCES/TRIGGER` and membership posture form literal
contract bytes in the v12 authority registry. Parent access is granted only to the closure declared
for that guard. The fixed owner is neither a table owner nor `BYPASSRLS`; every parent read remains
subject to the canonical forced-RLS policies and the transaction-local tenant context already
validated by the guard bodies. Privileged erasure helpers and the erasure owner/executor boundary
remain separate and unreachable to ordinary application credentials.

The executable registry distinguishes `CURRENT_0038`, `FUTURE_INACTIVE`, and
`EXTERNAL_DEPENDENCY` entries. The current set is exactly nineteen guard-owned routines: eleven
current trigger guards and eight ordinary helpers. Fourteen already-reviewed future trigger
guards remain hash-bound but inactive; migration 0038 must not grant access to, own, or attempt to
install their not-yet-existing relations. The erasure-context helper is the sole external
dependency and stays owned by the erasure-function owner, with `EXECUTE` granted only to the fixed
guard owner.

The current read authority is a per-function, column-level registry whose direct edges are checked
against the literal function bodies and whose transitive closure is derived through the helper-call
graph. It currently closes over exactly 36 relations and 366 column authorities. The older
`declared_parent_relation_tuple` narrative fields are not a grant source: they omit the transitive

The application principal is the authenticated runtime `session_user`, not a fixed login name;
the artifact binds that principal class and readiness proves the actual connection role has no
direct function execution grant.

Installation and downgrade are both contract bytes. Installation refuses a pre-provisioned guard
role with any pre-existing schema, relation, column, function, membership, or ownership authority;
it then grants exact column-scoped `SELECT(column,...)` privileges. Whole-table `SELECT` is neither

provisioned but has zero database authority after downgrade. Proofs inspect
`pg_attribute.attacl`/`aclexplode` as well as relation ACLs because table and column ACLs are
independent in PostgreSQL.

The materialization exact-diff gate permits only this authority registry, the four helper
corrections, v12 version literals, and dependent hashes. It rejects every other byte. The invariant

### 1.58 Standing contract-materialization autonomy

Until the Task 7 checkpoint, never-frozen candidates may be voided and incremented without an
additional ruling only for already-ruled mechanical classes: SQL syntax/dialect, PostgreSQL
execution mechanics, transcription drift against real DDL, FK/authority closure,
duplicate-derivation desynchronization, and census/fixture-shape completeness. Every successor
still requires a permanent void/blocklist record, exact-diff bounds, a class-closing admission
guard, reconciliation evidence, and a green application-role census before freeze. Three
consecutive failures in the same class stop this autonomy.

This autonomy cannot change any semantic vector or §18/§20.2 ruling, weaken a guard, add a table,
environment variable, flag, or role, alter erasure/ACL security, or touch money, rights, or
evidence meaning. It does not authorize commits, pushes, PRs, live calls, spending, or migration
consumption of an unfrozen contract.

Two honest boundaries prevent a Task-7 completion claim.

the authority exists and is isolated; it does not prove the product uses it. The current audit
validator also admits the legacy `erasure.*` taxonomy but rejects the reviewed literal
`task7.erasure.*` names, so the three exact names require a narrow contract regression rather than
a broad prefix bypass.

Implementing that composition requires semantic rulings outside the standing mechanical
materialization autonomy: the exact `attempt_id` derivation and event-hash preimage; first-attempt
null encoding; whether `prior_attempt_event_*` names the prior terminal or attempted event; closed
outcome literals; retry ordinal rules; serialization of concurrent ADMIN attempts; recovery from
an attempted event without a terminal event; and crash ordering around object deletion, the

request state. Authorizing that would change an approved authority/ACL ruling and may require a
frozen successor contract. No such choice is invented here.

Task 7 therefore remains incomplete and v18 remains `INACTIVE`. No full-tree completion result is

artifact SHA-256
`21bb93882a2d316bbf899cdae282c231b99a1aeea2239c2966954bfa7f4789c4`.
Its materializer SHA-256 is
`a94fe22a271498ac2276f4435271fb7db6d4160f2cabfd886cb5a7d6558620ae`;

V19 retains the exact callable signature
`public.idis_task7_execute_approved_erasure(uuid,uuid,uuid)`, the CURRENT48/FUTURE38/RUNNER86
relation sets and ordering, the existing erasure owner/executor roles, and every previously
approved semantic vector. It changes only the reviewed erasure authority/composition bytes,
their ACL/read closure, the closed lineage schema, version bytes, and dependent hashes. No table,
column, role, environment variable, or flag is added.

The hash-bound lineage contract is exact:

1. The only admitted event/outcome pairs are
   `task7.erasure.attempted`/`ATTEMPTED`,
   `task7.erasure.failed`/`FAILED`, and
   `task7.erasure.succeeded`/`SUCCEEDED`. The generic audit validator admits these three exact
   names without admitting a general `task7.*` prefix.
2. Attempt identities use UUIDv5 namespace
   `4520d8ed-e929-5486-821c-82f8901ae7fa` over canonical JSON
   `["task7-erasure-attempt-v1",tenant_id,deal_id,request_id,attempt_ordinal,prior_status,prior_event_id_or_null,prior_event_hash_or_null]`.
   The first attempt has ordinal `1` and explicit JSON null prior coordinates. An open attempt is
   reused. Only a preceding `FAILED` terminal permits a new attempt; its ordinal increments by
   exactly one and its prior coordinates name that immediate failed terminal event.
3. Audit-event identities use UUIDv5 namespace
   `dd76cee1-28d5-55f8-b21a-eb64bc2e4be4` over canonical JSON
   `["task7-erasure-audit-event-v1",attempt_id,event_type,outcome]`. An event hash is SHA-256 of
   the exact canonical validated stored event and is referenced by the next attempt; it is never
   embedded circularly in the event it hashes.
4. Each lineage event carries safe
   `request_id`, `attempt_id`, `attempt_ordinal`, `prior_status`,
   `prior_attempt_event_id`, `prior_attempt_event_hash`, and `outcome` fields and retains actor,
   tenant, deal, and timestamp. The succeeded event binds the exact persisted counts.
5. The attempted event binds the object target set as
   `SHA256(CJ(["task7-erasure-object-target-set-v1",tenant_id,deal_id,artifact_row_count,ORDERED[SHA256(UTF8(uri))]]))`.
   Raw URIs never enter safe reports or audit summaries. Object reconciliation stages only the
   honest integer `object_rows_reconciled`; already-absent bytes are reconciled rather than
   falsely counted as deleted.

The database function's read closure gains column-limited
`audit_events(event_id,tenant_id,event_type,event)`. Its owner retains only the already reviewed
request-row-lock privilege plus column-limited update of
`erasure_requests(request_id,status,executed_by,executed_at,counts)`. It receives no INSERT,
broader UPDATE, role membership, table ownership, or RLS bypass. Before any deletion, the
function validates exactly one valid open attempted event, the request/tenant/deal/actor/ordinal
chain, REQUESTED-or-FAILED state, current hold state, object-target digest, and
`object_rows_reconciled`. In one transaction it deletes the exact current relation set and
atomically persists `EXECUTED`, `executed_by`, `executed_at`, and exact database plus object
reconciliation counts, then returns those persisted values. Reconstructing counts after rows are
gone or manufacturing zeros is forbidden.

The owner receives no EXECUTE edge to the guard-owner canonical-JSON, SHA-1, or UUIDv5 helpers.
The runner therefore hash-binds a closed local realization of those already-approved algorithms
and proves it against the Python canonical-event and both UUIDv5 fixed vectors in PostgreSQL.
Using `jsonb::text` as a substitute for the canonical stored-event bytes is forbidden.

The production composition uses one connection authenticated by the existing
`IDIS_ERASURE_DATABASE_URL`. It acquires the existing deterministic tenant advisory lock at
session scope. After lock acquisition, request state and lineage are rehydrated through the
application role so a waiter cannot act on a stale pre-lock snapshot. While the dedicated
connection retains the lock, a separate short application-role/RLS read transaction enumerates
and hashes the immutable target set. The dedicated executor retains zero standing table
privileges and issues only lock/unlock plus the admitted function call. The composition persists
the attempted event carrying that digest before any object mutation, performs bounded object
reconciliation without holding a database transaction open across external I/O, then invokes the
function in a short transaction on the locked dedicated backend. Existing hold mutations use the
same lock key, and the hold check runs after the locked rehydration and before target enumeration
or object mutation. The connection is explicitly unlocked and closed; a process death releases
the session lock. This serializes the workflow without claiming cross-connection or cross-system
ACID.

Crash recovery is binding. If the function commits deletion, terminal request state, and counts
but the API dies before persisting `task7.erasure.succeeded`, a retry observes `EXECUTED` plus
the persisted counts and the open attempt before applying the ordinary already-executed
conflict. It writes the same deterministic succeeded event from those values and performs zero
object reconciliation, zero database deletion, zero function invocation, and zero recount.
An existing identical deterministic event is verified; a conflicting event with the same ID
fails closed.

The generic middleware event `erasure.executed` is suppressed only for the exact authenticated
execute-erasure route after durable Task-7 terminal lineage is proven. Before that suppression
lands, field-level tests must prove the Task-7 lineage retains everything the generic event
carried: actor, tenant, deal, outcome, and timestamp. No other mutation route may lose its audit
event, and an execute response without proven replacement lineage fails closed. Under PostgreSQL,
no production composition may construct or select the legacy `PostgresErasureExecutor` or the
in-memory twin. The app role cannot call the dedicated function, and no production composition
may issue the relation-set DELETEs through the app credential. Direct-SQL app-role cases retain
the existing canonical RLS/guard semantics; v19 does not invent a blanket global DELETE
revocation outside the reviewed ACL.

The full restart/equality boundary is now explicit. Task 7 proves the v19 schema, current guards,
ACLs, admission, deterministic derivation, and producer paths that exist today. It does not claim
the complete real-PostgreSQL 27-cut proof or three-way equality over all 48 rules. The existing

### 1.67 Task-7 full-strict-v20 RETURNING privilege closure

The approved v19 product composition and Task-11 boundary remain semantically authoritative,
but v19 itself is void. It was never signed, frozen, fixture-published, migration-consumed, or
runtime-selected. Its permanent void identifiers are:

- candidate artifact
  `21bb93882a2d316bbf899cdae282c231b99a1aeea2239c2966954bfa7f4789c4`;
- prospective frozen artifact
  `91a6d99322d7c2dd8fba862db9d4fe858e89f933cc22c1e126fdb9c5ec1dd76b`;
- prospective contract
  `47945b910257381adbf0b895d6596730762828592041c7bb1c4086bb5b25035b`.

The application-role census exposed two mechanical defects, in order. First, the isolated
census scaffold installed the hash-bound approved-erasure helper but omitted its separate
owner correction. The helper therefore ran as the setup administrator and rejected the guarded
`document_artifacts` delete with SQLSTATE `40001` / `TASK7_SOURCE_FREEZE_FENCED`. The corrected
composition now installs the external helper owner and read closure before the runner; this
changes census composition only, not contract bytes.

Second, the unchanged runner reached its atomic terminal
`UPDATE ... RETURNING status,executed_by,executed_at,counts`, where PostgreSQL returned
SQLSTATE `42501`: the function owner had the approved column-limited UPDATE privilege on
`executed_by` and `executed_at`, but not SELECT on those two returned columns. A disposable
hypothesis proof applied only the owner correction plus SELECT on those two columns. The
unchanged runner then persisted `EXECUTED`, the exact actor/timestamp, 49 count keys, and the
expected deal/artifact/object-reconciliation counts; transaction rollback restored the
REQUESTED request and all seed rows. The defect is privilege mechanics, not evidence or erasure
semantics.

`full-strict-v20` is the only successor candidate. Its deterministic unfrozen artifact is
`d046026da38b0eddbafc05368f43300b8c4aff7bfe0a8a9c1def0c960927f91a`.
It changes only:

1. version/reviewed-design/digest bytes and dependent hashes;
2. the current, future, activation, and restoration owner SELECT grant on
   `public.erasure_requests`, adding exactly `executed_by` and `executed_at`;
3. the versioned `task7-returning-select-closure-v1` admission authority.

The runner bodies, UPDATE columns, DELETE order, request persistence, audit lineage, UUID
namespaces, event/outcome set, CURRENT48/FUTURE38/RUNNER86, 28 steps, 48 rules, 1,756 routes,
19 extractors, 18 providers, rights routing, table census, RLS model, and every evidence
semantic remain byte-identical. Generation derives each current/future `RETURNING` projection
from the hash-bound body, derives the paired owner SELECT projection from the ACL, requires
exact closure with no unread extra grants, and requires the activation/restoration ACL copies
to equal their authoritative pairs. Missing, malformed, aliased, expression-bearing, duplicate,
or drifted projections fail `TASK7_RETURNING_SELECT_CLOSURE_INVALID` before fixture generation.

The v20 candidate remains `append_frozen=false`. It has no fixture, signature, signed census
receipt, migration consumption, runtime selection, or activation. The next admissible sequence
is the complete application-role census plus dedicated-executor supplement, signed receipt,
atomic fixture-first/artifact-last publication, retargeting of uncommitted migration `0038`,
application-role upgrade -> downgrade -> upgrade/readiness proofs, and the Task-7 checkpoint.
The Task-11 exit criterion is unchanged: Task 7 does not claim the real 27-cut/all-48-rule
three-way proof, and the exact blocked list remains E028-E036 plus E048 until Tasks 9-11 supply
the reviewed producers.

Reuse decisions:

- Reuse `PipelineWorker`, `RunExecutionService`, run-step repositories, PostgreSQL audit/idempotency, data-room package repositories, canonical claims/evidence/Sanad/calculation repositories, `PromptVersioningService`, BYOL credential encryption/repository, graph/RAG services, OCR and faster-whisper adapters, and product deliverable APIs.

Checkpoints list only skills actually used.

## 3. Read-only discovery verdict

The strict full-live claim is not currently attainable. The important blockers are structural, not merely missing secrets.

### 3.1 Data-room truth

Safe metadata-only local verification found:

| Company alias | Files | Bytes | Files over 50 MiB |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `company-001` | 58 | 496,782,706 | 3 |
| `company-002` | 209 | 361,424,748 | 0 |
| **Total** | **267** | **858,207,454** | **3** |

Format census: 223 PDF, 27 XLSX, 8 MP4, 3 DOCX, 2 PNG, 2 PPTX, 1 HTML, and 1 TXT. The older coverage matrix's 85-file/OneNote census is stale and is not an acceptance input.

PDF security census: 81 not encryption-marked, 142 encryption-marked but empty-credential-openable, zero requiring a non-empty password, and zero structurally unreadable during the census. These are parser admission dispositions, not a claim that all 267 files have completed the later authenticated ingestion run.

Current blockers:

- `real_example_run_harness.py` omits PNG from uploadable types and always defers MP4.
- `IngestionService.DEFAULT_MAX_BYTES` and `ParseLimits` are fixed at 50 MiB, so three MP4s cannot enter the existing path.
- Strict acceptance has no secure password/unlock input for an encrypted document and no successful terminal disposition contract for an unreadable/corrupt document.

### 3.2 Production-path truth

- `_default_api_client` constructs a `TestClient`, temporary object storage, `InMemoryAuditSink`, and non-production idempotency. It is forbidden for acceptance.
- `POST /v1/deals/{deal_id}/runs` currently calls `RunExecutionService.execute()` inline. It does not prove the canonical worker path.
- `PipelineWorker` does provide PostgreSQL polling, tenant scoping through `IDIS_WORKER_TENANT_IDS`, and `PostgresAuditSink`; strict acceptance must route through it.
- `build_run_context` discards the supplied audit sink, and enrichment, analysis, scoring, extraction, and deliverable helpers construct internal `InMemoryAuditSink` instances. All component audit must instead use the worker's durable sink.
- The API defaults to a JSONL audit sink and middleware can fall back to in-memory/SQLite stores. Strict startup/readiness must reject every such fallback.
- The filesystem object-store implementation is real, but acceptance must point it at an absolute persistent directory outside temporary/worktree cleanup and prove it across process restart.

### 3.3 Methodology/FDD truth

- The default methodology registry loads only the 21-question commercial JSON and coverage/tasks/results live in memory.
- The FDD map has 79 rows: P&L 29, Cash Flow 13, Liabilities 21, Assets 16; all four sheets use header row 2.
- The current FDD importer reads row 1 as the header, labels the source/version synthetic, and maps only coarse gross-margin/burn-rate formulas.
- `InMemoryMethodologyExtractionTaskExecutor` is explicitly synthetic-only, sets `external_calls_enabled=False`, and returns `EXTRACTOR_UNAVAILABLE`; the orchestrator supplies `extractor=None`.
- Methodology claims/evidence/Sanads are not merged into the canonical durable claim graph consumed by `GRADE`, `CALC`, agents, debate, scoring, and deliverables.
- Empty/partial methodology results can be recorded as completed-looking steps.

### 3.4 Semantic and consumer truth

- The orchestrator completes any returned dictionary without interpreting nested status/count fields. Final status only looks for ledger rows marked `FAILED`; it does not prove exactly 28 semantic successes.
- External-intelligence conflict checking currently produces a plan with blocked/deferred calls, not executed checks.
- Layer-2 readiness begins with `LAYER2_EXECUTION_DEFERRED`, contains no executed provider checks, is not consumed by the actual Layer-2 step, and can still look completed.
- Enrichment queries every provider with `company_name=deal_id`; a UUID is not a legal name, CIK, ticker, jurisdiction, domain, GitHub identity, or macro-series selection. Normalized provider facts are then discarded rather than durably stored.
- RAG retrieval is a probe, enrichment records are not indexed, and graph/RAG are not proven consumers for analysis/debate/scoring/Layer 2.
- Layer-1 debate's conditional-retrieval node marks retrieval complete without calling a retriever.
- Layer 2 has challenger/arbiter only, lacks `ic_advocate` and explicit dissent, and reflects VEP IDs without deeply consuming VEP content.
- Analysis and scoring results are step-summary JSON rather than durable first-class outputs.

### 3.5 Prompt and output truth

- Slice99's policy enumerates seven runtime prompt IDs. Runtime also executes eight analysis prompt families and two Layer-2 prompt families; Slice100 adds a methodology prompt and `ic_advocate`.
- All 17 current prompt artifacts are DRAFT; no live evaluation evidence or `registry.prod.json` exists.
- The deliverable catalog requires 15 durable rows per company: 14 content artifacts plus the manifest. Current code can return `durable_export=False` while the step is completed.

## 4. Definition of “real data, real API, real run”

Acceptance is the following single path:

`read-only company folder → authenticated HTTP upload → PostgreSQL document/artifact/span rows + persistent object bytes → queued PostgreSQL run → tenant-scoped PipelineWorker → 28 semantically validated steps with real providers and durable audit → 15 durable product artifacts → authenticated download and restart/isolation proof`

A company passes only when all of these are true:

- The source manifest matches 58 or 209 files and every row has matching source hash, durable object, document, parse result, and usable spans. Total reconciliation is exactly 267/267.
- Both frozen methodology versions are selected. All 100 rows have coverage records; every applicable row has planned tasks, real extractor calls, non-empty structured outcomes, canonical claims/evidence/Sanads, and durable lineage.
- Required FDD formulas run with Decimal inputs, version/hash, input claim IDs, assumptions, CalcSanad, and reproducibility hash.
- The ledger contains exactly the 28 named steps once, in the approved pipeline version, and every semantic validator passes.
- All applicable provider executions have real request provenance and durable facts; every infrastructure component has preflight and runtime proof.
- Graph, RAG, enrichment, Court/VEP, debate, analysis, scoring, and Layer 2 have durable consumer receipts that identify the producer object IDs actually used.
- All required agents and debate roles made real promoted-prompt LLM calls and persisted outputs, Muḥāsabah, challenge, dissent, and provenance.
- Fifteen product rows exist, object bytes hash to the manifest, authenticated downloads work before and after API/worker restart, and cross-tenant/cross-deal access is denied without an existence oracle.
- Safe report and log leakage scanners pass.

## 5. Production runtime design

### 5.1 API and worker

- In strict FULL mode, `POST /v1/deals/{deal_id}/runs` performs admission, creates a durable `QUEUED` run, emits durable audit/outbox records, and returns `202`. It does not execute inline.
- Add the exact process-role setting `IDIS_PROCESS_ROLE=api|worker`. `api` disables `create_app()`'s current in-process worker startup; `worker` exposes no API and runs only the queue loop. Strict startup rejects an absent/combined role. This prevents the API's existing auto-started worker from racing the standalone acceptance worker.
- `PipelineWorker` claims only its configured tenant's rows through the durable worker-lease contract below and invokes the shared `RunExecutionService`.
- The external acceptance client requires an explicit `--api-base-url`; strict mode rejects an injected client, ASGI transport, localhost `TestClient`, or the default harness client.
- All mutating requests carry `Idempotency-Key`. PostgreSQL replay/conflict semantics are tested across API restart.
- Final API and worker start in strict-production mode only if PostgreSQL/RLS, PostgreSQL audit/idempotency, Redis, persistent object store, tenant scope, service region, auth registry, worker-claim, and provider-budget readiness all pass. The only pre-promotion exception is the intrinsic `task4-ingestion-v1` API selected by `bootstrap-v1`/promoted-prompts-off: it executes only the seven real ingestion dependencies named in §1.6, starts no background worker, exposes only the Task-4 route allowlist, and cannot create a run or call a semantic/provider service.
- A strict run request must carry a fresh `strict_preflight_proof_id`. PostgreSQL stores the proof's tenant, deal, package/corpus hash, strict-profile generation/HMAC, pipeline-contract version/hash, expected-consumer-edge rule hash, immutable **pre-run static** decision-set hash, methodology version hashes, promoted-pointer hash, provider/dependency receipt-set hashes, creation/expiry, and consumed-at. Run creation atomically checks all bindings and consumes the proof. Step-5/16 runtime applicability decisions are append-only and finalized separately as §8.2 defines. Missing, expired, reused, cross-tenant, or mismatched proof IDs fail before a run row is created.

#### Durable worker lease

- Lease state lives on `runs`: `lease_owner_id UUID NULL`, `lease_fencing_token BIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (lease_fencing_token >= 0)`, `lease_expires_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL`, and `last_heartbeat_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL`. A fresh `QUEUED` run starts at token `0`; the token is never reset or decremented. `RUNNING` requires a non-null owner, positive token, heartbeat, and expiry. Requeue, cancellation, and terminal transitions clear the current owner/expiry while retaining the token and last-heartbeat history. PostgreSQL database time is authoritative.
- The lease migration takes a PostgreSQL table lock that excludes concurrent claims/writes, runs only after workers are drained, and aborts if any legacy `RUNNING` row exists, because no safe owner/token can be fabricated. Existing non-running rows backfill to token `0` with null owner/expiry/heartbeat. Downgrade requires both zero `RUNNING` rows and zero nonzero fencing epochs; once any row has lease history, downgrade fails closed rather than dropping and later repeating a token. The migration must prove upgrade -> downgrade -> upgrade on disposable PostgreSQL before review, and separately prove the nonzero-epoch refusal.
- Claim is one tenant-scoped atomic SQL `UPDATE ... RETURNING`, with candidate selection in that statement under `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`: it changes `QUEUED -> RUNNING`, sets the worker-instance UUID as owner, increments the fencing token, sets `last_heartbeat_at=statement_timestamp()`, sets `lease_expires_at=statement_timestamp()+IDIS_WORKER_LEASE_SECONDS`, and sets initial `started_at` if absent. The canonical worker always invokes this claim with `limit=1`; it never preclaims a batch or holds an unexecuted lease. There is no separate select-then-mark transition. The `WORKER_LEASE_CLAIMED` audit and state transition commit in the same PostgreSQL transaction.
- The owning worker renews with a compare-and-set update on exact `(run_id, tenant_id, status=RUNNING, lease_owner_id, lease_fencing_token)` before every canonical step and at least every `IDIS_WORKER_HEARTBEAT_SECONDS` while a step is in flight. Renewal is allowed only before the current database-time expiry and extends expiry by `IDIS_WORKER_LEASE_SECONDS`. A successful renewal statement that affects zero rows means ownership/token/status/expiry no longer matches and returns `WORKER_LEASE_FENCED`. A database, durable-audit, connection, or transport failure that prevents the renewal transaction from completing returns `WORKER_LEASE_HEARTBEAT_FAILED`. Both outcomes stop execution before output persistence or the next step; an in-flight result may not be persisted.
- Reclaim never transfers an expired `RUNNING` row directly to another owner. A reclaimer conditionally and atomically changes that same row `RUNNING -> QUEUED`, clears owner/expiry, and increments the fencing token; the next worker then uses the ordinary atomic claim, incrementing the token again. The successful requeue records `WORKER_LEASE_EXPIRED` and `WORKER_LEASE_RECLAIMED` audits in the same transaction and resumes only through the existing typed rehydration/semantic-validation machinery.
- After a claim transaction commits, the polling connection is released. The canonical worker constructs the claimed-run runtime through one production composition root using only the committed run identity, owner/token, typed lease configuration, and fresh repository/connection factories. No run-step repository, native-output writer, boundary callback, heartbeat callback, or resume validator may capture the polling connection or a prior worker's objects. Restart/reclaim constructs an entirely new runtime from durable run/step/native-output rows; no process-local producer state is an accepted rehydration input.
- Every worker-authored run-step, native step-output, output-root/receipt, and terminal-run write carries the claiming owner and fencing token. Its write transaction locks/validates the run row with exact tenant/run/status/owner/token and `lease_expires_at > statement_timestamp()` before mutation; failure rolls back the entire write unit and returns `WORKER_LEASE_FENCED`. Thus a crashed or zombie worker cannot append output, complete/fail the run, or overwrite a newer claim.
- Each canonical step uses explicit, short fenced boundary transactions; no PostgreSQL transaction spans provider/LLM execution or crosses from one canonical step into another. At the start boundary, one boundary unit of work opens and owns the transaction, re-establishes RLS with transaction-local tenant context, validates and renews the exact owner/token lease, writes the `RunStep -> RUNNING` transition, and emits the safe boundary/heartbeat audit. The provider/dispatch phase performs no durable step-output mutation: every producer returns typed staged native-output writes that accept the completion-boundary connection and cannot close over the polling/start-boundary connection. At the completion boundary, one boundary unit of work again owns the transaction, re-establishes RLS and validates/renews the lease, then atomically applies that complete producer write set (and, when introduced under the separately reviewed Task 7 schema, output roots/receipts), the `RunStep` terminal transition and result/error fields, and the safe boundary/heartbeat audit. The unit of work commits these mutations together, and only a distinct after-commit hook may inject/simulate a worker crash after the committed boundary; the transaction callback is never reused as a post-commit crash hook. Fence loss returns `WORKER_LEASE_FENCED`; any other failed boundary write/commit returns `WORKER_LEASE_BOUNDARY_COMMIT_FAILED`; either condition rolls back outputs, `RunStep`, heartbeat, and audit together. Commit or rollback clears the transaction-local tenant context, and every later boundary must establish RLS afresh before writing.
- Task 6 makes one narrow, explicit exception for Step 7's many paid methodology tasks. While the Step-7 `RunStep` remains `RUNNING`, each task may use (a) one fenced admission micro-boundary for the `STARTED` attempt, immutable budget reservation, and audit, and (b) after the transaction-free provider call, one fenced terminal micro-boundary for the attempt status, terminal result and normalized factual outputs, exact budget consume/refund, and audit. Each micro-boundary independently establishes tenant-local RLS, locks and validates the exact live lease owner/token, revalidates admitted source identity where specified in section 1.31, and commits or rolls back as one unit. It may not write canonical claim/EvidenceItem/Sanad/defect rows, any Task-7 output root/receipt/edge, or mark Step 7 `COMPLETED`. Only the final ordinary Step-7 completion boundary may complete the step after proving the full question/task/attempt/result equations. No other step inherits this exception.
- A periodic heartbeat while provider/LLM work is in flight never borrows or holds a step-boundary transaction. Each tick obtains its own short-lived connection and transaction, establishes transaction-local tenant RLS, performs the exact owner/token/unexpired CAS plus durable heartbeat audit, commits or rolls back, and releases the connection before the next wait. Its zero-row and infrastructure-failure outcomes use the fenced-versus-heartbeat-failed distinction above and prevent the in-flight result from reaching the completion boundary.
- Cooperative cancellation remains authoritative: cancellation may atomically change `QUEUED|RUNNING -> CANCELLED`, clears current owner/expiry, and retains the token. A worker observes `CANCELLED` at the existing boundaries and exits with `RUN_CANCELLED`; it never rewrites that terminal state. Reclaim operates on expired `RUNNING` only, requeues the same row so the one-active-run index is unchanged, and never creates a replacement run.
- Restart RED coverage applies the lease protocol at all 27 canonical cut points: commit worker A's complete prefix boundary, invoke the distinct after-commit crash hook, prove that prefix is durably visible while the next step and its outputs are wholly absent, expire and requeue A's lease, claim with worker B, rehydrate only from that committed prefix, and finish identically to uninterrupted execution. At every cut point the RED contract also rejects worker A's late write with its stale token and separately proves that an injected in-transaction boundary-write/audit/commit failure exposes neither a partial output nor an advanced `RunStep`.

### 5.2 Durable state

Strict mode requires:

- App-role PostgreSQL through `IDIS_DATABASE_URL`; admin DSN is migration-only.
- Applied Alembic head, app role `NOSUPERUSER` and `NOBYPASSRLS`, forced RLS/policies, tenant transaction context, and cross-tenant denial probes.
- Revision `0034` normalizes the exact 42-table tenant-bearing inventory under the Task 2b contract in §1.2: RLS is enabled and forced, and one null-guarded tenant policy supplies both `USING` and `WITH CHECK` on every table, including the tenant-identity predicate on `tenants`.
- `PostgresAuditSink` threaded through the context and every component. `IDIS_AUDIT_LOG_PATH`/JSONL is diagnostic fallback only and cannot count.
- PostgreSQL idempotency; `IDIS_IDEMPOTENCY_DB_PATH`/SQLite is forbidden.
- Redis-backed rate limits; in-memory rate limits are forbidden.
- Persistent filesystem object store at an absolute operator-provisioned root. A temporary or worktree-relative path fails admission.
- Durable provider budgets for Anthropic, OpenAI embeddings, and enrichment provider calls.
- A single strict-profile launcher loads `IDIS_STRICT_DOTENV_PATH` before importing any runtime component, applies process-environment overrides, validates the complete typed config, and passes one immutable config to the selected command. It computes a **base-profile HMAC** over effective required config using secret `IDIS_RUNTIME_PROFILE_HMAC_KEY`, explicitly excluding the launcher-injected command/process role; only the digest is persisted. The valid launcher commands are `migrate`, `governance`, `gate`, `api`, and `worker`; command/role is audited separately, and only API/worker set runtime `IDIS_PROCESS_ROLE`. Merely setting the dotenv-path variable is not treated as loading the profile.
- `IDIS_RUNTIME_PROFILE_GENERATION=bootstrap-v1` identifies pre-promotion migration/governance/probe calls and the one plan-pinned `task4-ingestion-v1` API/gate proof. For command `api`, only the exact pairs `(bootstrap-v1, promoted=0)` and `(acceptance-v1, promoted=1)` are valid; the bootstrap pair always selects the restricted scope, and every worker still requires the acceptance pair. Any other pairing or any worker/run/provider path under bootstrap fails closed. After promotion, the operator sets `IDIS_REQUIRE_PROMOTED_PROMPTS=1` and `IDIS_RUNTIME_PROFILE_GENERATION=acceptance-v1`; final API, worker, gate, preflight proof, runs, and restart must share the new base-profile HMAC. Bootstrap receipts remain usable only when their component-specific config hashes still match and are revalidated by the final profile; no bootstrap preflight proof is reusable.

### 5.3 Two-company isolation

The gate assigns non-sensitive aliases after sorting the two top-level folders, never emits the real folder names, and creates separate identities:

- `company-001 → deal-001 → documents-001 → run-001 → ledger-001 → package-001`
- `company-002 → deal-002 → documents-002 → run-002 → ledger-002 → package-002`

The acceptance ledger records only aliases, UUIDs, counts, aggregate bytes, and hashes. It fingerprints the source tree before and after execution and fails on mutation. No document, claim, provider fact, graph node, vector row, audit event, step row, or product row may cross the deal/tenant boundary.

## 6. Methodology and FDD target contract

1. Add authenticated import/freeze/select APIs and RLS-backed repositories for methodology versions, source hashes, sheet/row trace, questions, run selections, coverage records, task plans, task attempts/results, and canonical output links.
2. Fix the workbook importer to use the declared header row, preserve all 79 row traces, reject duplicates/malformed rows, and label the source honestly. `IDIS_FDD_METHODOLOGY_PATH` points to `C:\Projects\IDIS\IDIS\financial Due Diligence.xlsx`; the workbook remains read-only and only its safe hash/count/version metadata is reported.
3. Compose, rather than replace, the frozen 21-question CDD version and frozen 79-row FDD version. Strict admission requires both version IDs and exactly 100 coverage rows per company.
4. Each applicable row has at least one durable planned task and one non-empty terminal result. A valid `EVIDENCE_NOT_FOUND` result is structured and non-empty: it records searched document/span scope, real provider call provenance, and a governed gap finding. It is not `PARTIAL` or `DEFERRED`. A missing task/result or empty answer fails.
5. Replace the methodology executor on the strict path with a task-aware live extractor using the approved Anthropic extraction model and promoted `METHODOLOGY_QUESTION_EXTRACTION_V1` prompt. It receives question, row trace, answer schema, required evidence, candidate spans, predicates, and calculation requirements. No deterministic fallback exists.
6. Persist methodology outputs through canonical claim, EvidenceItem, Sanad/grade/defect, calculation, dashboard, Court, and VEP repositories. Merge their IDs into the same downstream evidence set used by the remaining pipeline.
7. Add versioned Decimal formulas for EBITDA bridge, EBIT bridge, net-income bridge, free-cash-flow/cash bridge, working-capital movement, debt roll-forward, tax reconciliation, asset tie-out, and liability tie-out, while retaining applicable core formulas. Every selected formula has inputs, row/claim IDs, assumptions, unit/currency/period, output, formula version/hash, CalcSanad, and reproducibility proof.

## 7. Versioned 28-step dependency order

The set of canonical step names remains unchanged. Slice100's sole governed append-frozen concrete contract ID is `full-strict-v18`; it preserves the reviewed ordering that puts identity after the conflict plan and enrichment after graph/RAG/Court/VEP. A canonical JSON serialization of the ordered names, semantic-rule versions, and expected consumer edges produces `pipeline_contract_hash`. V16 and v17 are immutable append-frozen but superseded and unselectable; all other earlier materialization candidates are historical evidence only and permanently inadmissible under sections 1.37-1.64.

Migration and compatibility contract:

- Add immutable `runs.pipeline_contract_version` and `runs.pipeline_contract_hash`; backfill those fields **and every existing run-step row's** version/hash/order.
- Register `snapshot-v1` from current `SNAPSHOT_STEPS`, `full-v1` from current `FULL_STEPS`/`STEP_ORDER`, immutable superseded `full-strict-v16`/`full-strict-v17`, and `full-strict-v18` below. Every strict entry stays `INACTIVE`; migration `0038`, artifact freeze, and census success do not activate v18. Backfill by stored run mode, never with one universal hash.
- Completed legacy runs remain read-only and retain their historical status/provenance. An unfinished `snapshot-v1`/`full-v1` run is resumable only if a verified migration reconstructs every required first-class output and loader hash from durable repositories. Summary-only analysis/scoring/deliverable state cannot be invented; if reconstruction is impossible the run is marked safely non-resumable with `LEGACY_RUN_RESUME_UNSAFE` and a new run is required.
- Only after the complete activation census passes and a separate reviewed activation change sets the code-owned entry to `ACTIVE` may strict Slice100 admission accept `full-strict-v18`. API creation then stores it; worker dispatch, step ordering, resume, retry, finalization, and product provenance resolve the stored registry entry rather than the global `STEP_ORDER` constant.
- Unknown or inactive version fails with `PIPELINE_CONTRACT_UNAVAILABLE`; a hash mismatch fails with `PIPELINE_CONTRACT_HASH_MISMATCH`.

`full-strict-v18` uses this inherited acyclic order:

1. `DATA_ROOM_INVENTORY_PACKAGE`
2. `DATA_ROOM_INGESTION_HANDOFF`
3. `INGEST_CHECK`
4. `DOCUMENT_PREFLIGHT`
5. `METHODOLOGY_COVERAGE_INIT`
6. `METHODOLOGY_EXTRACTION_TASK_PLANNING`
7. `METHODOLOGY_EXTRACTION_TASK_EXECUTION`
8. `METHODOLOGY_CLAIM_MATERIALIZATION`
9. `METHODOLOGY_EVIDENCE_ITEM_MATERIALIZATION`
10. `METHODOLOGY_SANAD_CREATION_LINKING_GRADING`
11. `METHODOLOGY_DETERMINISTIC_CALCULATION`
12. `METHODOLOGY_TRUTH_DASHBOARD`
13. `EXTRACT`
14. `GRADE`
15. `CALC`
16. `METHODOLOGY_COMPANY_IDENTITY_PACKAGE`
17. `ENRICHMENT`
18. `GRAPH_EVIDENCE`
19. `RAG_EVIDENCE`
20. `METHODOLOGY_EXTERNAL_INTELLIGENCE_CONFLICT_CHECK_PLAN` (plan plus executed checks)
21. `METHODOLOGY_EVIDENCE_TRUST_COURT`
22. `METHODOLOGY_VALIDATED_EVIDENCE_PACKAGE`
23. `DEBATE`
24. `ANALYSIS`
25. `SCORING`
26. `METHODOLOGY_LAYER2_READINESS_PACKAGE`
27. `LAYER2_IC_CHALLENGE`
28. `DELIVERABLES`

Semantic completion is a staged protocol. First, after dispatch, the typed validator checks the native result and rejects every internal non-success/empty/incomplete condition without depending on a success-output row. Second, one PostgreSQL transaction creates the native durable output, its `run_output_objects` and `run_step_outputs` roots, all rule-derived outgoing expectations that are currently determinable, and this consumer's incoming receipts; it then proves exact incoming-edge fulfillment and marks the step `COMPLETED`. Third, producer completion validates the completeness of outgoing **expectations**, never the future consumers' receipts. On resume, a `COMPLETED` step is trusted only after revalidating its native output/root/hash, its complete incoming-receipt set, and its complete currently derivable outgoing-expectation set; future receipt fulfillment is not required at an intermediate cut point. Finalization independently derives and checks the full contract set as specified in §8.2. Validation is typed per step and never uses substring/status-label scanning. Finalization is contract-version-specific: `snapshot-v1` requires its exact 8 `SNAPSHOT_STEPS`, `full-v1` its historical 28, and `full-strict-v18` the 28 below plus all required receipts. Auxiliary `RUN_LIFECYCLE` audit/lifecycle events are versioned separately and never counted as a canonical step row.

Resume is repository-driven, not summary-driven. Every step contract defines `load_output()`/`rehydrate_context()` from first-class durable rows and validates their version/hash/tenant/deal/run binding. Serialized underscore fields such as `_analysis_bundle`, `_analysis_context`, and `_scorecard` cannot be the only resume state. RED tests stop/restart after each of the 27 cut points and require an identical remaining execution/result; a missing/invalid loader fails `STEP_REHYDRATION_FAILED`.

Abbreviations: PG = PostgreSQL/RLS; OS = persistent object store; RS = durable run-step row; AU = PostgreSQL audit; CR = durable consumer receipt.

|---:|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `DATA_ROOM_INVENTORY_PACKAGE` | **Worker-side** package reconciler validates the immutable package receipt produced earlier by authenticated HTTP upload | PG package + 58/209 file-ledger rows; OS source objects; RS/AU | Handoff, ingest check, preflight, `DELIVERABLES:reconciliation` | Bound package/corpus receipt ID; expected count/aggregate bytes/hash; zero missing/extra/deferred/blocked rows. Post-run source-tree immutability belongs to the external verifier, not this early worker step. | `DATA_ROOM_INVENTORY_PACKAGE_FAILED`, `DATA_ROOM_RECONCILIATION_INCOMPLETE` |

A rights-restricted fact is excluded only from Graph/RAG and retains every non-index receipt. No error/block/defer. | `ENRICHMENT_PREFLIGHT_BLOCKED`, `ENRICHMENT_PROVIDER_FAILED`, `ENRICHMENT_PERSISTENCE_FAILED`, `ENRICHMENT_PROVENANCE_MISSING` |

### 8.1 Extraction partition and canonical union

Methodology and generic extraction have disjoint call ownership:

- Step 7 owns only keys `(run_id, methodology_version_id, row_id, task_id, source_span_id)`. Its unique task-attempt constraint prevents a second LLM call for the same approved task/version/input hash.
- Step 13 first builds a real, durable document-span/methodology-output RAG pre-index and executes tenant-scoped retrieval; `EXTRACT:rag_context` records its embedding/query/result IDs. This is the owner of the documented extraction-RAG edge. It then owns non-methodology discovery predicates and receives the completed methodology task keys as an exclusion set. It may reuse an existing methodology claim or attach new corroborating EvidenceItems, but it may not rerun a methodology question.
- Step 19 later rebuilds/finalizes the full RAG corpus with the completed generic claims, grades, calculations, graph summaries, and enrichment facts. It never reopens or mutates Step 13; it validates that the earlier pre-index receipt exists and supersedes its index version for downstream consumers.
- Canonical claim uniqueness uses `(tenant_id, deal_id, normalized_predicate, normalized_subject, period, unit, value_hash)` plus governed merge rules. The step records `methodology_claim_ids`, `generic_new_claim_ids`, `reused_claim_ids`, and `duplicates_prevented`.
- The exact union graded by Step 14 is `methodology_claim_ids ∪ generic_new_claim_ids ∪ reused_claim_ids`, with every ID appearing once. Provider budgets and audit receipts reconcile to the unique task/discovery calls.

### 8.2 Durable consumer-receipt contract

Add relational lineage roots, not a polymorphic pseudo-FK:

- RLS table `source_lineage_objects` owns immutable pre-run tenant/deal/package objects. Documents, artifacts, parse attempts, and spans each have a unique non-null FK to a source-lineage row; no run ID is required and one source corpus may feed multiple runs.
- RLS table `run_input_bindings` owns `binding_id`, run ID, source-lineage-object FK, selected object hash/version, corpus receipt ID, and pipeline contract hash. Unique `(run_id, source_lineage_object_id)` rows freeze exactly which pre-run documents/spans enter this run.
- RLS table `run_output_objects` owns every **run-produced** typed output, including canonical step/subrole success outputs, coverage/task/result rows, classifications, dashboard, identity, provider execution outcomes (`HIT`, real `MISS`, or verified `NOT_APPLICABLE`) and facts only when present, graph/RAG results, conflict-check plans/executions/findings, Court/VEP, debate/agent outputs, scorecard, readiness, Layer-2 outputs, and deliverables. Each native producing table has a unique non-null FK to its run-output parent.
- RLS table `run_step_outputs` links `output_object_id` to one canonical `run_step_id`, exact step/subrole/output kind, semantic-rule version, and summary hash. After dispatch-only typed validation, the native output/root, this row, outgoing expectations, incoming receipts, incoming-set assertion, and `COMPLETED` transition are committed in the same transaction; rollback leaves no success root or completed row. `RUNNING`/`FAILED` steps have no success output. Every canonical step has at least one first-class success output, so `INGEST_CHECK`, `DOCUMENT_PREFLIGHT`, coverage/planning/execution, identity, every provider outcome including `MISS`/`NOT_APPLICABLE`, conflict checking, and readiness are concrete FK-backed consumers/producers.
- RLS table `run_expected_consumer_edges` owns immutable `expected_edge_id`, exactly one producer FK (`run_input_binding_id` XOR `run_output_object_id`), canonical consumer step/subrole, usage kind, immutable applicability-decision FK when conditional, and contract rule hash. Check/FK constraints enforce the XOR; insert-only triggers deny update/delete. Every `run_input_binding` commit and every successful `run_output_object` commit must atomically materialize all unconditional and then-determinable conditional edges derived from the bound rule manifest; creation of a later runtime applicability decision atomically materializes every newly determined conditional edge. Any derivation/insert mismatch rolls back the producer or decision.
- RLS table `run_consumer_receipts` has `receipt_id`, `expected_edge_id` FK, consumer run-output-object FK that is linked to the exact successful `run_step_outputs` step/subrole, consumed-at, and safe usage evidence hash. Uniqueness is `(expected_edge_id, consumer_output_object_id)`. Inside consumer completion, the transaction first creates the validated consumer output/root, then inserts its incoming receipts, compares the independently derived incoming contract set to the persisted expected rows and receipt rows, and only then marks the step complete. A missing step-output root fails `STEP_OUTPUT_MISSING`; a status label alone cannot satisfy an edge.

The `full-strict-v18` contract contains an immutable expected-edge **rule** manifest. The preflight proof binds that rule hash plus only the immutable **pre-run static** decision-set hash (source selection, methodology versions, credential/rights readiness, and operator-supplied identity seed). Runtime applicability is separate: Steps 5 and 16 insert `run_applicability_decisions` rows bound to immutable upstream output/input IDs, hashes, and rule versions; update/delete is denied. Each decision and all newly derived expected-edge rows are committed atomically. A late status/applicability edit therefore cannot erase an edge. Finalization does not trust the persisted edge inventory: it independently derives canonical edge tuples from the bound rule manifest, complete `run_input_bindings`/`run_output_objects` census, static decisions, and append-only runtime decisions, then requires three-way equality: **derived contract tuples = persisted expected-edge tuples = receipt expected-edge tuples**, with exactly one correctly bound consumer receipt for every expected edge. It also verifies each receipt's consumer output belongs to the specified step/subrole and run. Extra, omitted, cross-run, duplicate, or hash-mismatched expectations/receipts fail `CONSUMER_RECEIPT_INCOMPLETE`.

Consumer IDs are canonical step names with explicit subroles—examples: `DEBATE:advocate`, `ANALYSIS:financial_agent`, `LAYER2_IC_CHALLENGE:ic_advocate`, and `DELIVERABLES:financial_diligence`. Labels such as “agents,” “FDD output,” or “final reconciliation” are not stored as consumers.

Required edge families are:

- package/inventory → handoff, ingest check, document preflight, `DELIVERABLES:reconciliation`;
- ingestion receipt/document/artifact/span outputs → ingest check, document preflight, methodology planning/execution, `EXTRACT`, graph, RAG, and evidence-index/provenance deliverable subroles;
- coverage/tasks/results/claims/evidence/Sanads/calculations/dashboard → their next materialization step and each applicable Court, graph, RAG, debate, analysis, scoring, Layer-2, and deliverable subrole;
- identity → each applicable enrichment provider and conflict check;
- enrichment E029/E030 → conflict checks, applicable members of the eight analysis roles, scoring, readiness, all three Layer-2 roles, and applicable deliverable subroles for every durable fact; E048 alone adds Graph/RAG external-context indexing when the immutable parent outcome has `external_context_index_allowed=true`; an index-denied fact retains all non-index receipts and has zero external-context Graph/RAG rows; no enrichment edge reaches Court/VEP or the five-role Layer-1 debate;
- internal-evidence graph/RAG → conflict checks, Court/VEP, all five Layer-1 debate roles, all eight analysis roles, scoring, readiness, all three Layer-2 roles, and applicable deliverables; external-context graph/RAG follows the enrichment edge family and is rejected from Layer 1;
- conflict checks/Court → VEP, debate, analysis, scoring, readiness, Layer 2, deliverables;
- VEP → debate, analysis, scoring, readiness, all Layer-2 roles, deliverables;
- debate → analysis, scoring, readiness, all Layer-2 roles, deliverables;
- analysis → scoring, readiness, Layer 2, deliverables;
- scoring → readiness, Layer 2, deliverables;
- readiness → all three Layer-2 roles; Layer-2 outputs → deliverables.

External acceptance/verifier code `SOURCE_TREE_MUTATED` is evaluated only after the post-run fingerprint. Admission proof codes are `STRICT_PREFLIGHT_PROOF_REQUIRED`, `STRICT_PREFLIGHT_PROOF_INVALID`, `STRICT_PREFLIGHT_PROOF_EXPIRED`, `STRICT_PREFLIGHT_PROOF_MISMATCH`, and `STRICT_PREFLIGHT_PROOF_ALREADY_CONSUMED`.

Worker-lease fail-closed codes are `WORKER_LEASE_CONFIG_INVALID`, `WORKER_LEASE_CLAIM_FAILED`, `WORKER_LEASE_HEARTBEAT_FAILED`, `WORKER_LEASE_FENCED`, `WORKER_LEASE_RECLAIM_FAILED`, and `WORKER_LEASE_BOUNDARY_COMMIT_FAILED`. Durable non-terminal audit event codes are `WORKER_LEASE_CLAIMED`, `WORKER_LEASE_HEARTBEAT_RENEWED`, `WORKER_LEASE_EXPIRED`, and `WORKER_LEASE_RECLAIMED`: claim records owner/new token/expiry; heartbeat records owner/current token/new expiry; and expiry/reclaim record prior owner/prior token/new token without secrets. Expiry and reclaim events exist only when the guarded `RUNNING -> QUEUED` update succeeds and commit in that transaction. `WORKER_LEASE_BOUNDARY_COMMIT_FAILED` is emitted only as safe post-rollback evidence after a non-fencing start/completion-boundary transaction fails; the failed transaction itself leaves no step, output, heartbeat, or boundary-audit fragment.

Task-4 watermark/image-only fail-closed codes are `WATERMARK_PAGE_NOT_ELIGIBLE`, `WATERMARK_PAGE_POLICY_INVALID`, `WATERMARK_PAGE_EXEMPLAR_INVALID`, `WATERMARK_PAGE_CLASSIFICATION_BELOW_THRESHOLD`, `WATERMARK_PAGE_SANITY_CAP_EXCEEDED`, `WATERMARK_PRESCAN_INCOMPLETE`, `WATERMARK_BATCH_ATTESTATION_INVALID`, `WATERMARK_BATCH_ATTESTATION_MISMATCH`, `WATERMARK_BATCH_ATTESTATION_NOT_ACTIVE`, `IMAGE_ONLY_PAGE_PROOF_INVALID`, `IMAGE_ONLY_PAGE_CONTRADICTED`, and `IMAGE_ONLY_DOCUMENT_REVIEW_REQUIRED`. `WATERMARK_RUNTIME_CANDIDATE_REVIEW_REQUIRED` and `IMAGE_ONLY_PAGE_POST_RUN_SPOT_CHECK_REQUIRED` are safe review statuses, never success substitutes. A watermark runtime candidate blocks the final Task-4 acceptance claim until reviewer confirmation; an image-only page is non-blocking only inside a parent document with independently persisted substantive spans, while an all-image-only document always blocks.

## 9. Enrichment-provider contract

Common rules for every provider:

- Strict runs force `EnvironmentMode.PROD`; ambient DEV mode cannot bypass rights policy.
- Gate A performs one bounded, non-private, real preflight call and persists safe call proof. Configuration-only health is insufficient.
- Company execution uses the verified identity package, not a UUID. Provider evaluation follows the §1.7 identity-before-rights order without exception. An applicable provider produces a real `HIT` or real `MISS` with request/retrieval/provenance proof. `NOT_APPLICABLE` is allowed only from an immutable decision over a positively verified jurisdiction/listing/entity identity and never counts as a runtime call; it also proves that rights, credentials, budget, cache, and transport were untouched.
- Normalized facts, identifiers used, retrieved time, rights class, response hash, safe source reference, and request provenance are stored in PostgreSQL. Raw payloads and credentials are not stored in reports.
- Every durable fact is indexed in RAG when rights permit and has consumer receipts from the applicable FDD, conflict-check, analysis, scoring, Layer-2, and deliverable consumers. External enrichment is excluded from Layer-1 Evidence Trust Court/debate inputs; its expected edges begin on the Layer-2 side of the VEP boundary.
- Common terminal codes are `ENRICHMENT_PREFLIGHT_BLOCKED`, `ENRICHMENT_PROVIDER_INPUT_MISSING`, `ENRICHMENT_PROVIDER_FAILED`, `ENRICHMENT_PERSISTENCE_FAILED`, `ENRICHMENT_PROVENANCE_MISSING`, and `ENRICHMENT_CONSUMPTION_MISSING`.

Every Gate-A provider probe has `timeout_seconds=15`, `max_attempts=1`, and `max_response_bytes=2,097,152`. It invokes the exact production connector/request builder and normalization schema used at runtime; a separate health-only URL cannot clear the row. A redirect may remain only on the same approved provider host; any other redirect, non-2xx, schema mismatch, empty expected fixture result, oversize body, timeout, or TLS error fails. PG table `provider_probe_receipts` stores only `tenant_id`, `provider_id`, `probe_contract_version=provider-probes-v1`, `fixture_id`, request-builder/version hash, approved host/operation, request ID, start/end, HTTP status class, schema-result code, rights-approval ID, credential-version ID, response SHA-256, attempt count, and timeout. No raw body, URL query secret, or credential is stored.

Exact probe contracts:

| Provider ID | Approved operation and non-private fixture | Required result |
|---|---|---|
| `sec_edgar` | `GET https://data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK0000320193.json`; fixture `apple-cik-0000320193` | 200 JSON object; normalized CIK `0000320193`; non-empty entity name/filing metadata |
| `companies_house` | `GET https://api.company-information.service.gov.uk/search/companies?q=TESCO%20PLC`; fixture `tesco-plc-search` | 200 JSON object; non-empty `items`; at least one normalized company number/name |
| `github` | `GET https://api.github.com/orgs/github`; fixture `github-org` | 200 JSON object; normalized `login=github` and numeric ID |
| `fred` | `GET https://api.stlouisfed.org/fred/series/observations?series_id=GDP&file_type=json&limit=1`; fixture `fred-gdp` | 200 JSON object; non-empty `observations` with date/value |
| `finnhub` | `GET https://finnhub.io/api/v1/stock/profile2?symbol=AAPL`; fixture `finnhub-aapl` | 200 JSON object; normalized ticker `AAPL` and non-empty company name |

BYOL bootstrap remains secret-safe: the strict launcher loads `IDIS_STRICT_DOTENV_PATH` before API/worker imports, reads the registry-declared credential variables (nine at the 18-provider Task-9 target) into the shared runtime config, encrypts them with `IDIS_ENRICHMENT_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, and idempotently inserts tenant-scoped credential versions. Reports show only variable name, source class (`process`/`dotenv`/`missing`), credential-row existence, and health status. The value, encrypted blob, and dotenv path are never emitted.

RED/YELLOW approval and budget bootstrap use one pinned trust contract. `IDIS_ENRICHMENT_RIGHTS_APPROVALS_PATH` and `IDIS_PROVIDER_BUDGET_POLICY_PATH` point to signed JSON envelopes; `IDIS_GOVERNANCE_TRUSTED_SIGNER_ID=<key id>` and `IDIS_GOVERNANCE_TRUSTED_ED25519_PUBLIC_KEY_PATH=<read-only PEM>` establish the trust root. Envelope schemas are `enrichment-rights-approvals-v1` and `provider-budget-policy-v1`; each contains `{schema_version, signer_id, payload, signature_base64}`. The Ed25519 signature covers the RFC 8785 canonical JSON bytes of `{schema_version, signer_id, payload}`. The signer ID must equal the configured trusted ID and the public-key file's safe hash is audited.

The strict launcher's governance subcommand authenticates with separate `IDIS_GOVERNANCE_ADMIN_API_KEY`, whose registry record is pinned in §10, verifies schema/signature/signer/tenant/actor authorization, then performs an idempotent import. Signature/schema/trust/auth failure codes are `GOVERNANCE_SIGNATURE_INVALID`, `GOVERNANCE_SCHEMA_INVALID`, `GOVERNANCE_SIGNER_UNTRUSTED`, and `GOVERNANCE_ADMIN_UNAUTHORIZED`.

Rights rows go to RLS table `enrichment_rights_approvals`, keyed by `(tenant_id, provider_id, environment, terms_version_hash)`. Rows contain status, rights class, license scope, attribution/no-store policy version, evidence hash, signer/approver actor, approved/expiry/revoked times, and credential-version link; no contract text or key is copied into safe reports. Gate A and each runtime call load the same active row and stamp `rights_approval_id` into provenance. Finnhub/FMP/Perplexity require a non-expired commercial PROD scope; Wayback/Google News/Semantic Scholar require a non-expired attribution/redistribution scope. Missing, expired, revoked, mismatched, or unauthorized approval fails `ENRICHMENT_LICENSE_BLOCKED` or `ENRICHMENT_RIGHTS_BLOCKED`. Task 9 RED-first expands the Task-2 governed-provider constraint and signed-envelope validator for these two new governed IDs; `epo_open_patent` remains GREEN.

### 9.1 Task 2 durable-governance schema clarification

Task 2 adds only the two tables already named by this plan: `enrichment_rights_approvals` and `provider_budget_policies`. Both are tenant-scoped, force RLS, and use the standard null-guarded tenant policy for both `USING` and `WITH CHECK`. Rights rows have a stable `rights_approval_id` and the approved composite uniqueness key. Each row is the verified signed **current snapshot** for that key: an exact envelope may renew it or transition it `APPROVED <-> REVOKED`, while DELETE remains trigger-forbidden. The importer locks the complete four-row Task-2 snapshot and commits the replacement together with one canonical append-only `integration.governance.imported` event. That event retains the exact prior safe rights payload, canonical hash, and prior import-audit reference; audit failure rolls the snapshot update back. Import-time expiry and active RED-credential checks apply only to `APPROVED`, while an expired signed `REVOKED` snapshot remains importable and retains its credential-version reference as historical lineage. An approval is not effective before its signed `approved_at`; a signed revocation timestamp cannot predate its approval or lie after the import time. Future-dated approval or revocation never becomes an early effective state. Exact-current replay remains idempotent, but a previously audited payload cannot be replayed as a rollback. `REVOKED -> APPROVED` requires the new `approved_at` to be strictly later than the latest durable revocation for that provider/environment across every terms-version hash and the append-only governance lineage.

Rights and budget envelopes have independent lifecycle dispositions and audit lineages. An unchanged rights replay may accompany a newly inserted budget replacement, and a rights renewal/revocation may accompany an unchanged budget replay; neither document is synthetically rewritten. The report and canonical audit event record separate `created`/`transitioned`/`replayed` dispositions and lineage event IDs for each document plus the operation event ID. Budget policy import remains all-or-nothing and strictly append-only: `UPDATE` and `DELETE` are trigger-forbidden, and replacement inserts a new signed policy hash with exactly one row per required component under `(tenant_id, policy_hash, component_id)`. The repeated policy header binds the complete component set to one version, USD total, signer, import audit, and import timestamp. Under the tenant advisory lock, PostgreSQL—not a caller-supplied wall clock—assigns a strictly monotonic complete-policy import time using the durable prior maximum; a newly inserted replacement must then prove that its hash is the runtime-selected active policy. A replay is accepted only when its signed hash is still that active complete policy, so a stale historical policy cannot accompany a rights transition or produce a misleading success report. At Task 9 the component set is derived from the 18-provider registry plus `anthropic` and `openai_embeddings`, for 20 rows; no literal count is an authority. A policy stays active until an explicitly signed replacement is imported; no unplanned effective-window field is invented outside the pinned v1 payload.

Every governed provider call carries a canonical tenant-unique UUID `reservation_id`; arbitrary caller prose is rejected so a secret cannot be smuggled into audit identity. The existing append-only RLS `audit_events` table is the exact reservation state ledger, so no unplanned reservation table or column is added: canonical validated `integration.provider_budget.reserved`, `.consumed`, and `.refunded` events bind the tenant, active policy hash, component, calls, USD, all token dimensions, and global cap. Under the same tenant advisory lock, the aggregate `provider_budget_usage` mutation and its event append commit atomically. The production store binds the global cap once from the reviewed profile and is governed-only: every **new** caller vector must match that bound, and no legacy caller-supplied-cap API remains reachable on a strict-certified instance. A reservation has exactly one reserve event and at most one terminal event; its immutable ledger vector authorizes exact current/terminal retries and completion of already-reserved spend across restart, later policy replacement, or a reviewed global-cap change. New spend always requires the currently active policy and construction-bound cap. A mismatched vector or opposite terminal transition fails `PROVIDER_BUDGET_CONFIG_BLOCKED`, and a late retry cannot debit another reservation's aggregate balance. The committed readiness proof uses deterministic IDs bound to tenant, policy, component, and global cap; it retains one conservative consumed call plus one net-zero reserve/refund lifecycle and reconciles aggregate state against the immutable ledger without rewinding either. Restart and concurrent-retry proof must show one aggregate mutation and one terminal event, with no credential, prompt, provider payload, or other secret in the event.

The same migration changes only two existing tables. `enrichment_credentials` gains a stable `credential_version_id`, changes its key to `(tenant_id, connector_id, credential_version_id)`, and enforces at most one row with both `rotated_at` and `revoked_at` null. Rotation marks the prior active version's `rotated_at` and inserts a new encrypted row; it never overwrites ciphertext. A RED-provider rights row links to the exact matching tenant/provider credential version; YELLOW rows have no credential link. `provider_budget_usage` retains the existing tenant/component identity and legacy consumed-call count, then adds the active policy hash plus reserved-call, USD-Decimal, and input/output/embedding-token balances needed for atomic reserve, consume, and refund. Existing call usage is preserved and remains conservative across a policy change; strict admission rejects an unbound legacy row or any outstanding reservation under another policy.

No runtime-profile, auth-registry, signer, component-catalog, credential-version, receipt, or edge table is added. The strict profile digest and launcher command are durable audit fields, not a new configuration table. A Task 2 downgrade must refuse rather than discard imported governance rows, multiple credential versions, or non-zero new budget balances; the disposable-PostgreSQL proof therefore exercises upgrade -> downgrade -> upgrade with preserved legacy rows before governance import.
